# SPEC — excalidraw fork

The source of truth for this project. Rows are `D-n` (decisions) and `AC-n` (acceptance, each naming the test file that checks it). Format required by `.tins/kit/src/spec.mjs`:

- a decision row is `| D-n | <label> | locked | <detail> |` — the **label** cell (2nd column) is where a dependency name must appear, and the **status** cell (3rd column) must be `locked`, `open` or `superseded by D-n`;
- an acceptance row is `| AC-n | <label> | <check-file> | <detail> |` — the **check** cell (3rd column) names a file that must exist and must mention the `AC-n` id.

Wave 1 (this file) covers user items 1, 4, 5 and 8. The design that these rows implement is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-page-document-model-design.md`.

Wave 2 (this file, rows D-12, AC-10, AC-11) covers user item 2 — content inserted while a later page is active must land on that page. Its design is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-image-page-stamping-design.md`.

Wave 2 (this file, rows D-13, AC-13, AC-14, AC-15) covers user item 3 — the pen produced broken, segmented curves because the pointer samples the device reported between two frames were thrown away. Its design is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-draw-performance-design.md`.

Wave 4 (this file, rows D-17, D-18, D-19, D-20, AC-23, AC-24, AC-25) covers user items 1, 2 and 5 — the right-click paste has to keep the code card, and the markdown viewer has to copy what is selected. Its design is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-21-paste-and-markdown-design.md`.

Wave 4b (this file, rows D-21, D-22, D-23, AC-26, AC-27, AC-28) covers user items 3 and 4 — a selection of text can be **reformatted** into a code card or into a drawn table. Its design is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-21-reformat-selection-design.md`.

Wave 4c (this file, rows D-24, D-25, D-26, AC-29, AC-30, AC-31) covers the owner's decision Q3 (2026-10-21) — a paste that does not fit the page is **offered** to the user, and the text that is left over goes back to the clipboard. Its design is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-21-paste-overflow-design.md`.

Wave 6a (this file, rows D-35, D-36, D-37, D-38, AC-41, AC-42, AC-43) covers user item 9 — a saved `.excalidraw` file can be inserted into the canvas that is already open. Its design is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-21-insert-document-design.md`.

Wave 6c (this file, rows D-42, D-43, D-44, D-60, D-61, D-62, D-63, AC-48, AC-49, AC-50, AC-51) covers user item 11 — a flowchart that comes alive: the current step is enlarged, the components in it animate, and the whole thing loops. The owner's decision (question 9, 2026-10-21) made it an **overlay** triggered from a **properties-panel section**, so there is no new element type and nothing is written into the document. Its design is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-21-flow-animation-design.md`.

---

## Decisions

| id | label | status | detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-1 | page geometry | locked | `ExcalidrawPage` gains `width`, `height` and `contentScale`. 1920x1080 is the **minimum** page size; a page may only grow. Pages are laid out left-to-right by **cumulative offsets**, because the index-based `index * PAGE_GAP` formula cannot express pages of differing sizes. `PAGE_GAP` is retained. The three fields are **optional in the type** and resolved by `normalizePage` to `PAGE_WIDTH` / `PAGE_HEIGHT` / `1`, so a document written before pages carried a size (and a host that builds a page itself) keeps working; a page's rect is always at least the minimum size. `PAGE_GAP` keeps its value — the pitch between two default-sized page origins — and `PAGE_SPACING` (200) is the gap between the two page rects, so the cumulative layout reproduces the old `index * PAGE_GAP` positions exactly for default-sized pages. |
| D-2 | one scaling axis | locked | **Scale content** is the only way to get more room on a page: it divides that page's element geometry by 2, leaves the page rect alone, and its reverse (x2) undoes it. There is **no** "grow page" / "shrink page" control any more — the page bar offered both, they did the same job as the content controls, and growing a page pushed the **later** pages sideways, because the layout is positional and the later pages' rects move (the user's own fix, 2026-10-21: "just select all the existing content on the page that needs to be expanded and just scale it down"). A page's rect still grows where it must — to contain the content restored onto it (`growPagesToFitContent`, D-5) — but that is the importer's job, not a control. Implemented as the pure helper `scalePageContent` (`scene/pages.ts`), which rewrites stored geometry and touches no renderer state. |
| D-3 | scaling persists geometry | locked | Scaling rewrites the **stored** element geometry. It is NOT a render-time multiplier. Reason: the fork has already been broken once by coordinate-convention drift (the scroll-sign defect recorded in `HANDOVER.md` section 2). A render-time multiplier would spread that class of bug across the renderer, exporter, hit-testing, bounds and presenter. The scale step is 0.5 and its inverse 2, both exact in binary floating point, so a down/up round trip is lossless. Scaled: `x`, `y`, `width`, `height`, free-draw / linear `points`, `strokeWidth`, and `fontSize` for text. Not scaled: `angle`, `groupIds`, `boundElements`, `link`, `frameId` — those are references, not geometry. A text container and its bound text are both anchored, so they scale together and the binding survives. |
| D-4 | floating elements | locked | A new **optional** per-element flag `floating?: boolean` marks an element as not owned by its page's coordinate space. A floating element is excluded from _scale content_, may sit outside the page rect, and widens the camera clamp domain to the union of the page rect and the floating elements' bounding box (`getPageClampRect`, used by the editor's camera clamp). The flag is opt-in and never set automatically — an element that does not carry it does not grow one on restore. |
| D-5 | imported content | locked | After restoring a scene, any page whose element bounding box exceeds the page rect **grows the page** to contain it, so imported content is never silently unreachable. To keep specific content deliberately outside, the user marks it floating and drags it out. Both directions are reversible. A page's origin is derived from the layout and is not stored, so a page can only grow to the right and downwards: content left of / above the page's origin cannot be contained by growing the page and has to be marked floating to be reachable. |
| D-6 | document storage | locked | The document is persisted through a `DocumentStore` interface with two backends: **IndexedDB is primary** (`idb-keyval`, already a dependency) and **localStorage is the fallback** when IndexedDB is unavailable. The page index and the per-page element buckets are written as **separate records**, so a failed element write cannot lose the page list. A write failure is surfaced to the user, never swallowed. |
| D-7 | restore does not inherit local structure | locked | `restoreAppState` must not take the running app's `pages` or `currentPageId` for a file that does not define them. The local appState is a fallback for **view preferences** only (theme, sidebar, grid), never for the document's own structure. A file without `pages` restores to exactly one page, and its elements are stamped to it. The file's own pages win (`getFilePages`), and elements without a `pageId` are stamped to the file's **first** page (`restoreElements`'s `defaultPageId`), not to a hard-coded default. |
| D-8 | page operations are undoable and synced | locked | `pages` and `currentPageId` are added to `getObservedAppState`, so adding, renaming or deleting a page becomes an undoable, collab-synced operation. Today they are omitted, which is why page changes are neither. They are standalone (not element) props, so `AppStateDelta.stripStandaloneProps` keeps them out of the element delta. |
| D-9 | page list and deletion | locked | The `PageBar` page list is **virtualised** — only the visible window of rows is rendered — so a document with thousands of pages does not create thousands of DOM nodes. A **single-page delete** control is added, using the existing two-step `guardDestructive` pattern. Deleting the last remaining page leaves one empty page rather than an empty document. |
| D-10 | measured, not guessed | locked | The document is instrumented: total and per-page element counts, save latency, page-switch latency, page count, active storage backend. A lazy-hydration redesign (loading only the current page's elements into memory) is **deferred** until a real document crosses a measured threshold: save p95 > 250 ms, page-switch p95 > 120 ms, more than 50,000 elements, or more than 2,000 pages. |
| D-11 | no new dependency | locked | Wave 1 adds **no** dependency. Everything it needs is already available: `idb-keyval` (storage), the existing `files-db` binary store (images/GIFs), and the existing fractional-indexing package. |
| D-12 | insertion belongs to the current page | locked | An element inserted into the **live** scene belongs to the page the user is acting on. Neither element factory can know it — `newElement()` cannot ask the app (that would be a dependency cycle) and `restoreElements()` resolves from the **file** — so one authoritative helper (`stampCurrentPage`, `scene/pages.ts`) stamps the current page onto the elements, and **every** insertion path goes through it: draw, paste, library, chart, the app's sidebar insert (image and code card), and the two actions that create an element ("wrap selection in frame", "wrap text in container"). The stamp is applied **in place**, because callers keep a reference to the element they created and go on to mutate it (drag handles, binding, text editing). Restoring a file is the one exception and does not come through the helper (D-7). |
| D-13 | recover the dropped pointer samples | locked | A throttled pointer handler runs **once per animation frame** and sees only the **last** event of that frame, so the intermediate positions the device reported are thrown away: a free-draw stroke recorded one point per _frame_ instead of one point per _sample_, and a dropped frame rate turned the stroke into a polyline with visible corners. The samples are recovered from the event itself — `PointerEvent.getCoalescedEvents()` returns the whole batch the browser coalesced — and the free-draw branch appends **every** sample, each with its own pressure, so the recorded `pressures` array stays index-aligned with `points`. The helper is feature-detected and falls back to the single event: jsdom has no `window.PointerEvent` and older browsers lack the method. The throttle itself is unchanged, and the shape generation and its `streamline` pass are untouched. The per-frame cost of a stroke no longer grows with its length: the bounding box is tracked incrementally and the whole-scene scans run only when the element set changes, not on every frame. |
| D-14 | the offer is made on import | locked | A file that defines **no pages** of its own and whose content does not fit on one page is **offered** a split when it is opened. A file that defines its own pages has a structure its author chose, and is opened as it is. The offer is never applied automatically: the user decides. The candidate is computed in `packages/excalidraw/data/pageSplit.ts` (which wraps `paginateContentIntoPages` from `packages/excalidraw/scene/pages.ts`) and handed back by `loadSceneOrLibraryFromBlob` in `packages/excalidraw/data/blob.ts` as `pageSplit`; `loadFileToCanvas` in `packages/excalidraw/components/App.tsx` raises the offer. `loadFromBlob` is unchanged, so every other caller keeps today's behaviour. |
| D-15 | three answers: split, keep, cancel | locked | The offer is answered by one of three: **split across pages** (recommended) tiles the content, **keep on one big page** leaves the grown page the import already produces (`growPagesToFitContent` + `translateContentForPageLayout`), and **cancel** imports nothing and leaves the canvas exactly as it was. The dialog is built on the library's own `Modal` (`packages/excalidraw/components/Modal.tsx`) and driven by the promise/atom pattern of `packages/excalidraw/components/OverwriteConfirm/OverwriteConfirmState.ts`, because `Dialog` is bound to the app's `openDialog` state and cannot carry a promise. |
| D-16 | the tiling is computed once | locked | `paginateContentIntoPages` is a pure pass over the whole scene, so the candidate is computed **once**: the page count the offer shows is the number of pages the split actually produces, and the very same candidate is the one that is imported. The candidate is computed from the **restored** elements — the ones the tiling acts on — and not from the grown-and-translated ones. |
| D-17 | the armed paste is decided in the library's paste path | locked | The armed code paste is decided **inside the library's paste path**, through the library's existing `props.onPaste` hook, and the app's `window` capture-phase `paste` listener is gone. The context menu's Paste (`actionPaste`) fires **no DOM paste event** — it reads the clipboard and calls `app.pasteFromClipboard(...)` directly — so a listener only ever saw the keyboard paste and the right-click paste came out as plain text boxes. `pasteFromClipboard` is the one funnel both paths go through, so the hook covers both and there is one behaviour. The hook returns `false` to mean "handled it, stop"; the library calls `event?.preventDefault()` only **after** the hook, so the app's handler calls it itself. The pointer is still tracked with a `pointermove` listener on `window` and used for the card's placement in **both** paths — `actionPaste` builds a synthetic event whose `clientX`/`clientY` are 0. The plain-text copy on the clipboard is left alone, so pasting into another app still gets ordinary text. |
| D-18 | the armed paste needs the stashed code on the clipboard | locked | The armed paste is consumed **only** when the clipboard still holds the stashed code. The library trims the text it parses out of the clipboard, so the comparison is made on the trimmed text on both sides. If the user copied something else in the meantime, the paste is an **ordinary** paste and the armed paste is not spent — it is still waiting for a paste of that code. |
| D-19 | selecting in the markdown viewer copies | locked | Selecting text in the markdown viewer **copies it to the clipboard automatically**. The copy happens on the **mouse-up / key-up** that ends the selection, not on `selectionchange`: a clipboard write needs a transient user activation and `selectionchange` carries none, so `navigator.clipboard.writeText` would be rejected with `NotAllowedError`. A link's text is ordinary text inside the body, so it is covered by the same code. A failed write is caught and reported with the app's own toast. |
| D-20 | only a selection inside the markdown body is copied | locked | Only a selection that lies **wholly inside** the markdown body is copied: both ends of the range have to be inside the body. A selection that reaches out of the viewer is left to the browser. |
| D-21 | the library offers the reformat, the app does it | locked | The library gains an `onReformatSelection` prop and one canvas context-menu entry, **"Reformat as..."** (`actionReformatSelection`). The entry appears **only** when the selection holds at least one text element, and only when the host app handles the prop at all. The library does **not** reformat anything itself: a syntax-highlighted code card needs the host app's own highlighter (`shiki` is the app's dependency, not the library's), so the library owns the _offer_ and hands the selection over — the same extension pattern `onPaste`, `onDuplicate` and `onLibraryChange` already use. The two formats are offered by **one** action over a selection, not by two separate actions: the owner's decision. |
| D-22 | the code-card reformat replaces the selection | locked | The code-card reformat reuses the app's own `insertCodeIntoScene(api, rawCode, { fileName, language, at })`, so the result is the very same card the sidebar's Insert button builds — a dark rectangle with the code on it as real, selectable text. The card is placed at the **selection's own top-left**, measured with the library's own `getCommonBounds`, never with a re-derived formula. The **original text elements are deleted**: the reformatted content _replaces_ the selection. A selection carries no filename, so there is no language to read — the app's plain-text language (`text`, the one `languageFor` gives a `.txt` document) is used, which is the language the app's own plain-text inserts use. |
| D-23 | the table is drawn behind ordinary text cells | locked | The table reformat recovers the cells from the lines of the selection: a markdown row (starts and ends with ` | `) is split on ` | `, any other line is split on a **tab** (what a table copied from a spreadsheet or a PDF puts between its cells), and the markdown **separator row** (all dashes and colons) is **dropped** — it is a rule, not a row of the table. The cells are laid out on a grid — a column is as wide as its widest cell, with a floor and a ceiling — and the table is **drawn**: one rectangle per cell, **behind** ordinary, **selectable** text elements (never an image, never a locked group), because the owner asked for the cells to stay selectable. The table starts at the selection's own top-left, measured the same way as the code card. |
| D-24 | the paste that does not fit is offered | locked | A paste that does not fit the **current page** raises an **offer** — it is never resolved automatically. The bound is the current page's rect (`getCurrentPageRect`), **not** the viewport: the viewport is what the camera happens to show, the page is what the camera is clamped to, and a paste that fits the viewport but hangs off the page is exactly the failure the owner reported. The offer's three answers are the owner's own — **discard remaining text**, **place all text on this page**, **place text on multiple pages** — and dismissing the offer without a choice is the first answer _without_ the clipboard write: what fits is placed and the rest is left in the clipboard. The offer is a promise/atom dialog beside the page-split offer (`PasteOverflowDialog/`, mounted in `LayerUI.tsx`, awaited in the paste path), exactly like the import offer (D-14). It is raised only when the pages system is on: without it the camera is not clamped to a page, so nothing can become unreachable. The pasted text is placed **inside** the page — a page grows to the right and downwards only (D-5), so a text that hangs over the page's left or top edge is nudged into the page, which is the only placement the camera can reach. |
| D-25 | the fit is decided per line | locked | The text that does not fit is decided **per line**: a line whose text element lies **entirely inside** the page's rect fits, and a line that **straddles** the edge overflows — half a line hanging over the edge is not readable, and the camera cannot reach it. The measurement is the library's own: the element's bounding box (`getCommonBounds`) against the page's rect (`isElementInsidePageRect` / `countElementsInsidePageRect`, `scene/pages.ts`), never a re-derived formula. The first line that does not fit, and everything after it, overflows. Splitting the text into chunks for the "across pages" answer uses the same rule: a chunk is the longest run of lines that fits the page, and the next chunk starts a new page. |
| D-26 | the leftover goes back to the clipboard | locked | Whatever is left over is **written back to the clipboard**, so the user can paste the rest somewhere else. The clipboard is the user's, and nothing is thrown away silently: the "discard remaining text" answer places only the part that fits and puts the rest on the clipboard, while the two answers that place everything leave nothing over and write nothing. The write happens on the answer the user gave — a click carries the transient user activation a clipboard write needs — and a blocked clipboard is reported with the app's toast, not thrown. |
| D-27 | the page-relative offset is signed | locked | The page-relative camera offset is a **signed** ratio, not a 0..1 fraction: it is how far the page's near edge is behind the viewport's near edge, measured in page widths. It is **negative** when the viewport is wider than the page and the camera has centred the page, and that negative value is what makes the mirrored window show the same slice. The regression: the offset was clamped to `0..1`, so the negative anchor of a centred page was thrown away and turned into "the page's near edge is at the viewport's near edge" — a different camera, a third of a page away from the editor's. The library's own page-camera test now checks the anchor against the **renderer's** slack in front of the page (zero when the page's near edge is flush with the viewport's near edge, negative when the page is centred with slack in front of it) instead of against the old clamped fraction. |
| D-28 | the offset is bounded above only | locked | The offset is bounded **above** at 1 and is **not** bounded below. The camera clamp guarantees the upper bound; the lower end is unbounded (a narrow, tall page in a wide window), so a lower clamp would throw away real geometry. |
| D-29 | the presenter test anchors on the library's page rect | locked | The presenter camera test uses the **library's** `getCurrentPageRect`, not a re-derived page rect. The fork's page layout is cumulative and each page is as big as its own content, so a fixed-pitch re-derivation silently stops being the truth — and it is exactly what hid the presenter's drift. |
| D-30 | the presenter's zoom floor is the **page's own** fit zoom | locked | `resolvePresenterCamera` clamps the mirrored zoom from below by the zoom at which the whole page fits the presenter's window. That floor is the fit zoom of **the page being mirrored**, not of the default page size: a legacy file's page is grown to hold the whole board, so its fit zoom is far smaller than the default's, and the default's floor would force the mirrored window to zoom **in** past the editor's own — showing a tenth of the board at ten times the size. `getFitPageZoom` already takes the page's size as arguments, so the fix is to pass the page rect's own width and height. |
| D-35 | inserting a document is an **explicit action** | locked | Inserting a document is an **explicit action** — "File > Insert document..." — and not a question asked on every open. Opening a file keeps **replacing** the canvas: `loadFileToCanvas` syncs the file's elements with `replaceFiles: true`, so the document that was open is gone. A modal on every open would ask a question the user almost never wants to answer, and the owner's wording is an action ("insert ... into another opened canvas"), not a change to what "open" means. The action is **not offered when the canvas is empty** — inserting into an empty canvas is exactly what "open" does, and offering both would be noise — and it is switched on by the host app through `UIOptions.canvasActions.insertDocument`, like every other canvas action. |
| D-36 | the inserted content is flattened onto the current page | locked | The inserted content is **flattened onto the current page** and stamped with it by the same authoritative helper every other insertion path uses (`stampCurrentPage`, D-12). The other document's **page structure is not merged**: interleaving two page layouts is a different feature, and the owner asked for the _drawing_ to come across. The file is read **without** the import path's page logic — `loadSceneOrLibraryFromBlob` grows the page to contain the content and translates the content to follow the grown page, which is right for an import and wrong for an insertion into a document that already exists — so the inserted content keeps its own coordinates and is only moved by the placement (D-38). |
| D-37 | the inserted binary files get fresh ids | locked | The inserted content's **binary files are given fresh ids** and the elements' `fileId`s are rewritten to match, so an id clash with this document's file store cannot silently change an image. The inserted **elements** get ids of their own for the same reason: an element id that is already in this document would collapse the two into one. Both remappings happen in `scene/insertDocument.ts`, and the fresh file id is checked against the ids already in this document's file store, so it cannot collide with one of them either. |
| D-38 | the inserted content is placed beside what is already on the page | locked | The inserted content is placed **beside** what is already on the page — to the **right** of it, one `PAGE_SPACING` away — and the page **grows** to contain it (`growPagesToFitContent` + `translateContentForPageLayout`, the same primitives the import path uses). The placement is measured with the library's own `getCommonBounds`, never with a re-derived formula, and it is measured against the page's **content** — the elements of that page that are neither deleted nor floating, the same set the page model calls the page's content. On a page with no content the inserted content lands on the **page's origin**. What arrived is **selected**, so the user can see it and move it. |
| D-42 | the animation is a properties-panel section, played as an overlay | locked | The owner's decision (2026-10-21, question 9): the animation is configured in a **section of the properties panel** and played as an **overlay** over the canvas. It is **not** a panel element: there is no new element type, the user's flowchart stays ordinary shapes and arrows, and playing the animation writes **nothing** into the document. The section is a `<fieldset>` + `<legend>`, like the Layers / Align / Actions sections around it, and it appears when the selection holds **two or more** elements, because a one-element selection has no flow. |
| D-43 | the step order comes from the arrows, with reading order as the tie-break | locked | The **steps are the selected elements**, ordered by the **arrows** between them: an arrow that is in the selection and whose `startBinding` and `endBinding` name two of the selected shapes says "these two are consecutive", and the order is a **topological sort** over those edges. Where the arrows do not decide, the tie is broken by the **reading order** (D-61). The resolver is a **pure function** in `packages/excalidraw/scene/flowOrder.ts`, so the order can be tested without a canvas. |
| D-44 | the overlay does not replace the canvas | locked | The overlay is a **DOM layer over the canvas**, exactly like the animated-image overlay: the canvas keeps drawing the flowchart as usual and the overlay only adds the **focus treatment** on top. Its position is computed with the library's own **`sceneCoordsToViewportCoords`** — the oracle (D-34) — and never with a re-derived formula, and it is not a copy of `animatedImageTransform`. The test asserts it the way the animated-image overlay's test does: through the oracle and the produced transform string, parsed and applied with plain matrix maths. |
| D-60 | a step is a container, not a shape | locked | `getTargetElements` folds the bound text into the selection, so a labelled box arrives as **two** `targetElements` entries. A step is therefore the **container** and its label together, and the resolver **dedupes on the container** rather than trusting `targetElements.length`. The step's bounds are the **union** of the container's and its label's, measured with the library's own `getCommonBounds`, and that union is what the focus treatment scales about. |
| D-61 | the reading order is a row band, then left to right | locked | The reading order is a **row band** and then left to right. The band test is an **overlap**, not a tolerance: two shapes are in the same band when their vertical spans intersect, so there is no magic pixel. A shape that lies entirely below another is in a later band, and a shape that straddles two bands joins the band it overlaps most. The bands themselves are ordered top to bottom. **No reading-order helper existed anywhere in the repository** — this one is written here, in `packages/excalidraw/scene/flowOrder.ts`. |
| D-62 | a cycle is broken, not refused | locked | The owner's own words are "this entire thing is in a loop", so a flowchart whose arrows form a cycle is **expected**, not an error. `dagre.layout()` does not tolerate a cycle, so the cycle is found with **`graphlib.alg.findCycles(g)`** and its **back-edge is dropped**, and the rest is laid out. In the **step order** the cycle is broken at its **lowest-reading-order member** and the flow carries on from there. The dropped edge is the loop, and the animation repeats that step. |
| D-63 | the clock is the AnimationController, never React state | locked | A `setState` per frame would re-render the whole Excalidraw app sixty times a second, so the per-frame clock is the library's own **`AnimationController`** (`packages/excalidraw/renderer/animation.ts`). The section and the overlay talk through a **jotai atom** (the `PageSplitState.ts` pattern), so nothing about the animation is written into `appState` — and therefore nothing about it reaches the saved document. Only the **step** that is focused changes, and it changes once per step, not once per frame. |
| D-70 | the clipboard comparison normalises the line endings on **both** sides | locked | The clipboard on Windows hands the text back with **CRLF**, while the code that was stashed has **LF**: `navigator.clipboard.writeText` writes what it is given and the clipboard normalises it on the way in. The comparison was byte-exact, so on Windows the armed paste was **always** declined and the code always arrived as plain text — a row of bordered text boxes, which is what the owner reported. The fix normalises CRLF to LF and a lone CR to LF on **both** sides before comparing, and **nothing else** about the comparison changes: a clipboard that holds different code is still an ordinary paste. It is deliberately **not** "compare the first line" and **not** "compare a prefix" — both would paste the wrong code when the clipboard holds something else. |
| D-71 | the armed paste survives the sidebar closing | locked | Clicking the canvas **closes** the sidebar, which unmounts the document viewer, and the viewer's cleanup cancelled the armed paste — so the owner's own sequence (Copy code, click the canvas, paste) had nothing armed by the time the paste arrived. The unmount cancel was redundant protection: the guard that matters already exists and is better, **`clipboardHoldsCode`**, which refuses a stale arm because a clipboard that no longer holds the code must produce an ordinary paste. The cancel therefore stays for the cases that really are a change of mind — the reader opens a **different** document (the `[entry]` change), the paste is consumed, and the reader goes **back to the file list** (an explicit cancel) — and it no longer runs on unmount. |
| D-72 | the pointer-position veto belongs to the **synthetic** paste only | locked | `lastViewportPosition` holds wherever the pointer last was, and right after clicking **Copy code** that is a point **inside the sidebar**, so the element-under-the-cursor check vetoed the paste and the keyboard paste did nothing at all. For the context menu's paste the pointer is meaningful — the user right-clicked there; for a keyboard paste there is no pointer at all. The veto therefore applies to the paste event the **library creates** (`createPasteEvent`), which is marked as such; the browser's own paste event is trusted and is never vetoed. The marker exists because **jsdom cannot produce a trusted event** (`ClipboardEvent.isTrusted` is an unforgeable own property), so `isTrusted` alone cannot be exercised by a test. The `isWritableElement` half of the guard stays for **both** paths: a paste into a text field must never land on the canvas. |
| D-73 | the sidebar gives the canvas the pointer and the keyboard while the user works on it | locked | The sidebar is `position: absolute` **inside** the editor container and the canvas is sized to the whole container, so the sidebar covers the canvas's right-hand strip: a wheel gesture there scrolled the sidebar instead of zooming the canvas, and a control inside the sidebar kept the keyboard, so Ctrl+V, the arrow keys and Delete went to the sidebar. Once the user clicks the canvas the sidebar **gives up the pointer** (`pointer-events: none`) and the **focus** (the focused control is blurred and the editor container takes it), and it takes the pointer back when the pointer comes back over it — the pointer position is compared with the sidebar's **bounding rect**, not with `elementFromPoint`, because an element with `pointer-events: none` is never returned by it. |
| D-100 | @babel/preset-env @excalidraw/eslint-config @excalidraw/prettier-config @types/chai @types/jest @types/lodash.throttle @types/react @types/react-dom @types/socket.io-client @vitejs/plugin-react @vitest/coverage-v8 @vitest/ui chai dotenv eslint-config-prettier eslint-config-react-app eslint-plugin-import eslint-plugin-prettier http-server husky jsdom lint-staged pepjs prettier rewire rimraf typescript vite vite-plugin-checker vite-plugin-ejs vite-plugin-pwa vite-plugin-svgr vitest vitest-canvas-mock | locked | The fork's pre-existing toolchain, inherited from upstream Excalidraw. Locked here unchanged: Wave 1 neither adds nor upgrades any of them, and the dependency lint in `.tins/kit/src/spec.mjs` requires every root `package.json` dependency to be named in a locked decision row. |
| D-200 | no behavioural change without a row | locked | Any change to the behaviour paths in `tins.json` must add or change a row in this file, or the session close is refused. This is the kit's rule made mechanical, and it is how "never fix a defect in code alone" is enforced. |

## Acceptance — Wave 1

Every row names the test file that checks it. The file must exist and must mention the row's id; the kit's SPEC lint enforces both at gate time.

| id | label | check | detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-1 | vanilla file opens | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pages.test.tsx` | A `.excalidraw` file that has no `pages` in its appState restores to exactly one page, every element is stamped to that page, and **every** element is reachable by the clamped camera. The regression: with the app on a second page, the same file must still open on page one. Test: `AC-1 opens a vanilla file on the first page`. |
| AC-2 | imported content is reachable | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pages.test.tsx` | A scene whose element bounding box exceeds the page rect makes the page grow to contain it, and the clamped camera can then reach every element. Test: `AC-2 grows the page to contain imported content`. |
| AC-3 | scaling geometry | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pages.test.tsx` | _Scale content_ divides the geometry of that page's **anchored** elements by two and leaves **floating** elements untouched; the page rect is left alone, because scaling the content is the only way to get more room on a page (D-2). Scaling down and back up is lossless. The pivot is the page origin, so content keeps its position relative to the page. Test: `AC-3 scales the page content and leaves the page rect alone`. |
| AC-4 | single page delete | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pages.test.tsx` | Deleting one page removes that page and its elements, leaves every other page and its elements untouched, and leaves `currentPageId` pointing at a page that exists. Deleting the last page leaves one empty page. Test: `AC-4 deletes a single page`. |
| AC-5 | restore isolation | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pages.test.tsx` | Restoring a file that defines its own pages keeps **those** pages and stamps elements without a `pageId` to the file's first page. Restoring a file that defines none must not inherit the running app's pages or current page, whatever page the app was on. Test: `AC-5 restores a file's own pages in isolation`. |
| AC-6 | page bar | `excalidraw-app/tests/pageBar.test.tsx` | The page list renders as a virtualised window — a document with 10,000 pages does not create 10,000 DOM nodes, and the window follows the scroll position — and a single-page delete control is present and guarded by the two-step confirmation: the first click only arms it, the second click deletes that page and its content and leaves every other page alone. The pages that follow the deleted one move one pitch to the left **with their content**, so they do not end up empty. Tests: `AC-6 virtualises the page list`, `AC-6 deletes a single page behind the two-step confirmation`, `AC-6 moves the pages that follow the deleted one, with their content`. |
| AC-7 | unbounded pages | `excalidraw-app/tests/pageBar.test.tsx` | 10,000 pages can be created, switched between, and survive a reload through the document store. The page index and the element buckets are written independently — the index first, as a record of its own — so a failed element write cannot lose the page list, and the failure is surfaced instead of swallowed. A bucket whose content did not change is not written again. Tests: `AC-7 persists 10,000 pages through the document store`, `AC-7 switches between 10,000 pages without rendering them all`, `AC-7 rewrites only the page buckets whose content changed`, `AC-7 keeps the page list when an element write fails`. |
| AC-8 | storage fallback | `excalidraw-app/tests/pageBar.test.tsx` | The IndexedDB backend is used when it is available. When IndexedDB cannot be opened, the localStorage backend takes over and the document still persists. The active backend is reported. Tests: `AC-8 uses IndexedDB when it is available`, `AC-8 falls back to localStorage when IndexedDB cannot be opened`. |
| AC-9 | one content scaling axis | `excalidraw-app/tests/pageBar.test.tsx` | The current page offers **one** size control: scaling the content. Scaling down halves that page's content geometry and leaves the page rect alone; it is reversible, and it affects only the current page. The page bar offers **no** grow-page / shrink-page control: growing a page pushed the **later** pages sideways, because the layout is positional, so scaling the content down is the only way to get more room. The content scale survives a reload. Tests: `AC-9 scales the page content on its own`, `AC-9 has no grow or shrink page control`, `AC-9 keeps the content scale across a reload`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 2

Same row format as Wave 1: the check cell names a file that must exist and must mention the row's id.

| id | label | check | detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-10 | paste lands on the current page | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pages.test.tsx` | A paste is an insertion into the live scene, so it belongs to the page the user is on: pasting while page 2 is active puts the pasted element on page 2. The regression: the paste path kept the clipboard's own page (the first page), so the pasted content was in the document — the exporter drew it — but the canvas filtered it out, and the user saw nothing. Test: `AC-10 pastes onto the page being edited`. |
| AC-11 | sidebar insert lands on the current page | `excalidraw-app/tests/pageBar.test.tsx` | The app's sidebar insert path puts the inserted content on the page the user is on: inserting an image while page 2 is active puts the element on page 2. The regression: the insert path built the element with `newImageElement` and no page, so it landed on the first page and was invisible on the canvas. The code card behind the same helper is covered by the same rule. Test: `AC-11 inserts an image onto the page being edited`. |
| AC-12 | the mermaid dialog tests wait for the state they assert | `packages/excalidraw/tests/MermaidToExcalidraw.test.tsx` | The TTD dialog has **two** transient states that the mermaid tests were racing. (1) The dialog renders three things with the class `ttd-dialog-input` — the CodeMirror container, a plain textarea fallback, and a loading placeholder — and starts in the loading state, so the shared `getTextEditor` helper, which waited only for the node to exist, returned the spinner, whose textContent is empty. (2) The dialog parses the editor's content as soon as it mounts, and the editor starts EMPTY before the example text is set; the mocked parser only accepts a definition whose first line is `flowchart TD`, so the first parse rejects and the dialog briefly shows "Invalid mermaid definition" before recovering. The first test waited only for a canvas to exist — but a canvas is rendered in the error state too, just with its container marked `invisible` — so the snapshot was taken at whichever state the dialog happened to be in, and the test failed intermittently under parallel load. Both tests now wait for the state they actually assert. Test: `AC-12 waits for a real editor and not the loading placeholder`. |
| AC-13 | pointer samples are recovered | `packages/common/src/utils.test.ts` | `getPointerSamples()` returns **every** sample the browser coalesced into the event, not only the last one, and falls back to the single event when `getCoalescedEvents` is absent. Tests: `AC-13 returns every coalesced sample, not only the last one`, `AC-13 falls back to the single event when getCoalescedEvents is absent`. |
| AC-14 | a stroke keeps every sample | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pages.test.tsx` | A free-draw stroke whose `pointermove` carries N coalesced samples keeps **all N** points — the stroke's point count tracks the device's samples, not the frame count — and the recorded `pressures` array stays index-aligned with the `points` array. Test: `AC-14 keeps every coalesced sample of a free-draw stroke`. |
| AC-15 | the bounding box is a single pass | `packages/element/tests/bounds.test.ts` | `getSizeFromPoints()` returns the **same** bounds as before — it is checked against the library's own single-pass `getBoundsFromPoints()` — and it does so in a single pass, so it no longer spreads the coordinates into `Math.max()`/`Math.min()` and cannot blow the argument limit on a long stroke. Tests: `AC-15 returns the same bounds as before, in a single pass`, `AC-15 sizes a long stroke without spreading the points into Math.max()`. |

### Also changed, deliberately without an acceptance row

Three **whole-scene scans** were removed from the per-frame path: `updateEmbeddables` is now gated on the element set changing, and `syncAnimatedImageFileIds` and `getAnimatedImageElements` are memoised on the visible elements and the files. `AnimatedImageOverlay` is now wrapped in `React.memo` with a comparator that compares the camera and the viewport by value, because both are rebuilt on every app state change.

This is a **behaviour-preserving optimisation**, not a behaviour change: the same GIFs render, the same embeddables validate, the same output is produced. It is recorded here rather than given an acceptance row because a row would need to assert an _implementation_ detail (how many times a component renders) rather than a behaviour, and this project does not have a render-count harness.

It is covered indirectly and adequately by the existing animated-image and embeddable tests, which still pass. Its purpose is to stop the frame rate collapsing as a document grows — which is what made CAUSE 1 (the discarded pointer samples) visible to the user in the first place.

## Acceptance — Wave 3

Same row format as Wave 1: the check cell names a file that must exist and must mention the row's id.

| id | label | check | detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-16 | the content follows its page | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pages.test.tsx` | Deleting a page in the middle shifts every later page one pitch to the left — and the content of those pages moves with them, so they do not end up empty. The regression: a page's position is **positional** (its x is the sum of the widths of the pages before it plus the spacing) while its content lives in absolute scene coordinates and is never moved when the page list changes, so deleting a page stranded the content of every later page a page to the left of the page that now holds it — the user saw pages 12..22 of a 22-page document as empty. The content of a page is translated by the difference between the page's rect before the change and after it; nothing is scaled, and a **floating** element — which is not owned by its page — is left alone. Test: `AC-16 keeps the later pages' content inside their own pages`. |
| AC-17 | a new page goes after the current one | `excalidraw-app/tests/pageBar.test.tsx` | Inserting a page while the user is on page N inserts it **after** N, and the pages that follow (N+1..) — and their content — shift one pitch to the right. The regression: the new page was appended at the end of the list, so the user's own rule ("if I am on page 9 out of 30 and insert a page it should move pages 10 to 30 by one and insert the page just after 9") was not honoured, and no page's content ever followed its page. Test: `AC-17 inserts a page after the current one and moves the later pages with it`. |
| AC-18 | a legacy scene is tiled across pages | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pages.test.tsx` | The content of a scene that sprawls over more than one page is **tiled**: it is measured, re-anchored onto the scene origin, cut into `pageWidth` x `pageHeight` cells and laid out again, **one cell per page**. The cells that hold no content are dropped, the surviving cells become the pages in **row major** order (the top-left cell first, then left to right, then down), so the board reads in reading order, and each page holds **only** the elements of its own cell — the elements are stamped with the page they landed on. The content is re-anchored so that nothing sits at a negative coordinate any more: the first page's rect always starts at x = 0, so content at a negative coordinate could never be inside a page. The regression: a vanilla file has no pages, so all of its content is stamped onto page 1 — a board of 2,507 elements then hangs outside the page rect the camera is clamped to, so most of it cannot be reached, and every element is drawn on every frame. Test: `AC-18 tiles the content across pages, one cell per page`. |
| AC-19 | tiling leaves what is already fine alone | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pages.test.tsx` | The tiling is a **no-op** for content that already lies inside the first page's rect: the elements come back unchanged (the very same objects) and the page list is the single default page. A **floating** element is never moved — not even when the content around it is tiled — because a floating element is not owned by a page (D-4). Test: `AC-19 leaves content that already fits on one page alone, and never moves a floating element`. |
| AC-20 | a page is as big as its content needs | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pages.test.tsx` | A page's size is the bounding box of the elements assigned to it, measured in **cell-relative** coordinates, with a minimum of `PAGE_WIDTH` x `PAGE_HEIGHT` — so a page grows only as much as its own content needs, and an element that straddles a cell border is never left hanging over the page's edge, where the camera (clamped to the page rect) cannot reach it. A free-draw stroke's origin is _inside_ the stroke rather than at its top-left corner, so a stroke can reach left of / above the cell it was assigned to, and a page — which can only grow to the right and downwards — cannot contain it there: the content of such a cell is therefore pushed into its page by as much as it must be, and by no more. The pages are laid out in the **cumulative** order the library uses (`getPageRectAtIndex`): the pages have differing widths, so they do not share a constant pitch, and they follow one another without overlapping. Test: `AC-20 puts every element fully inside its page and lays the pages out cumulatively`. |
| AC-21 | the offer is made, and the three answers do what they say | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pages.test.tsx` | Opening a file that defines **no pages** and whose content does not fit on one page raises the offer, and each of the three answers does what it says: **split** tiles the content across pages (every element inside its own page), **keep** leaves one big page that contains the content, and **cancel** imports nothing at all and leaves the canvas exactly as it was. Opening a file that **defines** its own pages raises no offer and opens the file as it is. |
| AC-22 | the candidate is computed once | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pages.test.tsx` | The tiling is computed **once**: the page count the offer shows is the number of pages the imported document actually has, and the number of times the tiling primitive is called for one import is one. |

## Acceptance — Wave 4

Same row format as Wave 1: the check cell names a file that must exist and must mention the row's id.

| id | label | check | detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-23 | the right-click paste keeps the code card | `excalidraw-app/tests/codeCardPaste.test.tsx` | A right-click paste of armed code produces a **code card**, not plain text boxes — the same as the keyboard paste. The regression: the armed paste was decided in a `window` capture-phase DOM listener, and the context menu's Paste (`actionPaste`) fires no DOM paste event at all — it calls the library's paste function directly — so the right-click paste never reached the armed path and the code arrived as plain text boxes. The decision now lives in the library's paste path, through the library's `props.onPaste` hook, which both paths go through. Tests: `AC-23 a right-click paste keeps the code-card format`, `AC-23 the keyboard paste produces the same code card`. |
| AC-24 | a paste of something else is an ordinary paste | `excalidraw-app/tests/codeCardPaste.test.tsx` | A paste whose clipboard no longer holds the armed code is an **ordinary** paste: the plain text lands as text, there is no code card, and the armed paste is not spent. The armed paste is consumed only when the clipboard still holds the stashed code (D-18). Test: `AC-24 a paste whose clipboard no longer holds the armed code is an ordinary paste`. |
| AC-25 | selecting in the markdown viewer copies | `excalidraw-app/tests/markdownSelection.test.tsx` | Selecting prose in the markdown viewer puts it on the clipboard, and the same holds for the text of a link. The copy happens on the mouse-up that ends the selection, because a clipboard write needs a transient user activation and `selectionchange` carries none (D-19). A selection that reaches out of the viewer is left to the browser (D-20), and a blocked clipboard is reported with the app's toast, not thrown. Tests: `AC-25 selecting prose puts it on the clipboard`, `AC-25 the text of a link is copied too`, `AC-25 a selection that reaches out of the viewer is left alone`, `AC-25 a blocked clipboard is reported, not thrown`. |
| AC-26 | the reformat is offered on a selection of text | `excalidraw-app/tests/reformatSelection.test.tsx` | The canvas context menu offers **"Reformat as..."** on a selection that holds at least one text element, and **not** on a selection that holds none (a rectangle), and not at all when the host app does not handle the prop. Picking the entry hands the selection over to the app through the library's `onReformatSelection` prop. Test: `AC-26 the context menu offers Reformat as... on a selection of text elements, and not otherwise`. |
| AC-27 | the code card replaces the selection | `excalidraw-app/tests/reformatSelection.test.tsx` | Reformatting a selection as a **code card** replaces it: the original text elements are gone, and the app's own code card — a dark rectangle with the code on it as real text in the code font, one run per line — stands **at the selection's own top-left**. Test: `AC-27 reformatting a selection as a code card replaces the selection with a code card at the selection's position`. |
| AC-28 | the table replaces the selection | `excalidraw-app/tests/reformatSelection.test.tsx` | Reformatting a selection as a **table** recovers the cells of a markdown table — the header and the data rows, with the **separator row dropped** — and draws them: one rectangle per cell, behind ordinary text cells that are selectable (not locked, not bound to a container) and laid out on a grid, with the table starting at the selection's own top-left. Test: `AC-28 reformatting a selection as a table recovers the cells and draws a grid of selectable text cells`. |
| AC-29 | a paste that fits the page is inserted as it is today | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pasteOverflow.test.tsx` | A paste whose lines all lie inside the current page's rect is inserted exactly as it is today — one text element per line, inside the page — and **no offer is raised**. Test: `AC-29 a paste that fits the page is inserted as it is today, with no offer`. |
| AC-30 | the paste that does not fit is offered, and the three answers do what they say | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pasteOverflow.test.tsx` | A paste that does not fit the current page raises the offer, and each of the three answers does what it says: **discard remaining text** places only the lines that fit the page, **place all text on this page** places every line and **grows the page** to contain them, and **place text on multiple pages** splits the text across pages, every line lying entirely inside the page it is on (measured with the library's own `getPageRect` and `getCommonBounds`). Tests: `AC-30 a paste that does not fit raises the offer`, `AC-30 the discard answer places only the text that fits`, `AC-30 the all-on-this-page answer places everything and grows the page`, `AC-30 the multiple-pages answer splits the text across pages`. |
| AC-31 | the text that did not fit is on the clipboard | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pasteOverflow.test.tsx` | The text that was not placed is **written back to the clipboard** after the offer, so the user can paste the rest somewhere else: the clipboard holds exactly the lines that were left over. Test: `AC-31 the text that did not fit is on the clipboard`. |
| AC-32 | the offset is signed | `packages/excalidraw/tests/pages.test.tsx` | A page **bigger than the window** is centred by the camera clamp, and the page-relative offset that reaches the presenter is **negative** — not clamped to zero. The regression: a legacy file's page is grown to hold the whole board, the viewport is then wider than that page, the clamp centres it, and the clamp on the offset (`0..1`) threw the negative anchor away — so the presenter rebuilt a camera 4,478 scene units away from the editor's. Tests: `AC-32 the page-relative offset is negative when the page is bigger than the window`, `AC-32 the offset is not bounded below`. |
| AC-33 | the presenter mirrors the editor's camera | `excalidraw-app/tests/presenterCamera.test.ts` | The presenter's camera, rebuilt from the page-relative offset, is **the editor's camera** — not a different slice of the page. The regression: the editor's negative anchor was clamped to zero on the way out, so the presenter re-anchored the page on its corner and looked at a third of a page away from what the editor showed. Test: `AC-33 the presenter's camera, rebuilt from the offset, is the editor's camera`. |
| AC-34 | the test anchors on the library's page rect | `excalidraw-app/tests/presenterCamera.test.ts` | The presenter camera test anchors on the **library's** page rect (`getCurrentPageRect`), not on a hand-written copy of the page layout. The fork's layout is cumulative and a page is as big as its own content, so a fixed-pitch re-derivation silently stops being the truth — and it is exactly what let the presenter's drift pass the test suite. Test: `AC-34 the presenter camera anchors on the library's page rect`. |
| AC-35 | the presenter mirrors the editor's zoom | `excalidraw-app/tests/presenterCamera.test.ts` | The presenter's zoom floor is the fit zoom of **the page being mirrored**, so the scene extent the presenter shows is the **editor's own** — not a tenth of it at ten times the size. The regression: the floor was the default page size's fit zoom, which for a grown (legacy) page is far too tight, and the mirrored window zoomed in past the editor. Test: `AC-35 the presenter's zoom floor is the page's own fit zoom, not the default page's`. |

The focused step grows about the centre of its own bounds (D-44, D-60). The animation is an overlay (D-42) and its state lives in a jotai atom, never in `appState` (D-63). Test: `AC-51 playing the animation changes nothing in the document`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5a

Same row format as Wave 1: the check cell names a file that must exist and must mention the row's id.

| id | label | check | detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-52 | a panel survives a save and restore | `packages/excalidraw/tests/panel.test.tsx` | A panel survives a **save → restore** round trip with its `posterMode`, its `panelKind`, its `panel` payload and its `posterFileId` intact. This is the test that would have caught the **silent drop**: `restoreElement` has no `default` arm and falls through to `return null` (`packages/excalidraw/data/restore.ts:678`), and `restoreElements` filters on `if (migratedElement)` (`:834`) — so a panel without a `case "panel":` works in the editor and then **vanishes on reload**. Tests: `AC-52 a panel survives a save and restore round trip`, `AC-52 a live panel survives a save and restore round trip too`. |
| AC-53 | a live panel is not painted, a poster panel is | `packages/excalidraw/tests/panel.test.tsx` | A **live** panel is not painted by the canvas — the canvas paints nothing for it, on the canvas path and on the export path alike, because its content is a real DOM surface (a frame baked in with `drawImage` is not a live surface). A **poster** panel **is** painted, at the panel's bounds: the poster is the content's own pixels, stretched to the panel's box. Tests: `AC-53 a live panel is not painted by the canvas`, `AC-53 a poster panel is painted at the panel's bounds`. |
| AC-54 | the overlay's position is the canvas's position | `packages/excalidraw/tests/panelOverlay.test.ts` | The overlay's screen position for a panel equals the position the **canvas** would use, computed **through `sceneCoordsToViewportCoords`** — the oracle, never a re-derivation (D-34) — and checked through the transform string the implementation actually produces, parsed and applied with plain matrix maths. The container's own offset comes **off** again, because the overlay's host sits inside the editor container. Tests: `AC-54 the overlay's screen position for a panel equals the position the canvas would use`, `AC-54 the placement tracks scroll and zoom the way the renderer does`, `AC-54 the panel's centre lands on the canvas's centre`, `AC-54 the panel's box covers exactly the box the renderer covers`. |
| AC-55 | locking produces a poster at the content's native resolution | `packages/excalidraw/tests/panel.test.tsx` | Locking a panel produces a poster at the content's **native** resolution, **regardless of the on-canvas display size**: a panel shown at 400×300 whose content is 1920×1080 produces a 1920×1080 poster, and resizing the panel on the canvas does not move the poster's size. The transition is **reversible**: the live payload is never discarded, so unlocking restores the panel exactly as it was. Tests: `AC-55 locking a panel produces a poster at the content's native resolution`, `AC-55 the poster's size comes from the content, not from the on-canvas size`, `AC-55 locking is reversible and leaves the live payload alone`. |
| AC-56 | cropping a poster gives a **new** image element | `excalidraw-app/tests/panelCrop.test.ts` | Cropping a poster yields a **new image element on the current page** — a new element, pointing at the very same poster file, showing only the cropped region — and it leaves the panel **untouched**. The crop rectangle is in the poster's **own pixels**, so a crop of a poster shown at 400×300 is a crop of the 1920×1080 poster and not of a thumbnail of it. The crop of a poster is app-level new UI (D-68), because the library's crop is an in-place crop of an image element and does not produce a new element. Tests: `AC-56 cropping a poster yields a new image element on the current page`, `AC-56 cropping a poster leaves the panel untouched`, `AC-56 the crop rectangle is in the poster's own pixels, not in the panel's size`, `AC-56 a live panel cannot be cropped`. |
| AC-57 | every dispatch site behaves as the checklist records | `packages/excalidraw/tests/panel.test.tsx` `packages/excalidraw/tests/panelDispatch.test.ts` | For **every** entry in the dispatch checklist, a panel behaves as the recorded decision says. TypeScript does not protect those places — `packages/tsconfig.base.json` sets `strict` but **not** `noImplicitReturns`, so a switch with a `default` arm fails **silently** — and the compiler catches only about a third of the 130 sites; this row and the two check files are what hold the rest honest. The rejects are rejects **on purpose**: a panel is not bindable, not a frame child, not convertible, has no background, no `scale`, no `crop` and no fill, and a live panel may not be added to the library while a poster may. Tests: `AC-57 a panel is an element, a rectanguloid and a rectangle`, `AC-57 a panel is not bindable and not a frame child`, `AC-57 a panel has no background, no scale and no crop`, `AC-57 a panel's bounds are its own rectangle`, `AC-57 a panel's distance and segment maths treat it as a rectangle`, `AC-57 a panel is grabbed by its interior, like an image`, `AC-57 a panel has a shape, and it is the rectangle`, `AC-57 a zero-size panel is not auto-deleted`, `AC-57 a poster panel keeps its aspect ratio and a live panel does not`, `AC-57 the overlay collects the live panels and not the posters`, `AC-57 a poster panel is embedded in the exported SVG`, `AC-57 a live panel exports as nothing`, `AC-57 a panel is not convertible to another shape`, `AC-57 the crop entry point accepts a poster panel`, `AC-57 the panel's chrome has a home in the properties panel`, `AC-57 a live panel may not be added to the library`, `AC-57 the live/poster discriminator is posterMode and not the base locked flag`, `AC-57 a panel is floating: it is not owned by its page`, `AC-57 a restored panel is floating too`, `AC-57 the library mounts the panel overlay, so the presenter gets it for free`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5b (the VS Code bridge)

The **extension half** (the bridge server, the shared protocol, the `.vsix`) is merged; these rows cover both halves, and the app-side rows are the ones this wave added. Same row format as Wave 1: the check cell names the files that must exist and must mention the row's id.

| id | label | check | detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-58 | a connection without the session token is rejected | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/bridge.test.ts` | A WebSocket connection that does **not** present the session token is rejected by the bridge server, and one that presents it is accepted. The token is negotiated **in band**, in the first frame (D-50). Tests: `AC-58 a connection without the session token is rejected`, `AC-58 a connection that presents the token is accepted`. |
| AC-59 | a connection from a non-localhost origin is rejected | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/bridge.test.ts` | A connection whose `Origin` is not localhost is rejected, and so is one with no origin at all: the bridge is on the teacher's own machine, and a page on the internet must not be able to talk to it (D-50). Tests: `AC-59 a connection with a non-localhost origin is rejected`, `AC-59 a connection with no origin at all is rejected`, `AC-59 only a localhost origin is allowed`. |
| AC-60 | a `run:start` produces a `run:started` / `run:output` / `run:exit` sequence in the panel | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/bridge.test.ts` `excalidraw-app/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The **wire**: the server answers a `run:start` with the three frames in that order. The **panel**: the app turns those frames into the panel's state — the run state, the output lines and the console lines — and writes it into the element's payload (D-46, D-51). The **surface**: the panel shows the three regions — the editor, the output and the console (D-51). Tests: `AC-60 a run:start produces a run:started / run:output / run:exit sequence`, `AC-60 a run:start produces a run:started / run:output / run:exit sequence in the panel`, `AC-60 the panel shows the editor, the output and the console`. |
| AC-61 | with no bridge the panel shows the reconnect state, not a blank editor | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/client.test.ts` `excalidraw-app/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | With **no bridge** — the extension is not running, or the token was refused — the client says so, the app writes that state into the panel, and the panel **shows the state** instead of a blank editor: a blank editor is indistinguishable from a program that produced nothing (D-52). Tests: `AC-61 with no bridge, the client shows the reconnect state, not a blank editor`, `AC-61 with no bridge the panel shows the reconnect state, not a blank editor` (the app writes the state), `AC-61 with no bridge the mirror shows the token state, not a blank window`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-331):** the panel that shows the state is the VS Code mirror: with no token it shows an error icon, the sentence that says to paste the token, and the field for it, and no window pretending to be the editor. |
| AC-62 | every message parses under the union, and vice versa | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/bridge.test.ts` | Every message the extension sends parses under the `BridgeMessage` union, every message of the union is one the extension sends, and a frame that is not on the protocol is refused. There is one protocol and one guard (`packages/vscode-bridge/bridge/src/protocol.ts`), so the two sides cannot drift. Tests: `AC-62 every message the extension sends parses under the BridgeMessage union, and vice versa`, `AC-62 a message that is not on the protocol is refused`. |
| AC-63 | the token never appears in a URL or a command line | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/bridge.test.ts` `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/client.test.ts` | The token travels in the **first frame** and nowhere else: not in the WebSocket URL, not in a query string, and not on the extension's command line (D-50). The client **refuses** a URL that carries it. Tests: `AC-63 the extension does not capture the token in a URL or a command line`, `AC-63 the client presents the token in the first frame, and never in the URL`, `AC-63 the client refuses a URL that carries the token`. |
| AC-64 | the `.vsix` builds and is a valid zip, and the manifest may declare a dependency | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/vsix.test.ts` | The extension's `.vsix` builds, and it is a valid zip with `extension/` at its root, `[Content_Types].xml` and `extension.vsixmanifest`. **AMENDED (wave 5j, D-89):** the manifest may now declare a dependency, and the one it declares is `tesseract.js` - the engine is **shipped inside the `.vsix`** as files, so the teacher installs nothing to get OCR (AC-121). Tests: `AC-64 the .vsix builds and is a valid zip with extension/ at its root, [Content_Types].xml and extension.vsixmanifest`, `AC-64 the manifest declares tesseract.js, and nothing else`. |
| AC-80 | the "Paste from clipboard" button fills the field from `navigator.clipboard.readText()` | `excalidraw-app/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The **Bridge** field in the panel's chrome has a **Paste from clipboard** button: it calls `navigator.clipboard.readText()`, and the token it read lands in the field — which is **masked**, because the token is a secret (D-52). The field's button asks the **host** for the token: the library draws the field, and the app owns the clipboard and the connection (D-21's pattern). Tests: `AC-80 the Paste from clipboard button fills the field from navigator.clipboard.readText()`, `AC-80 the paste button asks the host for the token, and the token is masked`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-332):** the field and the button are in the mirror's own body, where the token is needed (no longer in the properties panel); pressing the button reads the clipboard and the app holds the token and connects with it (the body moves on to the connecting state, so the token is looked for in the tab's memory and not in a field that is no longer there). |
| AC-81 | the token is never persisted **beyond the tab** | `excalidraw-app/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The token never outlives the **tab**: it is not in `localStorage`, not in a cookie, not in the URL, and **not in the document** — the panel's payload is saved with the document, so the token must not be in it (D-50, D-52). It IS in the tab's own **session memory** (`sessionStorage`), which survives a reload and is dropped when the tab closes (D-111) — that is what makes the paste **once per tab**. The payload's live state is plain data and survives a structured clone (D-46). Tests: `AC-81 the token is never persisted beyond the tab`, `AC-81 the panel's payload carries the live state, and not the token`. |
| AC-82 | the three connection states render | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` `excalidraw-app/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The panel shows **three** states, because three is what the teacher can act on: **not connected**, **connected** and **rejected** (a wrong token, or the extension is not running). The chrome renders the state it is given, and the app maps the client's status onto those three (D-52). Tests: `AC-82 the mirror shows a state for each of the connection's states`, `AC-82 the three connection states are the states the panel can show`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-331, D-333):** the mirror shows a state for each phase the host names: no token (the field), connecting (a wheel), an extension that does not answer (a wheel and the field), a token that was not accepted (an error and the field), connected (no field). |
| AC-83 | an element the app adds is accepted by the scene | `excalidraw-app/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | A **new** element is born with `index: null` - that is what a null index means: "not yet assigned to the scene". The library assigns the index itself when **it** inserts an element (`scene.insertElementsAtIndex` syncs before the scene sees the set), but a **host app** that adds an element through `updateScene` has to do it itself: the scene **validates** the indices before it syncs them, and an element with no index fails that validation - which in dev and test **throws**. So every place where the app adds an element syncs the indices first (D-74). The test asserts it with the library's own validator as the oracle. Tests: `AC-83 an inserted code panel gets a fractional index, so the scene accepts it`. |
| AC-84 | a connected panel with no editor says what it knows | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | A panel that is **connected** but has no editor content shows **"No file is open in the editor"** in its editor region - it is never a blank rectangle. A blank region looks like a broken panel, and the teacher cannot tell it apart from a program that produced nothing (D-52, D-75). The label already existed (`labels.panel.code.noFile`); it was never rendered. Tests: `AC-84 a connected mirror with no live view yet says what it is waiting for, not a blank region`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-331):** a mirror that is connected and has no live view yet shows a wheel and says it is waiting for the editor's window, with the editor the bridge named under it; there is no editor region to say "no file is open" in. |
| AC-85 | a reconnect replaces the connection line | `excalidraw-app/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The console keeps **one** connection line, not one per reconnect: the latest replaces the previous one, and the other lines are kept. The console rides in the element's payload and the payload is saved with the document, so a line that is added on every reconnect would grow the document for ever (D-75). Tests: `AC-85 a reconnect replaces the connection line instead of adding another one`. |
| AC-86 | the extension always says which editor state it has | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/editorState.test.ts` | The extension publishes the editor state **even when no file is open** - with an empty uri - instead of saying nothing. A message that is not sent is a message the panel cannot explain: the panel has to be able to tell "no file is open" apart from "the extension has not answered yet", and it can only do that if the extension always says which of the two it is (D-75). The rule lives in `packages/vscode-bridge/extension/src/editorState.ts`, which imports nothing from `vscode`, so it is testable without an editor. Tests: `AC-86 with no file open the extension says so instead of saying nothing`, `AC-86 the active editor's text, language and selection are published`. |
| AC-87 | the bridge identifies the editor it runs in | `excalidraw-app/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The bridge names the editor it runs in (`vscode.env.appName`: "Visual Studio Code", "VSCodium") in its `bridge:hello`, and the app shows it - in the panel's chrome and in the panel's console line. The port is **fixed** (D-67), so with two editors running only one of them holds it, and the app reaches that one, not necessarily the one the teacher has their file open in. Without the name, the teacher sees a connected panel and an empty editor and has no way to tell that the two are not talking to each other (D-76). Tests: `AC-87 the bridge identifies the editor it runs in, and the app shows it`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-331):** the mirror says it in the line under its waiting sentence; the console line is unchanged. |
| AC-88 | losing the port is loud and actionable | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/portConflict.test.ts` | An extension that cannot take the port says so, and says what to do: it names the port, says that another editor has it, and gives the two ways out - close that editor, or give this one a different port (`excalidraw-bridge.port`) and point the app at it (`VITE_APP_BRIDGE_URL`). A failure that is NOT the port says what the failure was, and does not blame another editor (D-77). The rule lives in `packages/vscode-bridge/extension/src/portConflict.ts`, which imports nothing from `vscode`. Tests: `AC-88 an extension that cannot take the port says so, and says what to do`, `AC-88 the port-in-use failure is recognised by its code and by its text`, `AC-88 a failure that is not the port says what the failure was`. |
| AC-89 | a hello without the editor's name still connects | `excalidraw-app/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The editor's name is **optional** in the protocol, so an extension that predates the field still connects - it simply does not say which editor it is. The guard accepts a hello with the name and one without it (D-76). Tests: `AC-89 a hello without the editor's name still connects`. |
| AC-90 | the mirror keeps the file when the focus moves away from it | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/editorState.test.ts` | The panel mirrors the file the teacher is teaching from **even when the editor does not have the focus**: the focus moves to the terminal, to the explorer, to the extensions view, and the file is still open and still what the panel should show. The extension therefore **remembers** the last editor and keeps publishing it while its document is open. Publishing the empty state in that moment **erased the mirror** - the code the panel was showing vanished, for a reason the teacher could neither see nor undo (D-78). Tests: `AC-90 the mirror keeps the last editor when the focus moves away from it`, `AC-90 the mirror follows the active editor when there is one`. |
| AC-91 | the mirror is cleared when the file is really closed | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/editorState.test.ts` | The mirror is cleared when the document is actually **closed** - not when the focus merely moved. With no editor at all, there is nothing to mirror. The rule lives in `packages/vscode-bridge/extension/src/editorState.ts` (`editorToMirror`), which imports nothing from `vscode`, so it is testable without an editor. Tests: `AC-91 the mirror is cleared when the file is closed`, `AC-91 with no editor at all there is nothing to mirror`. |
| AC-96 | the active tab is mirrored even when the editor has no focus | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/editorState.test.ts` | The panel mirrors the **active tab's** document even when there is no active editor - which is the case in the commonest moment of all: the teacher teaches from the editor and then switches to the **browser** to show the canvas, and the window loses the focus. The tab is always known, so the panel must keep showing the file the teacher is teaching from (D-79). The tab wins over the remembered editor, and with neither the last editor is still mirrored. Tests: `AC-96 the active tab is mirrored even when the editor has no focus`, `AC-96 the active tab wins over the remembered editor`, `AC-96 with no editor and no tab the last editor is still mirrored`. |
| AC-97 | the tab's document is resolved by its uri | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/editorState.test.ts` | The tab API carries only a **uri** - `TabInputText` has no `document` (verified against the installed declaration, VSCodium 1.135.06055) - so the document is resolved from the editor's list of **open documents** by that uri. A tab that names an open document resolves to it; a tab that names a document that is not open resolves to nothing; a tab that names no text document at all - a terminal, a notebook, a webview - resolves to nothing. Tests: `AC-97 the open document with the tabs uri is the document the tab names`, `AC-97 a tab that names no open document resolves to nothing`, `AC-97 a tab that names no text document at all - a terminal, a notebook - resolves to nothing`. |
