## Acceptance — Wave 5c (the video panel)

The video panel is the owner's item 7: a local video plays in a panel on the canvas, its captions live in an ordinary text element **beside** it, and the panel can be locked into a poster at the video's own size. The local path is the one this wave builds — a file, no bridge, no service, no network. The YouTube path is out of scope; its types and its protocol messages are in (`excalidraw-app/bridge/videoCaptions.ts`), so the wave that fills it in does not change the panel.

| id | label | check | detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-65 | a video panel is created with the right panelKind and source, and the live panel is not painted by the canvas | `packages/excalidraw/tests/videoPanel.test.tsx` | The panel is a `panel` element with `panelKind: "video"` and a **file** source - the video in the document's own file store - and the content's native size in the payload, because that is the size a poster is captured at. A live panel paints **nothing** on the canvas: the canvas is a single raster, and a live surface is a real element in the page (D-45, D-53). Tests: `AC-65 a video panel is created with the right kind and source, and the canvas does not paint it`. |
| AC-66 | a cue change replaces the bound text element text, and the element is on the same page as the panel | `packages/excalidraw/tests/videoPanel.test.tsx` `excalidraw-app/tests/videoPanel.test.tsx` | The cue that is on screen replaces the text of the **bound** element - through the ordinary element machinery, and therefore through undo (D-53) - and the caption element is on the panel's own page, because a caption of a panel on another page would be a caption nobody can see. The caption element is an ordinary text element: the teacher's own element, editable like any other. Tests: `AC-66 a cue change replaces the bound text element's text, and the element is on the panel's own page`, `AC-66 the caption element the insert makes is an ordinary text element`. |
| AC-67 | a video with no caption track creates no caption element | `packages/excalidraw/tests/videoPanel.test.tsx` `excalidraw-app/tests/videoPanel.test.tsx` | "If no caption, no such option" (the owner, 2026-10-21): a video without a caption track gets **no** caption element - not an empty text box, not a placeholder - and nothing is bound to it either. A sidecar that carries no cue is the same thing as no sidecar. Tests: `AC-67 a video with no caption track gets no caption element at all`, `AC-67 a sidecar with no cue is the same as no sidecar at all`. |
| AC-68 | locking a local video panel produces a poster at the videos intrinsic size, not the on-canvas size | `packages/excalidraw/tests/videoPanel.test.tsx` | The poster of a video panel is the frame the teacher is looking at, captured at the video's **own** size - the intrinsic size, never the size the panel is shown at on the canvas (D-55). The test watches the canvas the poster is written from: it is the video's size, and the panel's own size is something else. Tests: `AC-68 locking a local video panel produces a poster at the video's own size, not the panel's`. |
| AC-69 | the OCR request carries the ROI region, and the OCR benchmark is recorded - median and p95, full frame and subtitle crop | `excalidraw-app/tests/ocrRegion.test.ts` | The request carries the region in the image's **own pixels**, so the service crops before it reads - the one OCR lever that costs nothing and buys the most (D-55). And the budget is a gate, not a hope: the benchmark is **recorded** - the median and the p95 of a full 1080p frame and of a subtitle-band crop, the budget (1.5 s and 250 ms), the levers the numbers were taken with, and the verdict - in `excalidraw-app/ocr/benchmark-result.json`. Tests: `AC-69 the OCR request carries the ROI region`, `AC-69 the OCR benchmark is recorded: median and p95, full frame and subtitle crop`, `AC-69 the measured numbers meet the budget`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5d (the presenter panels, and OCR as an in-app capability)

The owner's item 10, in two halves that share the OCR service and the presenter's channel: "presenter should show these video playing like it does so for gif's right now and the live code editor", and OCR as a **separate capability built into the app** (the owner's question 7, 2026-10-21). The good news is the first half needs **no new transport**: the presenter is a second Excalidraw instance, the panel overlay is the library's own, and the channel already carries the whole scene.

| id | label | check | detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-70 | the presenter renders the SAME panel overlay the editor does, from the same element list | `excalidraw-app/tests/presenterPanels.test.tsx` | The presenter is a **second Excalidraw instance**, and the panel overlay is the library's own: the presenter mounts the very same overlay, from the very same element list the editor sent - no second overlay and no second transport (D-57, D-64). The panel in the presenter is the panel the editor sent: the same id, the same kind, and its own live surface. Tests: `AC-70 the presenter renders the same panel overlay the editor does, from the same element list`. |
| AC-71 | a playback position of t equals 5 reaches the presenter, and the presenter does NOT write playback back | `excalidraw-app/tests/presenterPanels.test.tsx` | The editor writes the position onto the **element** and it travels with the scene; the presenter **applies** it - the video is at 5 s - and **never writes one back**, because a view-only host does not write and two windows writing to each other would fight over the same video (D-56). Tests: `AC-71 the playback position reaches the presenter, and the presenter does not write playback back`. |
| AC-72 | a VIDEO binary file travels the presenter:files path, and a file over the size guard is refused with a clear error | `excalidraw-app/tests/presenterFiles.test.tsx` | The video's bytes travel the **existing** files path, on the same on-demand request an image file uses, and the file that arrives is the **video** with its own media type (D-58). The size guard is a **clear error** and not a silent drop: the editor does not send an over-size file and says why, and the presenter does not take one and says why. Tests: `AC-72 a VIDEO binary file travels the presenter:files path, and a file over the size guard is refused with a clear error`. |
| AC-73 | the OCR request carries the ROI region, and the result places PER-LINE text elements over the crop | `excalidraw-app/tests/ocrBridge.test.tsx` | The region travels **in the request**, in the image's own pixels, so the bridge reads only that part of the picture (D-59, D-55). The answer places **one text element per line**, each where its line was read from: the text is an ordinary element of the document, so it is moved, restyled and undone like any other, and it is never burnt into the picture. Tests: `AC-73 the OCR request carries the ROI region, and the result places PER-LINE text elements over the crop`, `AC-73 the placement of a line is the line's own box, scaled to the canvas`. |
| AC-74 | with NO bridge, the OCR option says the bridge is needed - it does not fail silently, and no OCR engine is bundled in the browser | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/ocrClient.test.ts` | With no bridge the read **fails loudly**: the request is refused with the sentence that says the bridge is needed, and the panel shows that sentence (D-59). And there is **no engine in the browser**: the browser's half of the conversation imports none, and no package the browser ships declares one - the engine is `tesseract.js` in the bridge's Node process, and the **extension** is the one that declares it, so that it ships inside the `.vsix` (D-89, AC-64, AC-127). Tests: `AC-74 with no bridge the OCR request fails with the sentence that says the bridge is needed`, `AC-74 the answer settles the request that asked for it`, `AC-74 no OCR engine is bundled in the browser`. |
| AC-75 | the OCR benchmark is recorded - median and p95, full frame and subtitle crop | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/ocrBenchmark.test.ts` | The budget is a **gate**, not a hope: the numbers are recorded from a run **on this machine** - the median and the p95 of a full 1080p frame and of a subtitle-band crop, the budget (1.5 s and 250 ms), the levers the numbers were taken with (one worker, the language data cached, the core not pinned to a single file), and the verdict - in `excalidraw-app/ocr/benchmark-result.json`. The crop budget is measured on the **crop's own** numbers; the full frame's number never stands in for it (D-55, D-59). Tests: `AC-75 the benchmark is recorded - median and p95, full frame and subtitle crop`, `AC-75 the budget is met - a full frame under 1.5 s, a subtitle crop under 250 ms`, `AC-75 the numbers were taken on this machine, with the engine's levers in place`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5e (the VS Code look)

The owner's words, 2026-10-21, after seeing the live code panel for the first time: "i thought i could get the actual vscode editor view to become visible in the canvas where it by default covers 50% of the canvas and why does the canvas look like a text box". The panel **cannot** be a pixel mirror of the VS Code window — the extension API has no screenshot facility of any kind, and a literal window view in the canvas is a screencast, which is the video panel's job (D-51, D-80). What the panel **can** be is the editor's look and the editor's size: the name of the file it is mirroring, the VS Code dark theme, and half of the visible canvas.

| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-92 | the panel's editor region names the file it is mirroring, and says so when there is none | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The editor region carries a **tab strip** that says WHICH file this is, the way a VS Code tab says it: the file's name and its language. The name comes from the uri the bridge already sends (D-51, D-78) — the **last segment** of it (`codePanelFileName`; `file:///home/sai/main.py` is `main.py`). With no file open the tab strip says so, with the wording the panel already uses for that case (`labels.panel.code.noFile`): the teacher is never looking at unnamed code, and unnamed code is what read as a text box (D-80). Tests: `AC-92 the mirror's header names the file it is mirroring, and says VS Code when there is none`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-331):** the mirror's header names the file the window shows (`mirror-panel-title`) and says "VS Code" when no file is open. |
| AC-93 | the mirrored code is rendered in the VS Code dark theme, with its line numbers | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The mirrored code wears the **VS Code dark theme** — the classic #1e1e1e background, the #d4d4d4 default text, and the VS Code token colours (keywords #c586c0, control flow #c586c0, functions #dcdcaa, strings #ce9178, comments #6a9955, variables #9cdcfe — the palette itself is AC-98's, and it is the **semantic** one, D-81) — **whatever the app's theme is**, because the teacher is looking at VS Code while they teach (D-80). The palette is plain data in one place (`components/vscodeTheme.ts`) and the panel's own surface, its tab strip and the editor all read it from there; the line numbers are the editor's own gutter. The **code card** — the thing that gets pasted — keeps its own colours: it is a different surface (D-51). Tests: `AC-93 the mirrored code is rendered in the VS Code dark theme, with its line numbers`. |
| AC-94 | the code region keeps its height: the chrome, the output and the console do not squeeze the code out of the panel | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The code is what the teacher reads, so the editor region has a **floor** (120px) and the output and the console **shrink** (and scroll) instead of taking the code's space (D-80). The contract is inline (`CODE_PANEL_REGION_STYLE`), because it is a rule and not a look. Tests: `AC-94 the output and the console shrink and scroll: they do not push the editor out of the panel`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-331):** the editor region and its floor belonged to the mirrored text, which is gone; what is held is that the output and the console of the code editor shrink and scroll instead of pushing the editor out. |

The owner's own numbers fall out of it: 990 x 1024 on a 1980 x 2048 screen, where the old fixed 520 x 380 was a fifth of it (D-80). Tests: `AC-95 a new code panel is about half the visible canvas, not a constant`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5f (the VS Code truth)

Two corrections, both found by reading the VS Code and VSCodium sources (VS Code 1.144.0, commit 1519239), and both **corrections** rather than preferences (D-81).

**The palette was guessed.** It was written from memory of the classic Dark+ theme, and the **semantic** token colours - the ones VS Code actually paints, because semantic highlighting is on - differ from the TextMate scope colours. The most visible error: VS Code paints a keyword in **#C586C0** (purple), not #569cd6 (blue). #569cd6 is the colour of the bare TextMate scope `keyword`, which the semantic keyword token does not even probe - it probes `keyword.control`. So in a real VS Code, `import`, `def`, `return` and `for` are purple, and the panel painted `import` blue.

**The bridge's own task could take the editor away.** The task `excalidraw-bridge.run` runs the program in a pseudoterminal, and a pseudoterminal is a terminal: where it lands comes from `terminal.integrated.defaultLocation`, whose default is `view` (the terminal **panel**), in which case the editor keeps the focus and `vscode.window.activeTextEditor` is untouched. But when that setting is `editor`, the terminal becomes an editor in the editor area, the active editor becomes the terminal, and `activeTextEditor` becomes `undefined` - exactly the moment the mirror used to blank (D-78). The task must never ask for the focus, whatever the setting says.

| id | label | check | detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-98 | the panel's token colours are the ones VS Code paints | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The palette (`components/vscodeTheme.ts`) is the **semantic** token palette of `extensions/theme-defaults/themes/dark_plus.json` and the chrome of the same file's `colors`, with the TextMate scopes of `dark_vs.json` for the tokens semantic highlighting does not cover: a keyword **#C586C0** (purple, not the blue #569cd6 of the bare `keyword` scope), a comment #6A9955, a string #ce9178, a function #DCDCAA, a type #4EC9B0, a variable #9CDCFE, a number #b5cea8 - and the chrome **#1E1E1E** on **#D4D4D4**. The palette is plain data with no imports, and the panel's editor wears it whatever the app's theme is (D-80, D-81). Tests: `AC-98 the panel paints the VS Code token colours`. |
| AC-99 | the current line is drawn as a border, the way VS Code draws it, and not as a tinted band | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | Neither Dark+ nor Dark Modern defines `editor.lineHighlightBackground`, so VS Code draws the current line as an **outline**: `border: 2px solid <editor.lineHighlightBorder>` (`src/vs/editor/browser/viewParts/currentLineHighlight/`), a 2px solid **#282828** border with `box-sizing: border-box`, and no tint. The panel drew a tinted band (#2a2a2a); it draws the border instead (D-81). Tests: `AC-99 the current line is drawn as a border, the way VS Code draws it, and not as a tinted band`. |
| AC-100 | the bridge's task reveals its terminal without ever asking for the focus | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/taskPresentation.test.ts` | The task `excalidraw-bridge.run` carries presentation options that **reveal** the terminal (`reveal: Always`) and **never take the focus** (`focus: false`) - in VS Code's own task system, `revealTerminal(terminal)` happens on its own and `focusInstance(terminal)` happens only when the presentation asks for the focus (`src/vs/workbench/contrib/tasks/browser/terminalTaskSystem.ts`). The rule does not depend on `terminal.integrated.defaultLocation`: the terminal may land in the panel or in the editor area, and the options are the same either way (D-78, D-81). The rule lives in `packages/vscode-bridge/extension/src/taskPresentation.ts`, which imports nothing from `vscode` and is therefore testable without an editor. Tests: `AC-100 the bridge's task reveals its terminal without ever asking for the focus`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5g (the live editor window)

The owner's ask, 2026-10-22: the code panel should show the ACTUAL VS Code editor window - a live view of it - and not only a text reconstruction of the file. The panel already wears VS Code's look (D-80, D-81); this wave gives it the window itself (D-82).

The **window capture** is `PrintWindow` — the standard Windows way to ask a window to render **itself** into a device context (the flags, the format and the scale are **D-87's**: the client's area, a JPEG at quality 60, scaled to 0.75). It was measured on this machine on 2026-10-22 against a real VS Code and a real VSCodium window: `ffmpeg`'s `gdigrab` returns a **black rectangle** for both of them (they are Electron apps, and gdigrab's window mode BitBlts from a device context that does not hold the GPU-composited surface), and a screen capture shows only what is **visible**, so an editor behind the browser shows the browser. `PrintWindow` draws the editor even when a topmost window covers it. The capture is therefore a **PowerShell helper that ships inside the extension**, spawned **once** for the whole session, and the extension reads the frames off its running stdout. **The transport was changed in wave 5i**: the frames do not travel over the websocket at all any more - the extension serves them as an **MJPEG stream** from its own HTTP server (D-85).

| id | label | check | detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-101 | the **live view draws the editor window live**, at the frame's own aspect ratio | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/windowCapture.test.ts` | The extension streams the frames of its own editor window as an **MJPEG stream**, and the panel draws that stream in the region that holds the mirrored text (`data-testid="code-panel-source"`), under the tab strip - the file's name and its language stay exactly as they were. The live view is a **plain `<img>`** whose `src` is the stream's URL (`data-testid="code-panel-live-frame"`); the picture keeps the aspect ratio it has in the editor because the `<img>` fills the panel with `object-fit: contain` and the browser takes the frame's own size from the stream itself - the app is never told the size and never holds a byte of the picture (D-85). **The app does NO per-frame work**: there is no per-frame data URL, no per-frame registry entry and no per-frame re-render; the panel re-renders when the capture STATE changes. On the extension's side the frames come out of **one** running helper, and the size the extension reports is the size the helper's header line carries (`HEADER <width> <height>`, the **scaled** size). **AMENDED (wave 5h):** the live view is the **opt-in** alternative now - the native editor is the panel's DEFAULT (D-83) - so the panel draws the stream only when the teacher has turned the live view on; the aspect ratio is unchanged. **AMENDED (wave 5i):** the transport is the MJPEG stream (D-85), and the per-frame data URL is gone. **AMENDED (wave 5m):** the live view is the **default** now (D-99), so this row no longer calls it opt-in; the test asks for that view **explicitly**, which is what a panel the teacher switched to shows. Tests: `AC-101 the live view draws the live stream in a plain img`, `AC-101 the extension reads the frames of its own window from one running helper`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-331):** the header over the picture is the mirror's (the file's name, the modes and the tools) and the picture is the whole body. |
| AC-102 | a frame **never** reaches the element payload | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` `excalidraw-app/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The panel's live state rides inside the element's payload (`withCodePanelState`), and the payload is **saved with the document** - so a frame in it would put the teacher's screen into the `.excalidraw` file. **AMENDED (wave 5i): the rule is now STRONGER, and it is structural rather than careful.** The frames do not travel over the websocket at all: the protocol's `editor:frame` message (which carried the base64 PNG) is **gone**, replaced by `editor:capture` (the state, the reason and a sequence number), and the picture itself is served over the extension's own HTTP server as an MJPEG stream (D-85). So a frame **never enters the app's data**: it is never in a message, never in the registry, never in the element's payload and never in the saved document. The registry the **library** owns (`packages/excalidraw/scene/panelFrames.ts`) is still the second channel - never serialized, never inside an element - but what it holds now is the capture's **state** and the stream's URL, not a picture. The test serialises the whole element list after the capture state has arrived and finds **no frame bytes** in it, and the panel's own state carries no frame field at all. Tests: `AC-102 a frame never reaches the element payload`, `AC-102 the app's capture handler writes nothing into the document`. |
| AC-103 | in the **live view**, with no window or with the capture stopped, the panel falls back to the text and says why | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The text mirror is the **fallback** inside the live view, not the alternative: with no window to capture, a helper that cannot start, a helper that died, or a machine that is not Windows, the panel shows the mirrored text **and says why** (D-75) - never a blank region, and never a silent one. The reason travels with the capture **state** (`editor:capture`), not with a frame, so the panel can say it before the first frame has arrived - and now that no frame travels the websocket at all, it says it without ever holding a picture. **AMENDED (wave 5h):** the fallback and the reason belong to the **live view**. In the panel's DEFAULT view the editor is what the teacher asked for, so there is nothing to explain and the reason line is not shown at all (D-83). **AMENDED (wave 5i):** unchanged in substance - the capture state is now the only thing that crosses the wire. **AMENDED (wave 5m):** the default view is the **live** view now (D-99), so the reason belongs to the default view as well; what has nothing to explain is the **editor** view - the one the teacher chose - and the row's second test asks for that view explicitly. Tests: `AC-103 in the editor view there is nothing to explain: the panel shows the editor`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-331):** the mirror has no text to fall back to: with the capture stopped it shows an error icon and the reason (AC-119); the code editor's second test stands. |
| AC-104 | the capture is **one** helper for the whole session | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/windowCapture.test.ts` | The frames are read out of **one** long-running helper whose stdout is a stream of length-prefixed **JPEG**s - four little-endian bytes, then the bytes. A helper process **per frame** is a bug, not a style: it would spawn a process for every frame of the whole lesson, and each of them would capture a different moment. Only the **newest** frame is kept as well - the panel wants the editor as it is now, so frames that arrive together are dropped rather than queued. **AMENDED (wave 5i):** the frames are **JPEG** bytes now, not PNG (D-87), and an unchanged picture is not published again (D-88) - but the helper is still ONE, and the frames are still read off its running stdout. Tests: `AC-104 the capture is one helper, not one per frame`, `AC-104 only the newest frame is kept: the frames are dropped, not queued`. |
| AC-105 | the extension finds its **own** window, and the capture is configurable | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/windowCapture.test.ts` | The extension asks the OS for the handle of its **own** window: the OS **process** name is not the editor's name (`Visual Studio Code` runs as `Code`, `VSCodium` runs as `VSCodium`), and the window is picked by the workspace folder's name in its title (`-like '*<folder>*'` - the title carries a dirty marker in front of it, so it is matched as a substring and not exactly), with a fall back to that process's first main window. The capture is **configurable**: the frame rate is a setting (`excalidraw-bridge.captureFps`, 6 a second by default - measured 5.8 a second end to end through the helper, the raw `PrintWindow` alone costing 12 ms), and the helper is the one that **ships inside the extension**, found relative to the extension's own installed path. There is nothing to install and no dependency to add: PowerShell is on every Windows machine, `child_process` is a Node builtin, and the extension's manifest still has **no `dependencies` field** (AC-64). **AMENDED (wave 5i):** the configurable parts of the **capture** are now the helper's **quality** (`-Quality`, 60) and its **scale** (`-Scale`, 0.75), and the window is still found by handle - now by its **client** rectangle, so the title bar is not in the picture (D-87). Tests: `AC-105 the extension finds its own window`, `AC-105 the capture is configurable`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-338):** the default is 12 frames a second at most. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5h (the native editor is the default again, and it knows the language)

The owner's ask, 2026-10-22: "what happened to native code editor inside the canvas which was the fallback lets make it default" and "day 1 support for javascript, java, python, c, c++ and rust languages". The two go together, because the native editor was applying **one** grammar to EVERY file - the hand-written MERMAID one - so a Python file was highlighted as if it were a mermaid diagram: `def` and `import` were matched by the grammar's "node name" rule and came out as VARIABLE names, not as keywords (D-84).

The **view** is the panel's own (D-83): the native editor is the DEFAULT and the live window is the opt-in alternative, and the teacher flips it from the panel's own header - the header is always mounted, while the panel's chrome is only rendered for a SELECTED panel. The **grammar** comes from a registry the library owns and the APP fills (D-84): the published package grows no dependency, and a language nobody registered gets **no** grammar at all, because an unknown file must never be highlighted as something it is not.

| --- | --- | --- | --- |

The panel's header says which view it is in (`data-view` on the toggle), so the teacher can see what a click will do. **AMENDED (wave 5m):** wave 5h made the native editor the default (D-83), and the owner asked for the opposite on 2026-10-22, so the default is the live window again - and a panel that carries no choice follows it. Tests: `AC-106 the VS Code mirror is the live window, and it has no editor view to switch to`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-331):** the live window is the mirror's only view: there is no editor view and no toggle, and the header carries the mirror's own tools. |
| AC-107 | the teacher turns the live view on **from the panel**, and the choice is the panel's own | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The toggle lives in the panel's OWN header - the header is always mounted, while the panel's chrome (`PanelChrome.tsx`) is rendered only for a SELECTED panel, so a button there would be invisible until the panel was selected, and the teacher must be able to flip the view mid-class without selecting anything (D-83). The button writes through the library's own path (`App.togglePanelView` -> `mutateElement(element, panelViewUpdate(...))`), so the host owns the write, the same way the lock button's does. The view is a property of the panel and it is **persisted** in the panel's payload (`CodePanelContent.view`) together with the flag that says the teacher **chose** it (`CodePanelContent.viewChosen`, D-99), and it **survives the bridge's own writes**: the bridge rewrites that payload whenever the teacher's editor changes - at most every 100 ms, with the last state always published (D-88) - and `withCodePanelState` keeps the view. Tests: `AC-107 the mirror's choices are the panel's own, and the bridge's writes do not wipe them`. **SUPERSEDED IN PART (wave 9a, D-331):** there is no view toggle; what is held is that the teacher's choices (what is mirrored, and following) are the panel's own and the bridge's writes do not wipe them. |
| AC-108 | the editor's grammar follows the **file's** language | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` `excalidraw-app/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The panel already knows the file's language - the bridge sends VS Code's `document.languageId` verbatim (`python`, `java`, `cpp`, `c`, `rust`, ...) - and the panel hands it to the editor, which asks the **registry** for that grammar (D-84). The library's test proves the editor asks for the FILE's language and **not** for the mermaid one; the app's test proves the difference is visible: a Python file paints `def` with the VS Code **keyword** colour (#c586c0) and not with the variable colour (#9cdcfe) the mermaid grammar gave it. The mermaid grammar belongs to the text-to-diagram dialog, which is the only thing that speaks mermaid. Tests: `AC-108 the editor's grammar follows the file's language, and it is not the mermaid one`, `AC-108 a python file is highlighted with the python grammar, not the mermaid one`. |
| AC-109 | the mermaid grammar is for **mermaid** only, and the text-to-diagram dialog keeps it | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The library OWNS the mermaid grammar and registers it itself (`mermaid-lang-lite.ts`), because it has no dependency and the text-to-diagram dialog is the only thing that speaks mermaid; the host never registers it. The dialog asks for it by name (`language="mermaid"`), so the dialog keeps its highlighting - losing it would be a regression (D-84). That is correct, and it is the bug being fixed - every file used to be highlighted as mermaid, which is exactly "an unknown language highlighted as something it is not" (D-84). Tests: `AC-110 a language that is not registered gets NO grammar`. |
| AC-111 | the six day-1 languages are registered by the **app**, and fetched lazily | `excalidraw-app/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The grammars are the **app's** dependencies: `javascript`, `javascriptreact`, `typescript`, `typescriptreact`, `java`, `python`, `c`, `cpp` and `rust` - the six the owner asked for, plus TypeScript and JSX, which come free with the JavaScript package. Each is registered with a **dynamic import**, so a grammar is downloaded only when a file of that language is actually shown, and the app's entry point imports the module, so the registration happens before any editor mounts (D-84). `c` is registered with the C++ grammar: `@codemirror/lang-cpp` is the only C-family grammar CodeMirror ships, and C is a subset of C++. Tests: `AC-111 the app registers the six day-1 languages, and fetches them lazily`. |
| AC-112 | the library's own package.json gains **no** dependency | `excalidraw-app/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The published package `@excalidraw/excalidraw` must not grow the five `@codemirror/lang-*` packages - that would add megabytes to every consumer of the library. The library exposes a REGISTRY (`registerCodeMirrorLanguage`, `loadCodeMirrorLanguage`, `codeMirrorLanguageIds`) and the APP fills it, so the grammar and the editor still share **one** CodeMirror instance: the app resolves the same `@codemirror/state`, `@codemirror/language` and `@codemirror/view` the library already has, because `@codemirror/language` defines its `language` facet per COPY of itself and two copies mean the editor cannot see the grammar at all (D-84). Tests: `AC-112 the library's package.json gains no dependency: the grammars are the app's`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5i (the MJPEG transport, and the coalesced publish)

The owner's ask, 2026-10-22: the panel stops re-rendering on every frame and on every keystroke. Wave 5g proved that the editor's **own window** can be captured (`PrintWindow` on an Electron window), but the app paid for the picture in its main thread: **154,512 bytes** over the websocket for a **115,884-byte** PNG, a JSON parse, a ~154 KB data URL built in JavaScript, a DOM swap and a layout - **3.3 ms** and **5 React re-renders of the panel a second**. This wave changes the **transport**, not the capture: the extension serves the frames as an MJPEG stream (`multipart/x-mixed-replace`) from the HTTP server it already has, and the panel plays that stream in a plain `<img>` - so the browser decodes it natively and the app does **nothing** per frame (D-85). The stream is authorised by a per-connection random capability in the URL path, issued in `bridge:hello`, and the session token is never in a URL (D-86). The capture becomes the **client area**, a JPEG at quality 60, scaled to 0.75 (D-87), an unchanged picture is not published again (D-88), and the editor's text publish is coalesced to at most one every 100 ms with the last state always published (D-88).

| id | label | check | detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-113 | the extension serves the frames as **MJPEG**, and the panel's live view is a plain `<img>` - the app does **NO** per-frame work | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/stream.test.ts` `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The extension's own HTTP server - the one that already carries the websocket upgrade - answers `GET /s/<capability>/frame` with `Content-Type: multipart/x-mixed-replace; boundary=frame`, and every part is `--frame\r\nContent-Type: image/jpeg\r\n\r\n<bytes>\r\n`, with the next part's boundary and header written at once behind it (D-85, D-372). The panel's live view is a **plain `<img>`** whose `src` is that URL: the app never sees a byte of the picture, so there is no base64 data URL to build, no per-frame registry entry and no per-frame React re-render, and the app's per-frame work is **nothing**. The panel re-renders when the capture STATE changes. The bridge's test asserts the response's content type, its boundary and the exact bytes of a part; the library's test asserts that the `<img>`'s `src` IS the stream URL and that it is **not** a `data:` URL. Tests: `AC-113 the extension serves the frames as MJPEG on its own HTTP server`, `AC-113 the panel's live view is a plain img pointed at the stream`. |
| AC-114 | the frames **never travel over the websocket**, and they never reach the element's payload | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/bridge.test.ts` `excalidraw-app/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The protocol has no message that carries a picture: `editor:frame` (the base64 PNG) is **gone** from the union, from `BRIDGE_MESSAGE_TYPES` and from the runtime guard, and `editor:capture` carries only the state, the reason and a sequence number. A frame is published to the **stream** and to nothing else, so it cannot reach the element's payload - the payload is saved with the document, and a picture in it would put the teacher's screen into the `.excalidraw` file (D-82, D-85). The bridge's test publishes a frame and asserts that the client is told **nothing** about it; the app's test feeds the capture state in and asserts that the document carries neither the picture nor the message type. Tests: `AC-114 a frame never travels over the websocket`, `AC-114 the app's capture handler writes nothing into the document`. |
| AC-115 | the stream endpoint refuses a request **without the connection's capability**, and two connections get different capabilities | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/stream.test.ts` | The capability is the authorisation (D-86): 32 hex characters from `randomBytes(16)`, drawn **per connection**, issued in the `bridge:hello` as `streamUrl`, and dead the moment its connection closes. A request that carries an unknown capability gets a **404** - the endpoint answers nobody else. The test opens **two** connections, reads the two `bridge:hello` messages and asserts that the two capabilities are **different**, and then asks the server for the stream with a capability that was never issued and asserts the **404**. Tests: `AC-115 the stream endpoint refuses a request without the connection's capability, and two connections get different capabilities`. |
| AC-116 | the capture is the **client area**, a **JPEG**, and **scaled** - the size, the format and the quality are all asserted | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/windowCapture.test.ts` | `PrintWindow` is called with flags **3** (`PW_CLIENTONLY` and `PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT` together), the bitmap is sized from the **client** rectangle (`GetClientRect` and `ClientToScreen`) and not from the window, so the OS title bar and the borders are gone (D-87). The frame is encoded as a **JPEG** with `Encoder::Quality` at **60**, after a bilinear downscale to **0.75**, and the helper's `HEADER` line reports the **scaled** size. The test asserts the helper's own script (the flags, the client-rectangle calls, the JPEG encoder, the quality and the scale) and the arguments the extension runs it with (`-Quality 60`, `-Scale 0.75`); the length prefix in front of each frame is still four little-endian bytes. Tests: `AC-116 the capture is the client area, a JPEG, and scaled`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-338):** the quality is 70 and the scale is 1 (the window's own size), so the helper does no resampling at the window's own size. |
| AC-117 | an **unchanged picture is not published again** | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/windowCapture.test.ts` | The helper's JPEG encoding is deterministic, so an unchanged editor produces byte-identical JPEG. The extension computes `zlib.crc32` over the encoded bytes and compares it with the CRC of the last published frame; an unchanged picture is **not** published again, so a static editor costs no traffic and no app work at all (D-88). The change detection is in the **extension**, not in the helper - the helper would have to read its own pixels back to know, and the extension already holds the bytes. The test writes the same bytes twice and asserts that the capture published **once**, and then writes a different picture and asserts that it published again. Tests: `AC-117 an unchanged picture is not published again`. |
| AC-118 | the editor's text is published **at most every 100 ms**, and the **last** state is always published | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/publish.test.ts` | A keystroke in the teacher's editor used to rewrite the whole scene, because `onDidChangeTextDocument` fires on every keystroke and the message carries the whole file (D-88). The publish is coalesced: **at most one** every 100 ms, and the **last** state is always published by a trailing timer, so the final text is never lost. The whole-file payload stays - a diff is a bigger change than this wave is for - because the coalescing is what removes the per-keystroke scene rewrite. The test asks for a publish on every tick of a fast typist and asserts that the number of publishes never exceeds one per 100 ms, and that the text the last publish carried is the **last** text. Tests: `AC-118 the editor's text is published at most every 100 ms, and the last state is always published`. |
| AC-119 | the live view falls back to the **text mirror** and says **why** when the capture is unavailable | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The text mirror is the **fallback** inside the live view, not the alternative: with no window to capture, a helper that cannot start, a helper that died, or a machine that is not Windows, the panel shows the mirrored text **and says why** (D-75) - never a blank region, and never a silent one. The reason travels with the capture **state**, so the panel can say it before the first frame has arrived, and now that no frame travels the websocket at all it says it without ever holding a picture. The reason is shown only in the live view: in the default view the teacher did not ask for the live view, so there is nothing to explain (D-83). Tests: `AC-119 a mirror whose live view cannot run shows an error and says why`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-331):** the mirror has no text mirror: when the capture is unavailable it shows an error icon and the reason, and draws the window by itself when it comes back. |
| AC-120 | the helper is spawned **ONCE**, and its frames are read from its running stdout (the **JPEG** path) | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/windowCapture.test.ts` | The frames are read out of **one** long-running helper whose stdout is a stream of length-prefixed **JPEG**s - four little-endian bytes, then the bytes. A helper process **per frame** is a bug, not a style: it would spawn a process for every frame of the whole lesson, and each of them would capture a different moment. The extension spawns the helper **once** and reads the frames off its running stdout; the test asserts that the helper was spawned **once** and that the bytes the extension read are the **JPEG** bytes the helper wrote - not a base64 copy of them, because the picture never becomes text (D-85). Tests: `AC-120 the helper is spawned once, and its frames are read from its running stdout`. |
| AC-121 | the extension's manifest **DECLARES** `tesseract.js`, and the built `.vsix` **SHIPS** it with its wasm core | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/vsix.test.ts` | The teacher installs **nothing** to get OCR: the engine is a **declared dependency** of the extension, and the `.vsix` ships the dependency tree **as files** - `tesseract.js` itself, and `tesseract.js-core` with its `.wasm` cores - because the engine loads its worker and its core from paths at run time, so bundling the extension's TypeScript with esbuild is **not enough** (D-89). The build **fails loudly** when a file it needs is missing: a `.vsix` that installs but cannot read is worse than one that does not install. The run-time install - a teacher who put `tesseract.js` in the project they teach from - stays as the **fallback**, so the declared copy is looked for **first** (D-89). Tests: `AC-121 the extension's manifest declares tesseract.js, and the built .vsix ships it with its wasm core`, `AC-121 the build fails loudly when a file the engine needs is missing`, `AC-121 the declared copy is looked for before the run-time install`. |
| AC-122 | the engine is **chosen by a setting**, and the panel is told which engine is in use | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/ocrEngine.test.ts`, `excalidraw-app/tests/ocrBridge.test.tsx` | The engine is a **choice**: `excalidraw-bridge.ocrEngine` is `tesseract`, `indra` or `paddle`, and it defaults to `tesseract` (D-90). The engine is resolved **once**, at activation, and the panel is **told** which engine is in use - the choice is visible, not a hidden detail. The setting sits in the same `excalidraw-bridge` section as the port and the token, and a value that is not one of the three falls back to the default rather than to a broken engine. Tests: `AC-122 the engine is chosen by a setting, and an unknown value falls back to tesseract`, `AC-122 the panel is told which engine is in use`, `AC-122 the panel says which engine is in use, and says why when there is none`. |
| AC-123 | the Python engines run in a **sidecar spawned ONCE** and reused, and it reports the engines it can reach **without importing them** | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/ocrSidecar.test.ts` | The Python engines are **Python**: they cannot be npm dependencies, so they run as a **sidecar** the extension spawns **once** and talks to over stdio, one JSON object per line - the same shape as the capture helper (D-90). The sidecar reports which engines it can reach with `importlib.util.find_spec`, which **does not execute** the package: asking "is paddleocr there?" must not pay for paddleocr's ten-second import. The chosen engine is imported **lazily**, on the first request, and the model is kept loaded for every later request - the model load is the expensive part. Tests: `AC-123 the sidecar is spawned once and reused for every read`, `AC-123 the sidecar reports the engines it can reach without importing them`, `AC-123 the engine is imported lazily, on the first request`. |
| AC-124 | an engine that is **not installed** is an EXPLICIT state, with the command that installs it | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/ocrSidecar.test.ts` | A missing engine is a **state**, never a silence and never a blank result (D-91): the extension's OCR state is `ready` (with the engine's name) or `unavailable` (with a reason). The reason names the exact command - `pip install indra-ocr` or `pip install paddleocr paddlepaddle` - and for PaddleOCR it also says the truth about this machine: **PaddlePaddle has no build for Python 3.14**; it needs Python 3.12 or 3.13. The sidecar knows its own `sys.version_info`, so it can say it (D-91). Tests: `AC-124 an engine that is not installed is reported with the command that installs it`, `AC-124 the paddle reason says that PaddlePaddle has no build for Python 3.14`. |
| AC-125 | the read result carries **the engine that ran** and **how long it took** | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/ocrClient.test.ts` | The answer says **which engine ran** and **how long it took**, so the panel can show both and the teacher can tell a fast engine from a slow one (D-90). `ocr:request` gains an **optional** `engine` - the app may ask for a specific engine, and the extension falls back to its setting when the field is absent - and `ocr:result` gains an optional `engine` and an optional `durationMs`. Both are optional in the runtime guard, so an extension that predates them still connects (D-50). Tests: `AC-125 the result carries the engine that ran and how long it took`, `AC-125 the request may name an engine, and the runtime guard accepts it`. |
| AC-126 | the benchmark reports **EVERY** engine - its median and p95 for the full frame and the subtitle crop - and says why an engine could not be measured | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/ocrBenchmark.test.ts` | The budget is a gate for **every** engine, not for one (D-90): the record names each engine, gives the **median** and the **p95** for the full frame and for the subtitle crop, and - for an engine that cannot run - says **why** (not installed, no build for this Python). PaddleOCR cannot be installed on this machine's Python 3.14, because **PaddlePaddle ships no cp314 wheel**: that is a fact to **report**, not a failure to hide. Tests: `AC-126 the benchmark reports every engine, with its median and p95 for both cases`, `AC-126 an engine that could not be measured says why`. |
| AC-127 | neither the library nor the app gains a dependency | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/ocrDependencies.test.ts` | The engines belong to the **extension**, and the app talks to the extension over the bridge: `packages/excalidraw/package.json` and `excalidraw-app/package.json` gain **no** dependency (D-89, AC-112). The engine is never in the browser (D-59) - a WASM OCR engine is a multi-megabyte download and a classroom connection is slow. Only the extension's manifest declares one; the **root** `package.json` may carry it as the build-time source the `.vsix` is built from. Tests: `AC-127 neither the library nor the app gains a dependency`, `AC-127 the browser's half of the conversation never imports an engine`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5k (the chrome that survives the zoom, the volume that works, and the panel that is never blank)

The owner looked at the two panels on 2026-10-22 and said the UI was atrocious, and that the video's volume slider did not mute. Both are real, and they have one root cause: a panel is a DOM overlay drawn with a `transform: scale(...)`, so everything inside it is multiplied by the canvas zoom - but the chrome was sized in **fixed CSS pixels**. At 51% zoom the code panel's header measured **11 screen pixels** tall (its text overlapped) and the video's control buttons about **5**, so the controls could not be seen and could not be hit. The chrome is now sized for the **screen** and the content for the **panel** (D-92), the volume and the mute have a test at all (AC-129), and a panel with nothing to show says **why** (D-93, AC-130, AC-131).

| Row | The acceptance | Check | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-128 | the panel's chrome keeps its size **on screen** at any canvas zoom | `packages/excalidraw/tests/panelChrome.test.tsx` | The chrome is sized for the SCREEN and the content for the PANEL (D-92): the code panel's header and the video's control strip are measured at **two** zooms (1 and 0.5) and their **on-screen** size - the size in the panel's own units, multiplied by the panel's own scale - is the **same** at both. The header is tall enough for its own text (22 screen pixels for a 12 pixel font) and the control strip is not squeezed to nothing (26 screen pixels). The scale is the panel element's own `--panel-scale`, and the test reads it **from the DOM**, so a hard-coded scale, or a second place that reads the zoom, fails the test. The chrome's sizes live in ONE place (`scene/panelChrome.ts`) and are applied inline, because a size that cannot be measured cannot be defended. Tests: `AC-128 the panel's chrome keeps its size on screen at any canvas zoom`. |
| AC-129 | the video's volume slider sets the element's `volume`, the mute button sets the element's `muted`, and the control says which state it is in | `packages/excalidraw/tests/videoPanel.test.tsx` | The ELEMENT is the truth and the React state only **mirrors** it: driving the slider sets `video.volume`, clicking the mute button sets `video.muted`, and the control **shows** the state it is in - the button's own label and `aria-pressed` say "Mute"/"Unmute", and the volume slider carries `data-muted`, so a muted panel is readable at a glance and not only in the DOM. The control is also **reachable**: the mute button and the volume slider are measured on screen and are big enough to hit (24 by 24, and 72 by 20 screen pixels), so a future change cannot quietly shrink them back to nothing. There was no volume or mute test at all before this wave. Tests: `AC-129 the video's volume slider sets the element's volume, the mute button sets the element's muted, and the control says which state it is in`. |
| AC-130 | the code panel's source region is **never blank** | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | A region with nothing in it says **why** (D-93, D-75): with the bridge disconnected the panel shows the reconnect state and never an empty editor; with a file open but **no content** in it the source region says that the file is empty; and with no file open it says that no file is open. The words live in the labels, so the panel and the rest of the app say the same thing. Tests: `AC-130 the mirror's body is never blank: each state says why there is no picture`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-331):** it is the mirror's body that is never blank: each state says why there is no picture. |
| AC-131 | the video panel says so when its **file is gone** | `packages/excalidraw/tests/videoPanel.test.tsx` | A video panel whose file is not in the document's store any more says so, in words, instead of showing an empty frame (D-93). A missing file is never an empty frame and never a silence. Tests: `AC-131 the video panel says so when its file is gone`. |
| AC-132 | the video's captions live **OUTSIDE** the player, in the text element beside it | `packages/excalidraw/tests/videoPanel.test.tsx` | The owner's rule of 2026-10-21: the caption the teacher reads is the one in the ordinary text element beside the panel, not the one the player draws. The `<track>` elements carry the cues but **none** of them is `default`, so the player does not draw the caption, and the cue on screen is written into the bound text element. The chrome work must not break this (D-92). Tests: `AC-132 the video's captions live outside the player, in the text element beside it`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5l (the video becomes real: big files, the format the browser cannot play, and the drag path)

The owner reported three video defects on 2026-10-22: a 620 MB `.mkv` he picked from the Files sidebar "doesnt actually allow to play the video", and a video had no drag path like an image has. The cause was in one line of the insert (`excalidraw-app/sidebar/videoPanel.ts:74`): the whole file was read into memory as a base64 data URL (`getDataURL`) and then read **again** for a SHA-1 (`generateIdFromFile`), so the document ended up carrying the video and the insert never survived. The size and the container are **different** problems, and this wave fixes both: the bytes go to a Blob in the origin's own IndexedDB instead of into the document (D-94), the library resolves the media through a registry the host fills (D-95), the presenter resolves it itself and no bytes travel (D-96), a container the browser cannot play says so with the command that converts it (D-97), and the video row drags onto the canvas with a loading wireframe where the player will land (D-98).

| Row | The acceptance | Check | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-133 | a video over the threshold is inserted **without a base64 data URL**, and the document does not carry its bytes | `excalidraw-app/tests/videoMedia.test.ts` | The insert of a video larger than 32 MB must not read the file into a base64 string (D-94). Tests: `AC-133 a video over the threshold is stored as media and not as a base64 data URL`, `AC-133 a video under the threshold still travels in the file store`. |
| AC-134 | the **presenter** resolves a big video from the media store: no bytes travel, and the video plays in the presenter window | `excalidraw-app/tests/presenterMedia.test.ts` | The presenter is the same origin, so it resolves the media itself (D-96). Tests: `AC-134 the presenter resolves a media-backed video from the store without a files message`, `AC-134 the presenter's legacy file guard still refuses a file over 32 MB`. |
| AC-135 | a video the browser **cannot play** says so, and shows the command that converts it | `packages/excalidraw/tests/videoPanel.test.tsx` | The panel must never be blank and must never be silent (D-97). Tests: `AC-135 a video the browser cannot play says why and shows the ffmpeg command`. |
| AC-136 | the video row **drags** onto the canvas, and a loading wireframe stands where the player will land | `excalidraw-app/tests/sidebar-ui.test.tsx` | The video takes the image's drag path (D-98). Tests: `AC-136 a video row drags onto the canvas and the panel lands under the pointer`, `AC-136 a loading wireframe stands where the player will land`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5m (the live window is the default, and the presenter shows it)

The owner reported two symptoms on 2026-10-22: "i need to click live editor (why is it not on by default) to check the output", and "as u can see the vscode is visible in the normal editor but not visible on the presenter". The first is a **default**: wave 5h made the mirrored editor the default at the owner's request, and the owner has now said the opposite — so the live window is the default, and the flip reaches the panels that **already exist**, whose payload says `view: "editor"` only because that was the old default (D-99). The second is **architectural**: the live view's descriptor (the capture's state and the stream's URL) lived in the library's in-memory registry, in the editor's own JavaScript context, and the presenter window is a **second context** with an empty registry — which is why the presenter's live view was black. The editor now publishes that descriptor over the presenter channel and the presenter writes it into its own registry (D-101). The design document is `docs/tins-tasks/wave5m-live-default-brief.md`.

| Row | The acceptance | Check | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-137 | a new code panel shows the **live window**, and a panel the teacher switched keeps the teacher's choice | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The default flips, and the flip reaches the panels that already exist (D-99). Tests: `AC-137 a code panel shows the live window, the ones saved in the editor view too`, `AC-137 a code editor of its own stays the editor across a reload`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-331):** a code panel, new or saved in the editor view, shows the live window; a code editor of its own stays the editor across a reload. |
| AC-138 | the **presenter** shows the live window, and says why when it has nothing to show | `excalidraw-app/tests/presenterPanels.test.tsx` | The presenter writes the editor's descriptor into its own registry (D-101). Tests: `AC-138 the presenter shows the live window from the editor's descriptor`, `AC-138 a presenter with no descriptor says why instead of showing black`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-331, D-332):** with no descriptor the presenter's mirror shows a wheel and the sentence that the window has not arrived, with no header and no text. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5o (the PaddleOCR fall-back)

The owner installed PaddleOCR on 2026-10-22 to have a faster OCR engine, and on this machine it **crashes before it reads anything**: PaddlePaddle 3.3.1 on Windows, with the PIR API, hits an unimplemented conversion in the **oneDNN** instruction of the new executor (`NotImplementedError: (Unimplemented) ConvertPirAttribute2RuntimeAttribute not support [pir::ArrayAttribute<pir::DoubleAttribute>]`). The sidecar reports the engine as **available** - it probes the module with `find_spec` - and then fails on the first real read, which is the worst shape: a state that says yes and then cannot do the work. Every way of switching oneDNN off from the outside was tried and **none** of them worked; the only configuration that works is **`PaddleOCR(enable_mkldnn=False)`**. So the sidecar tries the **fast** path first and falls back to the slow one **only** when the fast one has actually failed, **once**, remembers the model that works, and **says so** - because on this CPU-only box the slow path costs about 28 seconds a read, against 278 ms for tesseract and 147 ms for indra (D-91: a fact to report, not to hide).

| Row | The acceptance | Check | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-143 | an engine that **crashes on its first read** is retried with the slow path and then **works** | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/ocrSidecar.test.ts` | The sidecar must survive the crash the owner actually hit (D-106). Tests: `AC-143 a paddle read that raises the oneDNN error is retried with mkldnn disabled and then succeeds`, `AC-143 the answer says that the engine ran without oneDNN`. |
| AC-144 | the model is built **once**, and the fall-back is remembered for the later reads | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/ocrSidecar.test.ts` | The model load is paid once (D-107). Tests: `AC-144 the model is built once and every later read reuses it`, `AC-144 the ready line says how the paddle model was built`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5p (the chrome's icon-only buttons, and the record that told the truth about paddle)

Wave 5n added a screenshot button to a panel's chrome, and it was **measured** in the owner's app after 5n merged: the button came out **zero pixels wide** — unclickable, and the same complaint the owner made on 2026-10-22 ("there is a extra options panel and i dont undertand y there are unclickable buttons"). The cause is in the **layout**, not in the style: the button is a flex item of the panel's header, its `flex` is `0 1 auto` so the flexbox may shrink it, its content is an `<svg>` with a `viewBox` and **no intrinsic width**, and the stylesheet sizes that svg in **percentages** of the button — so the button's width depends on its content and the content depends on the width, and the browser resolves the circle to **zero**. jsdom cannot lay out a flexbox, which is why every unit test passed. So the fix is two mechanical rules (D-108): the button's **inline** style carries `flex-shrink: 0` (the flexbox may not shrink a control whose size is already decided), and the stylesheet gives the icon an **absolute** size (so the icon never depends on the button's width at all). The video's **play** and **mute** buttons are the control group: they carry a glyph as text, so their min-content width is the glyph and they do not collapse today — but the same rule now keeps their size as well. The second thing this wave fixes is the **record**: `excalidraw-app/ocr/benchmark-result.json` said paddle was unavailable, which was true of the **machine** the record was written on (Python 3.14 has no paddle wheel) and is not true of **paddle**: the owner installed paddleocr 3.7.0 / paddlepaddle 3.3.1 under Python 3.12.10, and wave 5o measured it for real through the shipped sidecar. The record now says **where** every engine's numbers come from (D-109).

| Row | The acceptance | Check | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-145 | the screenshot button keeps its width on screen, and it is **measured** in a real browser | `packages/excalidraw/tests/screenshot.test.tsx` | The button must not collapse (D-108). The unit test asserts the **style** that makes it impossible (the inline `flex-shrink: 0` and the width in **screen pixels** - the button now lives in the properties panel, so its width is no longer divided by the panel's scale); the **on-screen** size is a separate, recorded measurement - jsdom cannot lay out a flexbox, so the screen measurement is made in the real app and recorded in the close note, not asserted by the test. Tests: `AC-145 the screenshot button's inline style keeps its width and cannot be shrunk`, `AC-145 the stylesheet gives the chrome's icon an absolute size, not a percentage`. |
| AC-146 | the benchmark record tells the truth about paddle, with its provenance | `excalidraw-app/tests/ocrBenchmark.test.ts` | The record must not claim paddle is unavailable when it was measured (D-109). Tests: `AC-146 the benchmark record names paddle as measured elsewhere, with its numbers and its Python`, `AC-146 the benchmark test passes whether or not an engine is installed here`. |

---

## Acceptance — Wave 5q (the settings come with the extension, and the token survives a reload)

The owner asked on 2026-10-23: "cant u just edit and recreate the .vsix with these backed in?" - naming three settings: the bridge token, the OCR engine (`paddle`) and the Python path. Two of them name a **tool**, and a `.vsix` carries **defaults**, so the extension ships them and installing it is enough (D-110). The third is a **secret**: the `.vsix` is a published artifact in a public repository and the token is per-session by design (D-50), so it does not ship. The friction it was meant to remove goes a better way instead: the app keeps the token in the **tab's session memory** (`sessionStorage`), so the teacher pastes it once per tab instead of once per reload, and it is still never in `localStorage`, never in a URL and never in the document (D-111).

| Row | The acceptance | Check | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-147 | the extension's manifest ships the engine and the Python path as its defaults, and ships **no** token | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/vsix.test.ts` | The settings come with the extension (D-110). Tests: `AC-147 the manifest's default engine is paddle and the default Python path is the owner's interpreter`, `AC-147 the manifest ships no token value`. |
| AC-148 | the app's token survives a reload and dies with the tab | `excalidraw-app/tests/bridgeSession.test.ts` | The paste becomes once per tab (D-111). Tests: `AC-148 the token is restored from the tab's session memory after a reload`, `AC-148 the token is never written to localStorage and never appears in a URL`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5t (the live view survives a reload, a hidden window and a dead helper)

The owner tested the live view on 2026-10-23 and reported: "before reloading the page the view is visible but after reloading the page the editor becomes black ... after changing pages the vscode window needs to be selected (refocused) before the connection resumes again (this works for exactly 2 times and 3rd time onwards doesnt seem to work black box on both the presenter and editor ...)" - and their screenshot shows the panel's own message, **"The live window has not started yet."** The cause is in the extension, and it is in two places: the helper **resolved the editor's window once** and looped with that handle (and `MainWindowHandle` is 0 for a hidden window, and **changes** when VS Code recreates its window), and the extension **started the capture once per activation** and never restarted a dead one. So the window is now found **by rule** and re-resolved whenever the picture stops coming (D-113), the helper **waits and says what is wrong** instead of exiting (D-112), and the extension **restarts** a helper that has died and re-arms the capture when a client connects - so a reload, a hidden window and a Reload Window all recover **by themselves**.

| Row | The acceptance | Check | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-149 | the helper **re-resolves** the window when the capture comes back empty, and the extension restarts a dead helper | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/windowCapture.test.ts` | The capture must recover (D-112). Tests: `AC-149 the helper re-resolves the window when a capture comes back empty`, `AC-149 the extension restarts a helper that exited`. |
| AC-150 | the window is found **without being the foreground window**, and "not visible" is its own message | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/windowCapture.test.ts` | The lookup must not depend on the focus (D-113). Tests: `AC-150 a window that is not the foreground window is still found`, `AC-150 a window that exists but is not visible is reported as not visible, not as missing`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5u (the screenshot must be **visible**, and it must work on every panel)

The owner tested the screenshot on 2026-10-23 and reported five things: "i get a X button after clicking the camera icon" (5.1-5.3 - the three choices never arrived), "video does not get a camera icon at all" (5.4), "normal image doesnot get this option" (5.5), and "the whole right corner of the wireframe cannot be used to resize the panel (no screen shot icon (needs to be attached to the wireframe and visible even if i did not select the wireframe so i can take screenshot (after clicking this button the preview of the screenshot should appear as if it has moved up in the z axis and the canvas/ editor has moved back in the z - axis (visual effect only darkens background, leaving the screenshot to become the thing in focus with those 3 options i mentioned, also add a cropping tool that is turned on by default so we can crop the screenshot and revert the crop back one step aswell)))" (3.2a, 3.4). The causes are three, and each of them is a rule the chrome broke: the choice strip's buttons had a height and **no width**, and the strip's position came from the caller (D-114); the camera lived inside a panel's **content**, so a media-backed video had none at all and a control that acts on a panel was reachable only where the content happened to draw it (D-115); and a capture produced a picture with nothing **in front** - no dimming, no crop (D-116). And the OCR entry for an ordinary image asked for an image that was **already selected**, so a right-click on an image that was not selected hid it.

| Row | The acceptance | Check | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-151 | the choice strip's three buttons are **visible and hit-testable**, and the strip hangs on the panel | `packages/excalidraw/tests/screenshot.test.tsx` | The strip must be a real control (D-114). Tests: `AC-151 the choice strip's three buttons each have a width and a height`, `AC-151 the strip is positioned inside the panel's own rect`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-339):** the strip is gone; the same claim is held for the crop window's buttons - large, and the flexbox may not shrink them - and for the bar, which is across the top of the window and not on the panel. Tests: `AC-151 the crop window's buttons are large, and the flexbox may not shrink them`, `AC-151 the bar of choices is across the top of the crop window, whatever the panel is`. |
| AC-152 | **every** panel kind carries a camera, and it is reachable **from the panel's properties** | `packages/excalidraw/tests/screenshot.test.tsx` | The camera is a choice about a panel, so it lives where the panel's other choices are (D-135): the properties panel, which is ordinary DOM beside the canvas, so the button always works - a button drawn on top of a YouTube panel's picture is a button under an **iframe**, and an iframe takes the click. The teacher reaches the camera by **selecting the panel**; the picture itself carries no button at all. Tests: `AC-152 a video panel carries a screenshot button`, `AC-152 the screenshot button is reachable from the panel's properties`. |
| AC-153 | the capture opens a **focus state**: the canvas dims, the preview comes forward, and the picture can be cropped with one step of revert | `packages/excalidraw/tests/screenshot.test.tsx` | The z-axis effect and the crop (D-116). Tests: `AC-153 a pending screenshot dims the canvas and presents the preview in front`, `AC-153 the crop can be reverted once to the uncropped capture`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-339):** the capture opens an opaque crop window with the picture in it, over the app's whole chrome, with no dimming. Test: `AC-153 a pending screenshot opens an opaque crop window with the picture in it`. |
| AC-154 | an ordinary **image** offers "Read the text" from the canvas's own context menu, **whether or not it was selected first** | `excalidraw-app/tests/useOcr.test.tsx` | The owner's item 5.5: "normal image doesnot get this option". The action exists, the app passes `onOcrImage` (`excalidraw-app/App.tsx:1308`) and the action is in the context menu's list (`packages/excalidraw/components/App.tsx:14119`) - but its **predicate** asked for an image that was **already selected** (`actionOcrImage.ts:43-46`: `element.type === "image" && !!appState.selectedElementIds[element.id]`), so the entry belonged to the **selection** and not to the element the menu was about. The right-click selects the element under the pointer, so the plain case worked; the entry hid itself as soon as the selection was not the picture itself. The menu now remembers the element it is **about** (`appState.contextMenu.elementId`) and the entry follows **that** - so the picture is read whether or not it was selected first, and a rectangle still gets no read. Tests: `AC-154 the context menu of an image offers the ocr entry even when the image was not selected`, `AC-154 the ocr entry is not offered for an element that is not an image`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5w (the editor is a real editor: the teacher's own text, and the Run control)

The owner reviewed the app on 2026-10-23 (item 3.2c): "clicking the live window turns on editor which is a black box - i assumed it would be like leetcode's editor instead where i can run the code after writing it out, this was the bypass i was talking about this being there even if i dont connect to a live window". The editor view was a **mirror**: a CodeMirror that showed what the extension published and could not be typed into, so with no bridge there was nothing to show at all. The owner was asked whether the runnable editor should be a panel of its own or the panel they already had, and answered **"same panel"**. So the code panel gains a **draft** (the teacher's own text, on the panel, so it lives in the document), the mirror becomes the draft's **starting point** instead of something that overwrites it, the editor becomes **editable**, and a **Run** control sends the draft and the language to the extension, which writes it to a temporary file in the teacher's workspace, runs it with that language's runtime and returns the output - with a **timeout** and a **bounded output**, both reported.

| Row | The acceptance | Check | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-161 | the editor view is **editable**, and the teacher's text is not overwritten by the mirror | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The draft is the teacher's (D-123). Tests: `AC-161 the teacher can type in the editor view`, `AC-161 a mirror update does not overwrite a panel the teacher has edited`. |
| AC-162 | **Run** sends the draft to the bridge and shows the output in the panel | `excalidraw-app/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The run path (D-124). Tests: `AC-162 the run control sends the draft and the language to the bridge`, `AC-162 the output arrives in the panel's output region`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-331):** the Run control is the code editor's, and the editor shares the session the mirror connected: the test connects through the mirror's field and runs a language only VS Code runs. |
| AC-163 | a run that times out, or that prints too much, is **reported**, and a missing runtime is an **explicit** state | `packages/vscode-bridge/tests/bridge.test.ts` | No run lies (D-124). Tests: `AC-163 a run that exceeds the timeout is reported as timed out`, `AC-163 a language whose runtime is missing says so instead of running`. |
| AC-164 | the editor is usable **with no bridge at all** | `packages/excalidraw/tests/codePanel.test.tsx` | The owner's bypass (D-123). Tests: `AC-164 the code editor works with no bridge and says that running needs one`. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-331):** it is the code editor of its own that works with no bridge; it does not report a connection. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5r (the video's failure message must be true, and there is a fallback)

The owner wrote on 2026-10-23: "i was able to play .mkv in the earlier implementation of using base 64 maybe add that as fallback and also mention if the fallback also cannot play the file". Wave 5l shipped a panel that said _"This browser cannot play a {{format}} video."_ - a claim that was never measured, and that is **false**: Chrome demuxes Matroska (measured on 2026-10-23 with a real H.264 `.mkv`: `canPlayType("video/x-matroska")` answers `"maybe"`, a blob URL plays it with or without a type, and a data URL with an explicit type plays it). What broke the owner's 620 MB `.mkv` was never the container but the **insert** - the base64 of the whole file, twice - which wave 5l fixed. So the failure message becomes the browser's verdict about **the file**, the panel gets a **fallback** for the bytes (the other way of reaching them, tried once, never rebuilding a base64 string), and an error from a source that has already been replaced is ignored.

| Row | The acceptance | Check | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-165 | the failure message names the **file**, not the container, and does not claim a browser cannot play a container it can | `packages/excalidraw/tests/videoPanel.test.tsx` | The message must be true (D-125). Tests: `AC-165 the failure message does not claim the browser cannot play the format`, `AC-165 the failure message names the codec as the usual cause and keeps the ffmpeg remedy`. |
| AC-166 | the panel tries the **fallback** once and says whether it worked | `packages/excalidraw/tests/videoPanel.test.tsx` | The fallback and the mention (D-126). Tests: `AC-166 the panel asks for a fresh url when the first one fails`, `AC-166 the panel says the fallback was tried and that it failed too`. |
| AC-167 | an error from a **replaced** source is ignored | `packages/excalidraw/tests/videoPanel.test.tsx` | A stale source's error is not a fact about the file (D-127). Tests: `AC-167 an error from a replaced url does not report a playback failure`. |

## Acceptance — Wave 5s (the YouTube video: an iframe, and captions that say which tier answered)

The owner asked on 2026-10-22: "how do u insert outube videos" - and then, on 2026-10-23: "why not build the youtube one also (is this prone to break with any update that youtube makes ? if so hold)". The answer is the shape of the feature: the **video** is an iframe of YouTube's own player, so there is nothing of ours that talks to their pages and nothing that can rot (D-128). The **captions** are the fragile half, so they degrade in tiers and say which tier answered (D-129).

## Acceptance — Wave 5x (the YouTube video keeps its 16:9, and the caption note floats over it)

The wave 5s panel shipped the video and the captions, and the **live pass in a real browser** found what the tests could not see: the caption note was stacked **below** the iframe and took 130 of the panel's 270 units, so the iframe was only 480x140 - a 3.45:1 box - and YouTube's own player filled it, so the video was **stretched horizontally**. The panel is the video, so the iframe now **fills** the panel and the note **floats over** its top edge as one line, with the whole reason in the tooltip (D-131, D-132). The payload, the tier, the text and the protocol do not change: this wave is **layout only**.

| Row | The acceptance | Check | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC-171 | the YouTube panel's iframe **fills** the panel, and the note **overlays** it | `packages/excalidraw/tests/youtubePanel.test.tsx` | The video is the panel (D-131) and the note is an annotation (D-131, D-132). Tests: `AC-171 the youtube iframe fills the whole panel`, `AC-171 the caption note overlays the video and carries the whole reason as its tooltip`. |

The connection line is a **status**, and a status is the truth **now** (D-133).

## Acceptance — Wave 5z (the OCR sentence must name the engine the panel will use)

The **live pass in a real browser** found a **lie** in the panel's OCR row: the picker showed `indra` - the engine the teacher chose for that panel - while the sentence underneath named `paddle`, the extension's own setting. The panel sends the chosen engine with every OCR request (D-118), so `indra` is the engine that will run (147 ms against 27,822 ms on this box). The panel's sentence names **what will happen** (D-134).
