| D-276 | a pasted text that fits the page is one text box | locked | A multi-line paste that fits on the current page (wrapped, shorter than 90% of the page) becomes **one text box** with all its lines, and not a box for each line: a teacher can drag the side of one box to make its text flow in new lines, which a box for each line could not do. A paste that is too tall for the page keeps a box for each line, because the offer to split it (D-24, D-25) works on lines. Amends D-25. |
| D-277 | what an OCR read found is shown first, in the shape it had | locked | A read (of a panel, of a picture, of a crop) no longer puts a text box for each line on the canvas where the picture was. It opens a **preview** with the text as the picture had it: the lines in reading order, each with the **indent** it had (its distance from the leftmost line in characters, the width of a character being the median of the lines' own) and a blank line where the picture had a gap of more than one and a half lines. The text can be corrected there. The teacher then chooses: **Paste as code block** (a code card in the language they pick, default Python), **Paste as text** (one monospace text block that keeps its indents), **Copy** or **Cancel**. The block lands in the middle of the current page and the view moves to it (D-275). Nothing is put on the page before they choose. |
| D-278 | a screenshot can always be placed or left | locked | The three choices of a screenshot (place on this page, read the text, place on a new page) and a **Cancel** are shown on the screen with the picture, at the bottom of the focus state, and not only on the panel's own edge, which can be out of view. In the last step (placing) only Cancel is offered. **Esc** leaves the focus state at every step and drops the picture, so the teacher never has to reload the page to get out of it. |
| D-279 | PDFs are drawn by the app, with text that can be selected | locked | The document viewer no longer hands a PDF to the browser's own plug-in ("Inline PDF preview is unavailable in this browser"). It draws the pages itself with **pdf.js**, at the viewer's zoom, with the page's **real text** laid over the picture, so the selection bar of the markdown viewer (D-263) works on a PDF too. A page is read with OCR (D-277) from the toolbar's **Read this page** (D-302, D-307), for scanned pages. pdf.js is **not an npm dependency**: it is fetched once, from a pinned version (4.10.38), the first time a PDF is opened - the same way as the in-browser Python runtime (D-268) - and a failed fetch says why and is tried again next time. A document with more than 300 pages shows the first 300 and says so. |
| D-280 | a deleted page is kept and can be brought back, also after a reload | locked | Undo (D-260) brings a page back while the tab is open. Each page that is deleted is **also kept in the browser's IndexedDB** (`page-trash-db`), with its name and size, what was on it and the files those elements need, so it can be brought back after a reload, or long after the undo stack has moved on. The newest **100** are kept and older ones dropped. The page bar shows a **Deleted pages** button (with the count) when there are any: the list gives each page's name, how many elements it had and when it went, with **Restore** and **Forget**, and says how much of the browser's storage the site uses (`navigator.storage.estimate`). Restoring adds the page **after the last page** with what was on it in the same place on the page, so no other page moves, and the view goes to it. This replaces keeping the whole undo history across a reload, which is left to a later decision. |
| D-269 | a code panel is a **code editor** of its own, or a **mirror** of VS Code | locked | The menu offers **Code editor (in the browser)** and **VS Code mirror (bridge)**. The editor of its own has no bridge: it opens on its editor, shows no bridge field and no "not connected" line, never takes the text of a mirrored editor, and **always runs in the browser** (Python and JavaScript, D-272) even when a bridge is connected. The mirror is what it was. The two kinds are told apart by the panel's own `standalone` flag. They share the run output of the page, so a run in one is shown in the other when both are on the page. |
| D-281 | the presenter shows a panel that is full screen in the editor | locked | When a panel goes full screen in the editor (D-271), the presenter window **frames that panel across its whole window**, with a small margin and the panel in the middle, and keeps it there whatever the editor's camera does; when the panel leaves full screen, the presenter follows the editor's camera again. The editor tells the presenter with `presenter:focus-panel` (the panel's id, or none), sent when the browser's `fullscreenchange` fires and again to a presenter that opens while a panel is full screen. |
| D-282 | the editor has two themes, and the whole panel wears them | locked | Amends D-270: the themes are **Dark** and **Light**; VS Code Dark is gone as a choice and a panel saved with it reads as Dark. The theme paints the **whole panel**, not only the text area: the bars, the language and theme pickers, the console, its input, the dividers and the borders take their colours from the theme's palette. |
| D-283 | a program can read input | locked | The console has an **input box**: the lines the program reads with `input()`, typed before Run, kept in the panel. Each `input()` takes the next line; when the program asks for more than was typed the console asks for it and the program goes on with the answer (D-298), so the box holds the answers typed **in advance**, and is optional. |
| D-284 | the output of Python is exact | locked | Python's output is taken as the bytes it wrote, so `print("*", end="")` joins on one line and `print("\n", end="")` ends it, exactly as in a terminal. The earlier line mode lost the newlines and a pattern came out as one row. |
| D-285 | a run that ends badly says why | locked | A program stopped at the 10 second limit says it **may be an infinite loop**, or needs longer; one stopped at the output limit says the output reached its limit; an error that a teacher cannot read is followed by a plain hint (a recursion that is too deep, too much memory, input that ran out); a worker that dies while the program runs is reported as a crash (the browser ran out of memory, or the program crashed) and not as a failed download. The browser can end a worker that uses too much memory without a word, and that case can only be reported, not prevented. |
| D-286 | text goes on the page as a flexible box | locked | Pasted text that fits the page, a text sent from the viewer ("As a text box") and the text of an OCR read are **text boxes with a width of their own**: the box wraps its text, keeps the whole text, and a side can be dragged to make the text flow in the new width. The text of an OCR read keeps its indents in a monospace face. A table's columns are measured **after its font has loaded**, so the text no longer spills over a cell. |
| D-287 | the PDF reader zooms, pans and fits | locked | The reader opens **fitted to the panel's width**. Ctrl + the wheel (and a pinch) zooms, the plain wheel scrolls; the middle button drags the page about (a plain drag selects text, D-302); **Fit width** and **Fit page** are one click each; the zoom goes from 20% to 400%. A page with **no text** says it looks scanned and where to read it from (the toolbar's **Read this page**, D-307). The selection bar offers the same choices as for markdown: code card, flexible text box, table. |
| D-288 | OCR works in the browser, and shows how far it is | locked | With no bridge a picture is read by **tesseract.js**, fetched the first time (a pinned version from a CDN, not an npm dependency) and run in a worker, English; the worker is kept so the language data is loaded once and a failed download is tried again. A **progress bar** says what it is doing (the reader, the language, the reading) and how far, with a Stop button; a read that finds nothing, or fails, says so with the reason. With the bridge connected the engine ladder is as before. |
| D-289 | languages are packs, downloaded once | locked | The language picker lists Python, JavaScript, Go, Java, C and C++ with where each stands: built in, **a download icon** (a click starts the download and keeps the language until it is done, with a bar for how far), downloading, or **not available yet**. A pack is downloaded once and remembered by the browser. Python and JavaScript are built in; Go, Java, C and C++ stay **not available yet** until a runtime with a verified source and size is added for each, so the picker never offers a download that fails - except that a language VS Code runs on the teacher's machine is ready, and is run there (D-290). |
| D-290 | a language the connected VS Code runs is run there, from the browser editor too | locked | The languages VS Code runs came back to the code editor. When VS Code is connected and found a runtime on the teacher's machine (python, node, java, go, c, ...), the language picker lists it as **runs in VS Code** and Run sends the draft to VS Code to run natively - also from a code editor of its own, which had stopped doing so when it began to run everything in the browser (D-269). Python and JavaScript in a code editor of its own still run in the browser, with VS Code connected or not; a mirrored editor prefers VS Code's own runtime when it has one. A language with no runtime anywhere says what is missing: VS Code names the missing runtime, and with no bridge the panel says the bridge is needed. |
| D-291 | the teacher chooses what part of a page is read, before it is read | locked | The owner: "i want to select the area to scan before ... a preview of the full page with cropping ability first, then OCR only the cropped section". Read the text on a PDF page or an image first shows the **whole page** and asks what to read: the teacher drags out an **area** (a paragraph, a table, a few lines of code), or reads the whole page, or cancels. Only the area goes to the reader, as a region in the picture's own pixels, so the read is quicker and the text is only what they wanted. A panel keeps its own crop dialog (D-137). |
| D-292 | each Run starts on an empty output | locked | The console showed the lines of the run before when a program was changed and run again (a Fibonacci program changed from 10 terms to 5 printed the old series first, until the console was cleared). A run now **starts on an empty output**: `run:started` clears it, in the browser and through the bridge alike. What the console says about runs (run started, run ended, the connection) is a history and stays; the Clear button clears it as before (D-274). |
| D-293 | read text can go on the page where it was | locked | The owner: the text "is good but not exactly in the position the text from the original document has". Next to Paste as code block and Paste as text the preview offers **Paste where it was**: one text for each line, at the place it had on the picture (its row, its indent and its column), all in one group, in the middle of the current page. The lot is scaled to fit the width of the page (at most 85% of it), never so small that a typical line is under 24 tall, and a line that the face makes wider than the line it came from is made smaller until it is as wide, so lines do not run into their neighbours. What the teacher corrected in the preview is used, row for row, as long as no row was added or removed. |
| D-294 | PaddleOCR is not offered | locked | The owner: "i want to test indra ocr aswell remove paddle". The picker offers **indra** and **tesseract**, and the ladder is indra (it needs the bridge and its Python sidecar), then tesseract (the bridge, or the browser). Amends D-90, D-118 and D-137: the engine is no longer one of three and the slowest rung (27,822 ms) is gone. The extension's own setting and sidecar are untouched; the app never asks for paddle. Indra is a Python package, so it runs in the bridge's sidecar and cannot run in the browser by itself; tesseract is the one that works with no bridge. |
| D-295 | the console beside the editor leaves the editor in view | locked | The console docked to the right took **the whole panel** and the editor was out of sight: the Input box was a child of the panel's body, which is a row when the console is on the right, so a box as wide as its parent became a column as wide as the panel. The Input box now belongs to the console's own column in both docks. Docking also gives the console the default size of the new dock (a size dragged for the other side is not a size for this one), and beside the editor the console never takes more than 60% of the panel (under it, 80%), so the editor keeps its share. Amends D-274. |
| D-296 | the text of a PDF page can be selected | locked | The cursor changed over the words of a PDF page and nothing could be selected: the library puts `user-select: none` on `.excalidraw`, which the sidebar inherits, and the text layer of a page did not opt back in (the markdown viewer did). The text layer now says it can be selected and keeps the rules pdf.js ships for its layer (the layer is a stacking context, its text sits above the end-of-content marker, which covers the layer while a selection is dragged, so a drag across the gaps between words does not jump to the end of the page). A plain drag is left to the browser; the hand tool, which drags the page instead, turns selection off for the page as before. **Replaced by D-314**: a PDF is selected with a box, and the text layer is no longer selectable by the browser. |
| D-297 | a code editor opens in the middle of what you are looking at | locked | The owner: the window "is placed not in the center of what i am currently looking at and instead at some hard fixed location". A new code panel (the editor of its own, and the mirror of VS Code) is placed with its middle on the point of the scene that is under the middle of the screen - `screen / zoom - scroll`, with the part of the canvas that the sidebar covers left out. The place had been worked out as `(screen - scroll) / zoom`, which is the same only at a zoom of 100% or with no scroll, so a panel opened while zoomed out, or after the view had moved, landed far from the middle of the screen. |
| D-298 | a program that asks for input waits for the answer | locked | The owner: "input is broken, even before i can enter a value it breaks down". A program that calls `input()` with no line typed in advance is **stopped at the question**; the console shows what the program printed up to it and an **answer field**, and the answer (Enter) runs the program **again from the top with the answer added**. The lines printed before the question come out the same, and each answer is shown after its question as a terminal shows what is typed. The lines typed in the Input box in advance are read first (the newline that ends the box does not start another line); each Run starts the questions again; clearing the console ends the wait; an answer to a program that is not waiting any more is refused with a sentence. A program that goes on after it met the end of its input is stopped after a moment and asked all the same. A program whose output changes from run to run (random numbers, the time) prints other values on the run that goes on. Amends D-283: the Input box is the answers typed in advance, and it is optional. |
| D-299 | a table cell keeps its text inside it | locked | The owner: a table made from a markdown file "fails to account for the overflowing text in a single cell". A text longer than its column is wrapped to the column and the row grows to hold it. The columns are as wide as their widest cell while the table fits in 90% of the page's width; when it does not, the widest columns give way to a common width and the narrow ones keep theirs. A wrapped cell keeps its whole text and the width it was wrapped to, so editing it wraps it again inside the cell. Amends D-286: a column was never wider than 320, and a text wider than that ran out of its cell into the next. |
| D-300 | the engine that reads is chosen wherever a page is read | locked | The owner: the engine option "available everywhere wherever the option to read the page is provided". The picker of the panel's properties is also in the question about the area to read (a PDF page, a picture), in the PDF toolbar beside Read this page, and in the crop of a panel: each engine with the speed that was measured for it, and indra says that it needs the bridge when there is none. For pages and pictures it is one choice, kept while the page is open; the crop uses the engine chosen for its panel. The engine that was chosen goes first and the machine's own order is the rest (D-137). |
| D-301 | the text selected in a PDF can go on the page where it was | locked | The owner: "i want it to offer paste where it was for text selected using the normal left click mouse selection". The selection bar of a PDF has a fourth choice, **Where it was**: the selected text is put on the page one text for each line, each at the place it had on the page of the PDF, in one group in the middle of the current page, laid out like the text of a read (D-293). The lines are taken from the runs of the page's text that the selection covers (the part of the first and the last that is selected); the runs of one row are joined while they touch, and a wide gap - the column of a table - keeps them apart. A markdown document has no places, and no such choice. Since D-314 the text is the runs that the box of the selection covers, and the choice is an icon under the box. |
| D-302 | the PDF toolbar reads the page you are on | locked | The owner: "remove the hand button and put the read this page instead there". The Hand button is gone - a plain drag selects text, and the middle button still drags the page about - and the toolbar has **Read this page**, which reads the page that most of the viewer shows (the first when nothing says), with the picker of the engine beside it (D-300). Amends D-287. The page's own Read the text button is gone (D-307): there is one way to read a page. |
| D-303 | the YouTube link is a button beside the files | locked | The owner: "i still see the youtube link not added as a button alongside files". The Files tab has the buttons Open files, Open folder and **YouTube link**; the field for the link opens when the button is pressed, and closes with Escape or once the video has been added. Amends D-128's field, which was always in the way. |
| D-304 | a language nothing can run says so | locked | The owner: "i see javascript react, typescript and typescript react not sure if they work or not". In the language picker, a language that the browser does not run, that VS Code does not run on the teacher's machine and that is not a pack is shown as **colours only, cannot run**; the languages that run say where (here, or in VS Code). Pressing Run on a language that cannot run says that it cannot, and what runs where. |
| D-305 | the sentence of a read is one sentence, and it fits | locked | A reason that ends with a full stop is not followed by a second one ("start the Excalidraw Bridge extension in VS Code.."), and the sentence in the panel's properties wraps inside them instead of being cut off at their edge. |
| D-306 | a selection can be put away | locked | The owner: "no way to remove this window at all once triggered". The bar of the selected text has a close button and answers to Escape; closing it also lets go of the browser's selection, and it does not come back until text is selected again. Pressing one of the bar's own buttons never makes a new selection of the old one. |
| D-307 | the PDF keeps its place | locked | The owner: "there is a bug that resets the scroll from wherever it was back to the top of the file again" and "the extra read this page not removed". The pages are drawn when the file or the zoom changes, and for no other reason: a selection, the bar that shows it, or any other change round the viewer draws nothing again - the viewer stays where it was, and so does the selection. The **Read the text** button on each page is gone; **Read this page** in the toolbar (D-302) is the one way to read a page, and a page with no text says where. Amends D-279, D-287 and D-302. |
| D-308 | a link of a document goes where it points | locked | The owner: a link in a markdown document "is referring to the current location and in that directory there is another file with that name ... the markdown viewer should be capable of opening these automatically inside the viewer itself ... if it is an external website then a new tab with that website should open". A link to a file of the opened folder - relative to the document, from the root of the folder, or written with this app's own address - opens that file in the viewer: with or without its extension, a folder (its index, its README, or the file that is named as the folder), `./` and `../` in the path, a path written with %20; a link to a heading (`#heading`, or `file.md#heading`) scrolls to it. A link to another website (http, https) opens in a new tab, as before. A link to a file that was not opened says which one is missing, and never loads this app a second time in a tab. |
| D-309 | the text of a link can be selected | locked | The owner: "when there is a hyperlink in the table it does not get selected or highlighted so we cant paste it". A link in a document cannot be dragged: pressing on it and dragging selects text as it does everywhere else, so the bar of the selected text offers the text of a link as it offers the text round it. The first fix was not enough ("still cant select hyperlinks"): the text box had "Previous: · Index:" with the names of the links missing. The cause is the library, which puts `user-select: none` on **every link inside `.excalidraw`**, so the text of a link was neither highlighted nor part of the selected text. The links of a document opt back in to selecting. |
| D-310 | the open buttons and the PDF toolbar are icons | locked | The owner marked them with ticks: "need icons instead of whatever buttons are currently". Open files, Open folder and YouTube link, and Fit width, Fit page and Read this page, are icon buttons: each has its name as its tooltip and as its accessible name, and the picker of the engine stays beside Read this page. |
| D-311 | the full screen button and the console's bar fit beside each other | locked | The owner: "the full screen button needs to be moved here when they move the console to the side of editor, also + button is not visible for the console". With the console beside the editor the full screen button is at the editor's own top right corner, next to the console and not over it; and the console's bar wraps onto another line when it is too narrow for its buttons, so the + (and every other control) can always be reached. |
| D-312 | the open buttons are in the sidebar's header | locked | The owner, marking the picture: "the icons need to be placed here" - in the header row, with the tab button and the auto-fit switch, and not in a row of their own under it. Open files, Open folder and YouTube link are in the header; the field for a YouTube link still opens below, in the tab. Where there is no header to put them in (the Files tab on its own) they stay at the top of the tab. Amends D-303 and D-310. |
| D-313 | the PDF's tools are in the row of the zoom and never scroll away | locked | The owner: "the pdf icons vanish when scrolling (moving as i suggested can ensure that this does not happen and we can ensure the preview gets more area)". Fit width, Fit page, Read this page and the picker of the engine are in the row of the zoom, above the pages and outside the part that scrolls: they are always in view, and the pages have the room that a row of their own took. Where the viewer has no such row to put them in they stay at its top. Amends D-302 and D-310. |
| D-314 | a PDF is selected with a box | locked | The owner, with two pictures: "currently selecting text in pdf looks like this ... i want it like this instead" - a dashed box round the text in place of the highlight of each run. A drag on a page draws a box (dashed border, light fill, kept on its page) and the box is the selection: the runs of text that are for the most part inside it (a run is taken whole when half of it or more is inside), as the text of their rows - the far apart pieces of a row split by a tab, as the cells of a table are - and as the lines that keep the places they had (D-301), and the picture of that part of the page (D-315). The choices are a row of **icon buttons under the box**, on the pages and not in a row above them - code card, text box, table, where it was, image, close - so that nothing is moved and the pages keep their room. The box and its choices go when it is closed (the button, or Escape), when a new box is begun, with a click on the page, and when the zoom draws the pages again. The text is copied, as before. The browser's own highlight of a PDF's text is no more: the text layer cannot be selected. Replaces D-296; amends D-301, D-306 for a PDF. |
| D-315 | the part of a PDF page in the box goes on the canvas as a picture | locked | The owner: "this can also allow us to take screenshots images from pdf and paste them inside the canvas". **Image**, one of the choices under the box, puts the part of the page that is in the box on the current page as an **image**: cut from the page as it was drawn (at the sharpness of the drawing and not of the screen), in the middle of the page, selected, with the view moved to it, and the size an image of the sidebar has (D-104). It is the way to take a figure, a formula or a scan, which has no text; a box with no text has this choice and the close button only. |
| D-316 | the picture of a box is as sharp as the page can give | locked | The owner, with a blurred picture: "the resolution is based on the zoom level ... i want it to be the best resolution picture". **Image** no longer copies the pixels the viewer happened to draw (few of them at a low zoom). The part of the page in the box is **drawn again from the PDF itself**, at a scale of its own: six times the size of the page (432 dpi), or less when the part is so big that its long edge would pass 4096 pixels (a whole page comes out at about 3170 x 4096). The picture is the same at any zoom of the viewer, it lands on the page at the size an image has (D-315) with all those pixels behind it, so it stays sharp when the teacher zooms in on the canvas. When the page cannot be drawn again the pixels of the viewer are taken, as before. Amends D-315. |
| D-317 | the choices of a selected text in a document are the same icons | locked | The owner: "add the icons only from the pdf viewer, not the selection method, to the markdown viewer". Selecting text in a markdown (or source) document is still the browser's own selection - there is no box - but the choices are the same icon buttons: the code card, the text box, the table and the close button, in a row that floats under the end of the selection **inside the document** (it scrolls with the text and moves nothing). The block of three buttons with words above the document is gone. Amends D-306. |
| D-318 | the choices are always where they can be seen | locked | The owner: "the pdf viewer's selection tool does not trigger the options sometimes" - a box round a picture had none. The row of choices was put under the box in the coordinates of the column of pages, and a page that is wider than the viewer (zoomed in, and scrolled) or a box low in the view put it where nobody was looking. It is now put against the part of the pages that is **shown**: under the box when there is room in view, else over it, else at the bottom of the view - and never further to the left or to the right than the view. Amends D-314. |
| D-319 | a PDF keeps up at a high zoom | locked | The owner: "zooming in too much in the pdf viewer makes it a little laggy (the scroll bars update slowly, and panning becomes laggy)". Every page was drawn at its full size, all of them, one after the other, each as one canvas that at 400% is tens of millions of pixels, and the scroll bars grew a page at a time. Now every page has its **room at once** (so the scroll bars are right at once), only the pages **near the view** are drawn (a screen's height above it and below it) and the pages that go far from it give their pixels back; a canvas is never more than 16 million pixels (the browser stretches it beyond that); a drawing that is still going is stopped by a change of the zoom, and the pages near the view are drawn once the zoom has settled. A change of the zoom keeps the part of the document at the middle of the view where it was (it went back to the top). Amends D-287 and D-307. |
| D-320 | a part of a text can be bold and italic | locked | The owner, pointing at the properties of a text: "currently bold and italics are not present, please add those", and then, seeing the whole box change: "i cannot ensure that this is only applied to a part of the text box selection if i choose to do so". The properties of a text - and of a shape that has text in it - have **Bold** and **Italic**, two buttons in a row of their own under the font family, each with its name and its key as the tooltip. In the editor of a text they are for **what is selected there**: pressing one makes that part bold (or italic), or takes it off when all of it has it, and the rest of the box is left as it is; with nothing selected, and for a text that is only selected on the page, they are for the whole text. The buttons are lit when all of what they are for has the style, and they follow the selection as it moves. The keys are **Ctrl/Cmd+B** and **Ctrl/Cmd+I**, with a text selected or being typed (with none, the browser keeps them). The style is kept as runs over the text (`styleRuns`: characters `start` to `end` are bold, italic or both). It is drawn by **thickening and leaning the letters of the font the text already has** and not by another face of it, so no letter changes its width and nothing that measures, wraps or sizes a text - the box, the shape it is in, the editor - has to know of it; the stroke is 5% of the font size and the lean 0.25. The editor is a textarea, which has one style, so a text with styled parts is shown by a styled copy of it underneath, with the same box, font and wrapping, while the textarea keeps the caret, the selection and the typing and does not show its own letters. What is typed has the style of the letter before it, and what is deleted takes its own. The parts are in the canvas, in an exported image and in the SVG (a `tspan` of its own, thickened by a stroke of the text's colour and leaned by `font-style`), are saved with the drawing and read back from it (made to fit the text), are copied with the text, and can be undone. A text with no styled part has no such field. Not for a line that reads from right to left, which is drawn plain. |
| D-321 | a text made of what was selected keeps what was bold or italic | locked | The owner: "can the copied text that is inserted also carry bold, italic from the parent viewer (markdown and pdf) over when it is pasted ... if the source is bold ... if the source is italic ... if the source is mixed normal with italic/bold". A text box made of a selection has the bold and italic parts the selection had, and so has each line of Where it was. **In a markdown or source document** they are read from how the browser draws the selected text - the tags (`strong`, `b`, `em`, `i`, headings, the heads of a table) and the style the document gives (the comments of highlighted code are italic) - with each character of the selection found, in order, in the characters the selection covers, so the spaces the browser collapses and the breaks and tabs it puts in do not move the parts; a selected heading is a bold text box. **In a PDF** the pieces of a page's text are marked when the page is drawn with the style of the font they are set in (what pdf.js knows of it, and where that says nothing the name of the font: `Arial-BoldItalicMT`), and the box carries the marks over to the text, the lines and the text box; the space between two bold words is bold, a tab between cells is not. Where the pieces of the text layer do not line up with the text nothing is marked, rather than the wrong things. A code card and a table take the text, not its style. |
| D-322 | the mirrored window has **three modes**: whole, part and follow | locked | The owner asked for the VS Code window to be shown in three ways: the **part** that was chosen, the **whole** window, and a part that **follows the mouse**. They are one control with three choices, and the panel's own, saved with the document like the region (D-143): **Whole** is the default and shows the window as a screenshot does; **Part** shows the region the teacher drew on the whole window and keeps it until they draw another; **Follow** shows a region of the chosen size that moves with the pointer inside the panel (a follow with no region starts with one of 60% by 60% in the middle, and the teacher sets the size they want - from 20% to 90% of the window - with a slider beside the mode, which keeps the area's shape and its middle). It replaces the choose button and the follow switch, it is changed as often as the teacher likes, and a change never restarts the capture or changes how the window is found (D-112, D-113). **Part** opens the picker, and the picker is drawn over the page: a panel that is full screen is all the browser shows, so it leaves full screen first and the picker is there to be seen. The rule: **three ways to look, one control.** |
| D-323 | the canvas can **control** the mirrored window, and the teacher chooses when | locked | The owner asked for the keyboard and the mouse to go from the canvas into the mirrored VS Code window. A **Control** switch on the panel does it: it is **off** in every new session and in every panel until the teacher turns it on, and it is **lit** while it is on. While it is on, a click (left, middle or right), a double click, a drag, the wheel, the typed keys and the shortcuts made over the mirrored view go into the editor's window, and only then. The keys go only when the view has the keyboard - after the teacher clicked it - so the canvas's own shortcuts are not taken while the teacher is looking at the canvas; and a chord (Ctrl+Alt+Home) gives the keyboard back to the canvas at any moment. With Control off the panel works as it did (a follow moves the region, the wheel moves the canvas). A double click is two presses, and the extension makes it the way Windows does. The rule: **the canvas touches the editor only when the teacher said so, and they can always take it back.** |
| D-324 | a point of the view is a point of the **window** | locked | The panel shows a part of the editor's window (D-322), so a point of the view is not a point of the window until it is mapped. The view's own box is read when the pointer is over it, the point is turned into a fraction of that box, and the fraction goes through the region that is shown (D-143) to become a fraction of the **whole** window: that is what travels, so the same place is the same place whatever size the window is, whatever the zoom of the canvas and whatever the DPI of the screen. The extension turns the fraction into the window's own pixels only when it delivers it. The whole window is fitted into the view without being stretched, so the picture can have bars beside it: a press or a wheel in a bar is not a place in the window and is not sent, and a move or a release outside the view is held on its edge, so a drag that leaves it still ends at it. While a mouse button is down a follow stands still, so a drag does not run away from the pointer; and the picture has no mouse cursor, so the panel draws none - the teacher's own pointer is on it. The rule: **what the teacher points at is what the editor receives.** |
| D-325 | the input reaches the editor's window **without taking the focus** | locked | Measured on this machine on 2026-10-29, on a real VS Code window that was not in front and then parked far off every display: messages posted to the window's own page (its Chromium render widget) are enough for the mouse (move, press, release, double click, drag, the wheel) and the keyboard (typed characters, Enter, Backspace, arrows), and the text, the selection and the save came out right. Two things needed care: a **modifier** is read by Chromium from the thread's key state and not from the message, so a shortcut (Ctrl+S) did nothing from posts alone and worked once the key state was shared with the window's thread for the length of the press and then put back; and Enter, Tab and the like are **keys**, not characters (a carriage return sent as a character is ignored). So the extension sends a printed character as a character, everything else as a key, a modifier by sharing the key state, and the wheel with its place on the screen; and two things only a real run could show: a key that types a character goes as the **character alone** (the window's own message loop turns a posted key into a character as well, so key and character together typed it twice), and Enter, Tab and Backspace get a short settle (the window makes their character at the end of its queue, so the text posted right behind them was typed first). Measured on 2026-10-29 with the extension's own modules driving a throwaway editor: typing, Ctrl+S, a committed text with a line feed, a double click, Shift+End and the wheel all landed in an unfocused window, and again after it was parked and rested for thirty seconds; and the focus route typed and saved into the same editor. The teacher's own focus, keyboard and mouse are never touched. The rule: **the teacher keeps typing wherever they are.** |
| D-326 | where that does not work, the input goes **with the focus** and says so | locked | The graceful fall-back. Some windows are not Chromium (no render widget to post to), and a teacher may want the plain way: then the input is **real input**. For the length of a burst of events the window is brought to the front, the events go in with `SendInput` (the mouse by its place on the screen, the keys by their scan codes, a printed character as itself), and when the burst has been quiet for a moment the window that was in front is put back and the pointer returns to where the teacher left it. It needs the window **on a display** - a parked window is brought back first (D-328) - and it does take the teacher's keyboard while a burst lasts, which is why it is the second choice. The route is a setting (`excalidraw-bridge.inputRoute`: `auto`, `background`, `focus`); `auto` is background where the window has a page to post to and focus where it has not. The panel says which route is in use and why, in the capture's descriptor. The rule: **the input always has a way in, and the teacher is told which.** |
| D-327 | the control has **limits**, and a refused input is said | locked | The bridge already refuses a connection without the session token (D-50), and the control adds to that: it accepts input only **after the panel turned Control on**, and it is **off again** when the connection ends; the events are a **closed list** (a pointer event, a wheel event, a key event, a text event) and any other is refused; every number is checked (a place is a fraction between 0 and 1, a wheel step is bounded, a text is at most 256 characters and has no control characters), and a key is a name from a table and never a raw code; the number of events a second is bounded, and what is dropped is **counted and said** to the panel; the events go only to the window found **by rule** (D-112, D-113), and never to another one. And what the machine itself keeps is never sent: the Windows key, Alt+Tab, Alt+Esc, Ctrl+Esc, Ctrl+Alt+Delete, and Alt+F4 that would close the teacher's editor. The rule: **the control can do what a keyboard and a mouse can do in that one window, and nothing else.** |
| D-328 | the editor's window can be **parked**, and it keeps working there | locked | The owner asked whether the virtual display driver is needed at all. Measured on this machine on 2026-10-29, it is not: a VS Code window moved to -30000,-30000, far off every display, kept drawing its frames to `PrintWindow` (a line typed in it showed in the next capture, with its caret) and took every kind of input (D-325) and saved the file. So **parking** is the way to get the editor out of the teacher's sight: one control on the panel, a command and a button on the input panel, **Park** and **Bring back**. It is the teacher's choice and never the default (D-148); the window is found by rule and moved without taking the focus (D-147); the move is measured and a maximised window is un-maximised first (D-150); and the way the window was - its place and size, and whether it was maximised - is kept as **facts** and not as a handle, so it comes back the way the teacher had it (D-149), and when nothing was kept (the extension was restarted while it was parked) it comes back on the primary display. A window that is parked when the extension ends is brought back. The rule: **the teacher's window is theirs: it can go out of sight and it always comes back.** |
| D-329 | the **virtual display driver is not used**, and what served it is removed | locked | Parking (D-328) does what the driver was installed for - a window that is out of the teacher's way and still seen and driven from the canvas - without a driver, a reboot, an administrator's rights or a second screen to set to Extend. So nothing in the project uses or needs the driver: the extension's display-finding code and helper, the placement on a virtual display, the reasons that named the driver's control app, the commands, the installer's step that unpacked and installed it, its check in the install report and the sentences of the guides are removed, and the `.vsix` ships the new helpers. This supersedes D-146 and changes D-145 and D-147 to D-150 to say **parking** where they say a virtual display: their rules stand. The presenter's window is no longer placed by the extension; it opens where the browser opens it, and the teacher shares that window. The rule: **nothing is installed for what a window move can do.** |
| D-330 | the live view shows the **whole** window, at any display scaling | locked | The teacher, with a picture of the live view next to a picture of their window: _"the whole window is not the whole window"_ - the panel showed the top-left two thirds of the editor, with no right side and no status bar. Measured on their machine: the screen is at 150%, and the capture helper was not told it is scaling aware, so Windows handed it **virtualised** sizes - the window's client area of 1920 by 1008 read as 1280 by 672 - and `PrintWindow` into a bitmap of that size draws the top-left two thirds of the window and nothing else. It never showed on a screen at 100%, which is why the capture's own tests did not see it. The rule: **every helper of the extension is DPI aware (per-monitor v2) before it reads a window**, so the size it reads is the window's real one and a place in the picture is a place in the window - the capture, the window helper and the input helper all read the same pixels. |
| D-331 | the VS Code mirror is a panel of its own and it is **always the live window** | locked | The teacher, seeing the old panel after a reload (the toggled "Live window" that says the window has not arrived): _"i think u can just redo the window completely since this toggleable version is depreciated now due to bifurgating the two options in the hamburger menu"_. The menu has the two panels - the code editor in the browser and the VS Code mirror - so the mirror no longer has an editor view to switch to, no run bar and no output console: it is the editor's window and moves between a few states (D-333): a wheel with a sentence under it while it connects or waits, an error icon with a sentence under it when it is refused or cannot draw, and the live window. A code panel that is not a code editor of its own opens as the mirror, the ones saved with the old editor view too (D-99 is amended: the live window is the only view). The code editor of its own keeps the editor, its run bar and its console. |
| D-332 | the mirror's tools are **in the mirror**, and the properties keep the screenshot and the read as a backup | locked | The teacher: _"remove them add them inside the panel itself maybe keep the ocr and screenshot in the properties panel as well as a backup"_. The mirror's own header carries the modes, the follow size, Control, Park, the screenshot, the read (with its engine choice and its way to stop), and the full screen; the field for the token is the body of the state that needs it (no token, a token that is not accepted, an extension that does not answer). The properties panel keeps the screenshot and the read and has **no** bridge field any more. The presenter's mirror (read only) has no header and no field. |
| D-333 | the page **reconnects by itself**, and a refused token is said | locked | The teacher, after reloading the editor: _"auto reconnect is not working"_. The cause was two things. The token was **new every time the extension started**, so the page kept offering a token that was gone; and the page's client answered a refused token the way it answers an extension that is not running - it kept retrying the same dead token for ever and said _"is the extension running in VS Code?"_, which was not the truth. So: the token the extension makes is **kept in the editor's own secret storage** (the system's keychain behind it) and the next start uses it again, so a reload of the editor or of the page reconnects by itself (a token the teacher sets in the settings is theirs and is never kept; a kept value that is not a token of ours is not trusted); and the extension's answer to a wrong token is a close with its own code, which the page reads as **refused** - a different fact from "no extension" - says so in a sentence that names the command that copies the new token, and stops asking with the old one until a new token is pasted. The rule: **the page never retries a token the extension has refused, and never says the extension is missing when it answered.** |
| D-334 | the part picker is drawn **into the full-screen panel** | locked | The teacher: part works "even if iam full screen", and sometimes needed choosing again - the first way (leave full screen, then open the picker) waited for the browser and lagged. The browser draws only the element that is full screen, so the picker is drawn **into that element** (a portal) and fitted to the screen it fills; the teacher never leaves full screen to choose a part, and nothing waits. |
| D-335 | the **input route** is chosen from the editor's own notification | locked | The teacher: _"include this mode switching as a drop down or a radio option in the notification through which i am copying the token id from the vscode extension so excalidraw-bridge.inputRoute is flipping to focus (so it needs to be easy to use to test)"_. The notification that shows the token (the status bar's entry) has a second button, **Input route...**, which opens a **pick list** of the three routes - automatic, background, focus - with the current one ticked and each one said in a sentence; the choice is written to the same setting (`excalidraw-bridge.inputRoute`, for the user) and applies **at once**: a helper that is running is made again with the new route, and the notification says which route is in use. The same list is a command (`Excalidraw Bridge: Choose how the canvas types into the editor`). The rule: **the way in is one click from the token, and a change of it never needs a restart.** |
| D-336 | the focus route **brings a parked window back**, and says so | locked | The teacher asked whether the fall-back can work without the editor's window opening, and believed the focus route needs the virtual display driver. It cannot work without the window: real input goes to the window **in front**, so the window has to be on a display and shown for the moment it types. A parked window is on no display, so the helper asks (`NEED display`) and the extension **brings the window back** - once for each change, never in a loop - and tells the helper to look again, and the reason the panel shows says it was done. This needs no driver: the driver would only have been a second place to hide the window while it takes the keyboard all the same. The background route is the one that works with the window out of sight, which is why it is the default. The driver stays out (D-329) until the teacher has tried the focus route and says otherwise. |
| D-337 | the following area **rests** while the pointer is near the middle, and **Control takes the keyboard at once** | locked | The teacher: _"follow with the slider is too responsive at smaller window lengths"_ and _"it connects but i need to press the editor once"_. The area used to put its middle under the pointer, so in a small panel a small move of the hand swept it across the window. Now the middle 70% of the window is a **rest zone**: the area stays where it is while the pointer is in it, and outside it the area moves towards the pointer by a step - slower at the edge of the zone, at most a fifth of its own size at the edge of the window - every 120 ms for as long as the pointer stays out. The speed is the pointer's distance from the zone and not its place, so the same move of the hand does the same in a small panel and in a large one. And when the extension says Control is on, the window takes the keyboard at once (the dashed outline becomes the solid one), so there is no first click. **AMENDED (wave 9a, D-339 round):** the rest zone is the middle **70%** of the window (the teacher: _"increase the rest area"_); verified in a browser against the real extension - the area does not move while the pointer wanders inside it, it steps towards a pointer parked near the edge and stops at the window's edge, and Control takes the keyboard with no click. |
| D-338 | the live view is **sharper, quicker and lighter** | locked | The teacher: _"the resolution seems too low, delay is also high"_, the presenter lagging, and _"the whole computer becomes a little unresponsive"_. Measured on this machine against a throwaway VS Code (2182x1361): the old setting (6 a second, quality 60, scale 0.75) made 4 frames a second and took 19% of a core in the helper, most of it the resampling; at the window's own size the picture is saved as it was taken and the helper takes 9% of a core for 10 frames a second. So the picture is now the window's **own size** at quality 70, the helper asks for 12 frames a second and **waits only what is left of a frame's time** (it waited a whole frame's time on top of the work), and it runs **below normal priority** so the machine's own programs come first. The stream no longer queues frames for a client that is behind: past about two frames the client is skipped and gets the **newest** frame when its socket drains, so a slow window (the presenter, a busy tab) has a delay that does not grow. A window that does not change still costs no traffic (D-88). |
| D-339 | the screenshot has **one crop window**, for every panel, with the choices in a bar on top | locked | The teacher: the camera showed the crop box and the picture but _"i dont see the buttons at all"_ (they were drawn over the canvas, which a full-screen panel hides), and asked for the crop window of the flow - _"insert into this page, new page, ocr for the whole page or the cropped page"_ - and, once insert is chosen, _"the rectangle ... should follow the mouse ... the size ... what the screenshot's picture's dimensions will look like on the canvas"_, with a left click to place it; and the same window for the screenshot from the video. The screenshot's focus view is now that window: opaque, over the app's whole chrome (a plane of its own, above the main menu), drawn into the full-screen panel when there is one, with a bar across the top that holds **Insert into this page**, **New page**, **Read the text of the whole picture** (of the **crop** once there is one), the revert and Cancel, in large buttons; the picture is below it with a white crop and white handles, like the flow's. The strip that hung off the panel's edge and the dimming of the panel and of the canvas are gone. Choosing leaves full screen (the rectangle is on the canvas), then the host gets the choice: Insert and Read go to the rectangle, **New page asks for a new page at once** - there is no ribbon (their answer). The rectangle is a **hover effect**: it stands with its middle under the pointer wherever the pointer is - no button down, no drag - at the size the cropped picture will have on the canvas at its zoom, the pointer goes through it, and a left click anywhere places the picture where it stands; Esc and Cancel leave. The properties panel's camera and the video's open the same window. Cancel in a crop window - this one and Part's - changes nothing (Part's Cancel used to make the whole window the mirror). |
| D-340 | the **focus route** keeps out of the teacher's way | locked | The teacher: the other route _"is very slow compared to the other automatic one"_. On this route a hover over the picture moved the teacher's own pointer into the editor at every move, and the window was given back after a pause of 0.6 s, so every word cost a new trip. A move with no button down is now dropped on this route (it needs neither the window in front nor the pointer), a press makes the moves that follow it a drag, and the window stays in front through a pause of 1.5 s before the pointer and the focus are given back. Verified on the real editor: typing and Ctrl+S, the parked window brought back and typed into (9 of 9). The route is still slower than the automatic one by nature - real input goes to the window in front - which is why the automatic route stays the default. |
| D-341 | a mirror that appears **after** the editor's window was announced is given it | locked | Found by driving the real app in a browser against the real extension, after the teacher's _"after hard refresh in focus mode the mirror does not come back at all"_: after a reload the page reconnected at once, the document was restored a moment later, and the announcement of the editor's window had already been given to a scene with no mirror in it - and it does not change again, so the mirror said "Waiting for the editor's window" for good, on both routes. The announcement is now given to the mirrors that have not been given it whenever the scene changes (the way the connection's truth already was, D-133), and a mirror inserted later is given it too. |
| D-342 | the focus route can take the window to the **front** while another program has the foreground | locked | The teacher: _"the focus seems to be broken"_. Found in a browser run against the real extension: the route's reason said _"the editor's window would not come to the front"_ and nothing typed - Windows lets only the program that has the foreground, or one that has just had input, give it to another window, and the helper is the editor's child while the teacher works in the browser. The helper now asks in turn: the threads attached to each other and the window raised; a key that does nothing (F24) tapped, which makes it the program that has just had input; and the shell's own switch to the window as the last resort. Verified on the real editor: a click and typing through Control on this route landed in the window while a browser had the foreground. |
| D-343 | a denser window is **sharper**, and what it costs was measured, and not built | locked | The teacher asked whether a 4K or 8K virtual display, mirrored, would improve the quality and whether to test it. Measured on this machine against throwaway editors parked off screen (12 frames a second asked, quality 70): a window of the editor's own size 1920x1080 - 10 frames a second, 74 KB a frame, about 2% of a core; 3840x2160 - 10 frames a second, 182 KB, 34% of a core; 7680x4320 - 3 frames a second, 585 KB, 55% of a core, so 8K is out of reach for this helper. A bigger window alone does not sharpen: it shows MORE of the editor in the same panel, smaller. What sharpens is more pixels for the same layout (a higher device pixel ratio, as a 4K display at 200% or 300% gives): the same 22% of the window shown 480 pixels wide is soft at 1.5 (this screen), clearly sharper at 2 (2560x1440 frames, 111 KB, 7% of a core) and sharpest at 3 (3840x2160 frames, 216 KB, 40% of a core). So it improves the picture where a part is shown large (Part and Follow, the presenter on a big screen) and costs about 1.5x the bytes at 2 and 3x at 3. It needs no driver to be measured, but the editor shows itself at that density only on a display that has it - so it would need the virtual display, which the teacher kept out (D-329) until they have tried the focus route. Not built; the numbers are here for the decision. |
| D-344 | a presenter whose canvas was **emptied by its own start-up** gets the scene back | locked | The teacher: the presenter _"seems to not show what is on the editor window"_, and asked to check it before touching the code. Checked by running the real app in a browser against the real extension: the presenter window opened, the editor's scene arrived on its hello, and then the presenter's own start-up emptied the canvas - and the scene is sent once, on hello, while the camera messages that kept coming kept resetting the heartbeat, so hello was never sent again. The presenter now looks again after 300 ms, 1.2 s and 3 s (`SCENE_CHECK_MS`): when it is still the latest scene and the canvas has fewer elements than the scene has, the scene is put back without being recorded for undo. Checked in the browser: the canvas went from 0 to 1 element and the picture of the editor painted. No second display was needed. |
| D-345 | a **window that holds the keyboard** is not answered by the editor's shortcuts | locked | The teacher: _"i cant use ctrl+/ when in control mode"_ - commenting several lines of the editor through the mirror. Found in the browser: the command palette listens on the window in the capture phase for Ctrl+/ and Ctrl+Shift+P, and it called `preventDefault` and `stopPropagation` before the mirror's surface ever saw the key. The live window now carries `data-keyboard-taken` while Control is on and the window holds the keyboard, and the command palette and the search menu (Ctrl+F, Esc) leave a key alone when it comes from inside such an element (`isKeyboardTaken`). With that, Ctrl+/ comments the lines in the editor. |
| D-346 | a press on the PDF's toolbar is **not the start of a box** on a page | locked | The teacher: in the PDF viewer _"ocr only works on the first page"_, and not on page 2 or between pages. Found in the browser: the toolbar (Read this page) is drawn in a portal above the scroll, so a press on it found a page that was scrolled up under it (`sheetAt`), took it for the start of a box on that page, captured the pointer and swallowed the click. The viewer's press handler now leaves a press that does not come from inside the viewer to its target. Checked at several scroll positions: Read this page reads the page in view. |
| D-347 | reading the **mirror** asks which part, and the picture is the editor's window | locked | The teacher: _"pressing the ocr icon in vscode mirror window does not do anything"_ - it should open the "What should be read?" window. For a code panel that is not standalone and has a live capture, the read now fetches the extension's snapshot of the editor's window as its picture (a toast says so when there is none) and, when the panel has no remembered crop, opens the same area window the PDF and the video open, with that picture, before reading; the whole page and a part are both there. A panel that remembers a crop is read in it without asking, as before. |
| D-348 | a **picture on the canvas** is cropped in the crop window, from a double click, the crop button or Enter | locked | The teacher: _"double clicking any image/gif should trigger [the crop window] so i can crop it"_, and, after a cropped screenshot was placed, the double click opened the in-canvas crop in a layer underneath the canvas that could be left with Esc. The in-canvas crop of an image is replaced by the full-screen crop window the screenshots and the media use (`media-crop` styles): a bar with Reset, Cancel and Apply, the whole picture with a box on it, a dimmed outside, rule of thirds, eight handles, Esc to leave. A double click on an image that is not a media element opens it (a hit test with the rotation taken into account), and so do the crop button and Enter (`croppingElementId` is cleared at once). Apply puts the crop on the element keeping the scale it had on each axis, the part that is kept in place, a turned picture cropped about its middle and the whole picture as no crop at all (`imageCropUpdates`); undo takes it back. Checked in the browser: 1485x967 of 2160x1350, Reset gives no crop. |
| D-349 | the crop window of a screenshot offers **the pages**, and a new page goes after the current one | locked | The teacher: _"insert into new page should add a new page (at page 3 of 5 it inserts a page after 3, as 4, and the total grows to 6), and it should show a drop down of the pages so I select where to insert it"_. The bar of the crop window (D-339) has a drop-down of the pages when there is more than one (the current one marked "this page"); Insert becomes "Insert into <page>" when another is chosen and reaches the host with the page's id; New page asks the host for a page after the current one (the host already inserts it right after the current page). Checked in the browser: insert into page 3 put the picture on page 3; New page from page 1 made a page 2 and the total went from 3 to 4. |
| D-350 | the focus route puts the editor's window on a **virtual display**, when the machine has one | locked | The teacher: on the focus route the editor's window _"literally opens ... and goes back and forth, not usable like that (needs the virtual display method)"_. The window helper has a new `-Mode Displays` that lists the displays of the desktop (`EnumDisplayMonitors`, `GetMonitorInfo`, `EnumDisplayDevices`) in real pixels and says which are virtual (the adapter names itself virtual, indirect or IDD). When the route is the focus route and Control is on - and again when the window is needed in front - the extension moves the window to fill the virtual display without taking the focus (`moveToVirtualDisplay`, once for each Control session), the facts of where it was are kept as for a parked window (D-149) and the window is brought back when Control ends, the bridge goes away or the extension ends. With no virtual display nothing moves and the panel's reason says that one would keep the jump out of the way. The extension still names no driver and installs none (D-329): it uses a display that is there. Measured on this machine (a display at 1920,0): the window moved from 2182x1361 to 1920x1080 on the virtual display, typing landed, and Control off put it back. Only the picture's jump is gone: the keyboard focus still passes through the editor for a moment, so the route stays slower than the automatic one. |
| D-351 | the teacher has a **button** that puts the editor's window on the virtual display, and one that brings it back to the main display | locked | The teacher: _"there is no button to shift the window to the virtual display and bring the window back to the main display"_ (D-350 moved it only by itself, for the focus route). The mirror's header has a button with a monitor icon, shown when the machine has a virtual display or the window is on it: **Virtual** puts the window there and **Main** brings it back to the display it came from. A new action `virtual` is in the closed list of the window's actions (`park`, `bring-back`, `virtual`), and the descriptor carries `virtual` and `virtualAvailable`, read from the window and the display list (`virtualState`). A window the teacher put there stays there when Control ends; a window the teacher brought back to the main display is not taken to the virtual one again by the focus route until Control ends. The same two choices are VS Code commands (_Move the editor's window to the virtual screen_, and the existing _Bring the editor's window back_). Checked in the browser: Virtual moved the window from -131,-135 2182x1361 to 1920,0 1920x1080 and Main put it back. |
| D-352 | the YouTube panel's **camera** and **read** work: the picture is the tab's picture of the player, and the camera has its own icon | locked | The teacher: the YouTube panel's _screenshot and read the text_ did nothing, and asked to _change the icon and make them functional_. A YouTube player is an iframe of a foreign document, which no page may draw into a canvas, so the host had no picture and said nothing. The picture is now the **tab's own picture of the player**: the page asks the browser to share this tab (`getDisplayMedia`, the current tab preferred), the teacher presses Allow, and the player's rectangle is cut out of a frame (`regionInFrame`, the scale measured from the frame and the viewport, the part outside the viewport cut off). The stream is kept for 20 seconds so a second picture does not ask again, and then stopped, because the browser says "sharing this tab" while it runs. A browser that cannot, and a teacher who did not allow it (or chose a window or a screen, whose geometry is not the tab's), are told in a sentence. The camera in the properties takes the picture into the crop window (Insert, New page, Read the text, with the page drop-down), and "Read the text" asks which part, in the window every read opens (D-347). The properties' camera has a new icon - a frame round a point, the picture of a part of the screen - in place of the photo camera. A real browser took the picture of the iframe's rectangle at the right size; the player's own content could not be shown in that browser, so the picture there was its blank page. |
| D-353 | the **indra** engine installs itself, in an environment of its own, when the teacher says so | locked | The teacher saw _"indra-ocr is not installed - pip install indra-ocr"_ and asked how to have it packaged with the extension. It cannot ride in the `.vsix`: it is Python, tied to the interpreter's version and the machine, and its set (an inference runtime, an image library, a PDF library) installs to about 270 MB. What the extension does is make the install **one yes**: a read with indra missing no longer raises an error (the read was taken by the next engine) but offers once - _Install indra now? one download of about 270 MB_ - and on _Install indra_ it makes a virtual environment in the editor's own storage for the extension, installs `indra-ocr` there, restarts the sidecar in it and publishes the engine as ready; the teacher's own Python is not touched. The same install is the command _Install the indra OCR engine_. When wheels ship in `wheels/` beside the extension they are used with `--no-index`, so the install needs no internet; the engine's model downloads the first time it reads. Checked on this machine: a fresh environment installed the package and the sidecar reported indra available. |
| D-354 | the mirror's picture is **sharpened where it is drawn**, and it costs nothing in the capture or the stream | locked | The teacher asked whether the mirror's stream can be sharpened, and whether that costs computation. The window is captured at its own size and drawn at a third of it or less, so the browser has to shrink it, and a shrunk picture is soft - text first. The live picture is drawn through a 3x3 sharpening kernel (an SVG `feConvolveMatrix`, weights `0 -0.4 0 / -0.4 2.6 -0.4 / 0 -0.4 0`, which add up to one, so brightness is kept). It works on the pixels that are drawn, not on the ones that are streamed: the capture helper, the stream and its bytes are unchanged. Measured against the extension's own snapshot at the panel's scale: edge energy 0.91 plain, 2.16 with this kernel (2.7 with a stronger one, not used), mean brightness 23.3 and 23.4. Measured CPU of the browser's processes with frames flowing: the same within noise with the filter and without (about 2 to 5 percent of one core either way). |
| D-355 | the capture follows the window's **size**, and on the focus route the **mouse takes nothing** | locked | The teacher: on the focus route _"mouse clicks are horrible"_, and with the screencast on they saw the editor's text get smaller on the virtual display. Found in a real browser against the real extension: the capture helper measured the window's client size when it found the window and never again, so a window that changed size - dragged, maximised, or moved to a display with another scale, as the virtual display is - was drawn into a bitmap that did not fit it (the editor in a corner of the old picture, stale margins), while the pointer's place is a fraction of the live client area and landed somewhere else. The size is now read on every frame and a size that changed sends the helper back to measure the window and tell the reader the new size. The picture followed the window: 1904x1072 for the 1920x1080 window on the virtual display. And on the focus route the **keyboard** stays real input (it goes to the window in front) but the **pointer and the wheel are posted to the window's page**, as on the other route: a real click had to take the teacher's own pointer to the editor's window and bring it back, and bring the window forward for each one. Checked: a click at line 3 put the typed character on line 3, and the real pointer did not move (860,640 before and after). A window with no page to post to keeps the real pointer. |
| D-356 | a window on the **virtual display** cannot be lost | locked | The teacher: after sending the window to the virtual screen, _"bringing it back to main seems to fail ... every launch of VS Code launches it in the virtual display, and I can't access it"_, and asked what Park and Bring back are. The two are not the same thing: **Park** moves the window far off every display (it keeps working and keeps being mirrored, and nothing shows on the teacher's screens), **Virtual** puts it on the virtual display (a real display that nobody looks at, with the editor drawn at the density of that display), and **Bring back / Main** returns it to where it was. The way back needed the facts of where the window had been, and kept them in memory only: a window on the virtual display when the extension restarted - Reload Window, an update - had no facts, was found to be "already on a display", and stayed there; and the editor opens where it was closed, so the next launch opened on the virtual display too. Now a window on the virtual display with no facts kept comes back to the primary display (centred, no bigger than the display), and when the extension starts and finds the editor's window on the virtual display it brings it to the main display itself. Checked in the browser: a window put on the virtual display behind the extension's back came back to 460,204 1500x900 on the main display with the **Main** button. |
| D-357 | the YouTube panel shows **no caption note** when there are no captions | locked | The teacher asked for _"the no captions: the YouTube API key is not set error message"_ on the videos to be removed. A video that has no captions is a video: the line over its top edge that said why is gone for that case. The note that says where captions came from (the API, the public endpoint) stays when there are captions. |
| D-358 | a **picture's crop window** can read its text | locked | The teacher: _"regular images on double click should also offer OCR capabilities in the crop window"_. The window has a **Read the text** button beside Cancel and Apply: it reads the part the box is on - in the picture's own pixels, whole numbers - with the engine the teacher chose, puts nothing on the canvas by itself, and leaves the window. The same read every picture on the canvas has, without the question of which part, because the box says it. |
| D-359 | **Reset** in the picture's crop window takes the crop off at once | locked | The teacher: _"the crop revert does not work ... it should just reset the crop"_. Reset used to move the box to the whole picture and wait for Apply, so nothing seemed to happen on the canvas. It now puts the whole picture back on the element - the crop removed, the size the picture had at its scale - and leaves the window, and undo takes it back. Enter applies the box as it stands, as in the crop window of a poster. |
| D-360 | a **rescue file** brings the editor's window back when nothing else can reach it | locked | The teacher: _"how can I do that if I can't see VS Code ... before even connecting it?"_ The whiteboard's Main button and the extension's own start-up rescue (D-356) both need the editor to be running with the extension, or the whiteboard connected; a window that is parked off every display or on the virtual display, with neither, cannot be reached. `packages/vscode-bridge/scripts/rescue-editor-window.cmd` (it runs `rescue-editor-window.ps1`) is double-clicked: it looks at every visible window of the editor's program (`Code`, or `-ProcessName VSCodium`), and a window whose middle is on no REAL display - a virtual display is one whose adapter names itself virtual - and that is not minimised is placed on the main display's work area, centred, no bigger than 90 percent of it. A window on a real display is left alone, `-List` says what would move and moves nothing, and nothing of the machine is changed. Checked on a throwaway editor: parked at -30000,-30000 and put on the virtual display, it was brought to the main display both times; the teacher's own window, on a real display, was not touched. |
| D-361 | the mirror's header stays on **one row** | locked | The teacher, with a picture of it: the header's buttons spilled onto a second row, which took a third of the panel, and _"the icons can be smooshed together to only be in one row"_. The row is measured: it has wrapped when the middles of its children are not on one line (`useCompactWhenWrapped`, a `ResizeObserver` on the header). A header that wrapped is a header with no room for its words, so the buttons that have an icon - Control, Park, Virtual or Main, Read the text - keep the icon, their words stay in their tooltips and labels, the route's note ("no focus taken") is dropped, and the file name may shrink. The Whole, Part and Follow buttons keep their words: they have no icon. The width at which the row wrapped is kept, and the words come back when the header is clearly wider than that (by 120 pixels or a fifth). |
| D-362 | the YouTube camera and read take the picture with the player **full screen** | locked | The teacher asked whether the picture's resolution could be higher, proposing a hidden window with the video full screen and the ads skipped. That is not realistic: a window that is full screen is not hidden, and an advertisement in a player of another origin cannot be skipped by code. What can be done is the same thing in the page the teacher is already in. The camera (and the read) asks the player's iframe to go **full screen first** - in the click that asked, because the browser lets only a click do it - then shares the tab (a stream that is there is used, so nothing is asked while the player is full screen), leaves the player alone for 2.5 seconds so that its controls go away, cuts the picture from the frame in the player's own shape (`fitAspect`, 16:9 on any screen, the bars left out) and leaves full screen. A browser that will not go full screen gets the picture of the player where it is, as before; a refusal to share leaves full screen first. Measured in a real browser: the picture of the same panel was 337x189 and is 1620x910 in that window - the size of the window, so on a 1920x1080 screen it is 1920x1080 and more on a screen of a higher density. |
| D-363 | a **frame rate above thirty** is used, up to sixty | locked | The teacher raised `excalidraw-bridge.captureFps` to 48 to cut the mirror's delay, and felt no change - because the extension accepted 1 to 30 and turned anything else into the default of 12, silently. A number of one or more is now used, at most 60 (a larger one is 60, and the setting says its range, 1 to 60); a number that is not a number, or is less than one, is still the default. The rate that is really reached is how fast the window can be drawn: the helper takes the next frame when the last is done, so a big window may not reach sixty. Found by watching the teacher's recording: the picture changed about fifteen times a second, which is twelve frames and the cursor's blink. |
| D-370 | the mirror **says where its time goes**, and names who was slow | locked | The teacher: _"when I press something here I don't see it on the VS Code screen"_, and a recording in which the terminal's picture did not change for about ten seconds while digits were typed, then caught up. The recording could not say **which link** of the chain was slow: the editor's own window (the capture waits for it to draw itself), the extension host (the one thread that all of the editor's extensions share - and the teacher has 34 - and the mirror's stream and input go through), or the capture helper. So the capture helper now says, once a second, how many frames it took, how long the window took to draw itself (`PrintWindow` waits for the window, so a busy editor shows there) at its average and at its worst, and the longest pass of its loop; the extension measures how far behind its timer the host is (a 100 ms timer that does nothing); and the command _Show where the mirror's time goes_ writes the last minute to the Output panel - the frame rate, the draw time, the host's lag, each stalled second (a pass of 0.8 seconds or more) with **who was slow in it**, and one sentence for the teacher. A second in which the window took nearly the whole pass is the editor's; one in which the host's timer was late is the host's; one in which neither was is the helper's. |
| D-371 | the mirror **times each key** and says whether the editor was in front | locked | The teacher's recordings: a digit typed in the mirrored terminal showed seconds late, the same digit was already there when they switched to the real window with Alt-Tab, and after restarting the editor the first test was fast and the later ones slow - while the capture's own numbers (D-370) were smooth. The recordings do not say when each key was pressed, so the delay could not be measured. The extension already publishes a picture **only when it differs** from the one before, so the time from a key being sent to the next published picture is a direct measure of how long the key took to show. The extension keeps the sending time of the first key of a burst (a typed key or a text), and the next picture that differs ends it; a wait longer than 15 seconds is no measurement (nothing on the screen changed). The capture helper adds one more number to its second: whether the editor's window was **the one in front** (`GetForegroundWindow`).
