A node the highlight has no outline for (a free-form shape, an image) takes the rectangle of its bounds. |
| D-193 | the editor can **preview the class loop** | locked | The bar gains **Preview class loop**: while it is on, the class loop (D-184) plays in the **edit view** too, for every marked chart on the page - so ticking **Show in class** has a visible effect, and untick or undo takes the animation away together with the mark. Ticking **Show in class** turns the preview on, once. The preview is a setting of this editor only: it is **not saved** with the document and **not sent** to the presenter. It never takes the pointer and has no Steps button (D-185), so the board can be edited while it plays. |
| D-194 | a flow that is done **holds its last step** | locked | A flow whose loop is off plays once and **holds its last step** (D-184): it stays on the step it ended on, **highlight included**, so the board shows where the flow ended. That is what it does today and it does not change. A looping flow never ends. |
| D-195 | the look **travels** with the flow | locked | The class entry (D-183) and the presenter's snapshot (D-181) carry `nodeColor`, `markColor`, `arrowColor` (or hidden) and `nodeTarget` with the rest, so the class view, the presenter, the mirror and a pasted chart show the look the bar shows. An entry made before this wave has none of them and takes the defaults (D-188): its look is exactly what it was. |
| D-196 | the controls for the look are a **Look** section of the bar | locked | The bar (D-177) gains a **Look** section, closed until it is opened, so the bar stays as small as it was: a colour for the highlight, a colour for what travels, a colour for the arrow with **Hide arrow** beside it, a choice of **whole shape** or **text only**, and **Preview class loop** (D-193). Opening it makes the bar taller; the bar still stays **inside the viewport** and is still placed below the chart (D-177). While **Show in class** is on, a change in the Look section writes through to the chart like any other bar setting (D-183). |
| D-197 | the bar is a **movable pill** | locked | Like the toolbar and the properties panel, the bar has a **move grip** and a **collapse** button. Dragging the grip moves the bar anywhere in the editor: it is kept **inside the viewport** (with the margin of D-177) and it **snaps** to an edge it is dropped near. Until the teacher moves it, the bar sits below the chart and follows it (D-177); once moved it **stays where it was put**, whatever the chart or the view does, and **double-clicking the grip** sends it back to following the chart. Where it was put is remembered between sessions, as a place in the window and not in the document - as the other pills are. A remembered place that is not usable (missing, off the window, wrong type) is ignored and the bar follows the chart. |
| D-198 | the bar **collapses into a pill** | locked | The collapse button turns the bar into a small **pill** where the bar was, and pressing the pill opens the bar again. Collapsing changes only what is shown: a flow that is playing keeps playing, and the settings are untouched. A collapsed bar is remembered like the place (D-197), and the pill is movable by the same grip rule. |
| D-199 | moving the bar does not touch the canvas | locked | Pressing or dragging the grip or the pill is **not a click on the canvas**: nothing is deselected, no element is moved, and the flow the bar controls stays the same. The bar's own controls work as before, and after a **window resize** the bar is brought back inside the viewport. |
| D-207 | the colours are chosen from a **swatch pad** | locked | Each colour in the Look section (D-196) - the shape and the text (D-209), what travels and the arrow (D-204) - is chosen from a **swatch pad** in the editor's own style instead of a bare colour input: a row of swatches - **default** (the accent colour; choosing it clears the colour, D-189), then the **neon** red, orange, yellow, green, cyan, blue, purple and pink (#ff3131, #ff9100, #fff01f, #39ff14, #00f0ff, #3d7bff, #bc13fe and #ff2bd6), then white, black and grey - with the **current choice ringed**, and a last swatch that opens a **custom** colour (the browser's own picker) for anything else. A swatch is a button with a name, the pad is one radio group and can be reached and used from the keyboard, and what a swatch sets is a hex colour like any other, so it is saved, carried and read exactly as D-189 and D-195 say. A custom colour that is the same as a swatch rings that swatch. |
| D-201 | what is highlighted: **five choices** | locked | `nodeTarget` (D-191) has **five** values - what the highlight is put on: **shape** (the node's outline: what the highlight is today), **shape + text box** (the outline, and a rectangle on the label's own box), **shape + text** (the outline, and the label's letters), **text box** (a rectangle on the label's box and nothing else: what D-191's "text only" drew) and **text** (only the label's letters, D-202). The bar's _Highlight on_ menu offers them in that order, named _Shape_, _Shape + text box_, _Shape + text_, _Text box_ and _Text_. A node with no label has no text and takes the **shape** for every choice that names a text. A value stored before this wave is read as what it drew: _shape_ stays, and the old _text_ is read as **text box**. Same carrying rules as D-189 and D-195. |
| D-202 | the label's **letters** are highlighted as letters | locked | **Text** and **shape + text** highlight the label's **letters**: the overlay draws the label's text again, **over** the canvas's own, in the highlight colour - in the label's own font, size, line height, alignment and angle, at the label's own place, with the glow of pulse or the lift of focus - so that the letters on the page are the letters that light up, and **no rectangle** is drawn round them. It is the overlay's copy: the label in the document is not changed. Only the label of the step that is current lights up. |
| D-203 | the labels of **arrows** are above the animation | locked | A label on an **arrow** (the yes and no of a decision, D-163) is drawn **above** whatever is animated along that arrow - the beam, the token, the dashes, the pipe - and above the highlight: the overlay draws each arrow label again over the marks, in the label's own font, size, colour and alignment, at its own place, as D-202 does for a node's letters. It is drawn in every view that animates (the editor, the class view, the preview, the presenter) and only while something is animated; it is the overlay's copy and the label in the document is not changed. |
| D-204 | **hiding** and **recolouring** the arrow are the canvas's own drawing | locked | Replaces D-190. **Hide arrow** and the arrow's colour stop being a cover painted over the arrow and become **the canvas's own drawing**: while a flow is animated (played or stepped from the bar, or looping in the class view or the preview) the editor's canvas **does not draw** a hidden arrow - nothing is over it, so nothing is left at its ends or on a node's border - and draws a recoloured arrow **in its colour**. The list the canvas is given to draw is built in one place, the renderer, and that is where the animation's state is applied: for each arrow of an animated flow the canvas is given the arrow **without**, or a **copy** with its stroke colour changed. A copy is kept and reused, so it is not rebuilt each frame, and it is made again when its arrow changes. The arrow in the document and the elements the editor holds are **never changed**; an exported image or file shows the arrow as it is drawn; selection and hit-testing see the arrow as before; and the labels bound to an arrow stay (D-203). The moment the animation stops the arrow is drawn again. The colour of **what travels** (the beam, the token, the dashes and the pipe's highlight) is the overlay's, as D-190 said, and the pipe's strokes are made from it (D-210). D-190's cover - the stroke and the head circle - is removed. |
| D-205 | every window does the same | locked | The presenter window (D-64) and the class view do all of this the same way: the presenter's canvas is its own, so it applies D-204 to its own drawing - from the class entry (D-195) for the flows it loops, and from the snapshot (D-181) for the flow it mirrors - and the letters (D-202) and the arrow labels (D-203) are drawn in its overlay. A window with nothing animated draws everything as it is. |
| D-206 | the colours of the whole animation come in **presets** | locked | The Look section (D-196) opens with a row of **colour presets**. Each sets the **shape** colour, the **text** colour (D-209) and the colour of **what travels** (D-204) **together**, and turns **glow** (D-208) on, so the colours of the whole animation change in one press, with nothing to customise; each colour can still be changed afterwards. There are eight: **Classic** (the accent colour, no glow - the default), **Cyan and pink**, **Green and purple**, **Yellow and blue**, **Orange and cyan**, **Pink and lime**, **Fire and ice** and **High contrast**. The colours are **neon**: saturated, bright colours that read as light and not as paint. A neon yellow or green cannot be seen on a white page at any glow, so each preset has two variants - the **pure neon** for a **dark page**, and a **deep neon** for a **light page**, the most vivid colour that still reaches 3 : 1 contrast against white, the WCAG 2.1 bound for graphics (SC 1.4.11) - and the variant that reads on the **current page background** is the one applied. **Every colour of every preset is held to at least 3 : 1 against the page it is for**, and the shape colour and the colour of what travels differ in **hue by at least 60 degrees**, so the two never blur into one. A preset sets nothing but those colours and the glow (the arrow's colour, Hide arrow, the target and the speed are left as they are), writes through to a marked chart like any other setting (D-183), and shows as **chosen** while its colours are the ones in use. **Classic** clears the colours and the glow, which is the default (D-189). |
| D-208 | **glow**: the highlight as a **neon tube** | locked | **Glow** is a setting of the look that makes the highlight, and what travels, look like a **neon tube** - the neon of a sign - and not like paint: a thin, bright **core**, which is the colour lightened toward white, with a **halo** round it in the colour itself, made of several soft shadows of growing reach so that it blooms, and **no fill**. With glow on, a highlighted node is a glowing outline and nothing else - not a tinted box - and a text box is a glowing rectangle. It applies to the outline of a node, the rectangle on a label's box, the label's **letters** (a hot core in the letters themselves, with the halo as a soft shadow round them) and to the beam, the token, the dashes and the pipe. It is a switch in the Look section (_Neon glow_), **off** by default, so the default look is what it has always been; **on** in every preset but Classic (D-206); remembered between sessions; and carried in the class entry (D-183, D-195) and the presenter's snapshot (D-181) like the colours. An entry from before it has none and takes the default (D-188). The glow takes the colour of what it surrounds - the shape's colour round a shape, the text colour (D-209) round a text box and letters, the animation colour round what travels - and it is the overlay's: it is drawn with strokes, drop shadows and text shadows, so nothing is added to the document. |
| D-209 | the highlight's **shape** and **text** are coloured apart | locked | The highlight colour (D-189) is **bifurcated**: the **shape** colour (`nodeColor`, as before) is the colour of the node's outline, and a new **text** colour (`textColor`) is the colour of the rectangle on a label's box (D-201) and of its letters (D-202). With glow on (D-208) these colours are the **neon highlight only**: they are the colour of the tube and of its halo, and nothing else on the node is coloured by them - there is no fill. Until the text colour is set it **follows** the shape colour, so nothing changes for a look that never uses it. The Look section's _Highlight_ row becomes two, _Shape_ and _Text_, each its own swatch pad (D-207). The presets set both (D-206), and the text colour is validated, remembered, written to the class entry (D-183, D-195) and sent to the presenter (D-181) as the shape colour is; an entry from before it has none, which is "follow the shape" (D-188). |
| D-210 | the **pipe** takes the animation colour | locked | While the animation colour (D-204) is set, the pipe's three strokes (D-171) are made from it: the **outer** stroke a darker shade of it (60 % of the way to black), the **body** the colour itself, and the highlight that runs along it a lighter tint (80 % of the way to white); and the pipe glows (D-208) when glow is on. While it is not set the pipe is what it has always been - dark, grey and white. The shades are computed from the colour and not chosen from a list, so every swatch, preset and custom colour has a pipe. |
| D-211 | what the overlay draws **lines up** with the canvas | locked | A label's **letters** (D-202) and the arrow labels (D-203) are drawn as **SVG text with the canvas's own rule** - the same font, size and line height, and the **same baseline offset** the canvas uses for each line - so a letter lands on the letter under it; a **text box** or **letters** highlight is lifted by _focus_ about **its own centre**, not about the centre of the whole node, so it grows in place instead of drifting off the text it belongs to; and the outline of a node is drawn **inside** the node's box, so that its stroke does not reach past the edge. |
| D-212 | a change of the **view alone** sends the view alone | locked | While nothing in the scene changes - the camera flight of D-170, a scroll, a zoom - the editor sends a **camera** message, carrying the view (the same description the scene message carries), at the screen's own rate, instead of the whole scene; the scene message goes only when the elements, the files or the pages change, and once when a presenter connects (D-181). The presenter applies a camera message by moving its **own view** and does **nothing else**: it does not replace its elements or draw them again, so a flight is as smooth there as in the editor. |
| D-213 | a label is **never seen twice** | locked | While the overlay draws a label again - the letters of a lit label (D-202) and the label of an arrow above the animation (D-203) - the editor's canvas does **not** draw that label: the list the canvas is given (D-204) is built without it. So there is one set of letters on the screen, never two, wherever the chart is moved, scrolled or zoomed, and a copy that sits a pixel away from the original can no longer be seen as a second set. The label in the document is not changed, an exported image shows it, and the canvas draws it again the moment the overlay stops drawing it. It holds for the class loop, the preview, the flow that is played or stepped, and the presenter (D-205). |
| D-214 | leaving the animation is **clear** | locked | Three things that were easy to mix up are told apart. **Minimise** (the close button the toolbox has, on the bar, D-198) only turns the bar into a pill, and the animation goes on. **Stop** ends the animation that is played or stepped, and switches off the preview of the class loop (D-193). And the way out is always in view: the pill says what is running - _playing_, _stepping_ or _preview_ - and carries a **Stop** button, so the animation can be ended without opening the bar; the bar has the same Stop; and **Esc** exits whatever is running - a played flow, the step mode, the preview - in one press, wherever the focus is, unless text is being edited or another mode already uses Esc. Nothing is left running with no visible way to end it. The class view in view mode (D-182) is left by leaving view mode, as before. |
| D-215 | the **laser pointer** is a neon tube | locked | The laser pointer (the tool on the key K) is drawn as a **neon tube** (D-208): a thin, bright **core** - the laser's colour lightened toward white - laid over a **halo** in the laser's own colour, made of several soft shadows of growing reach, so the trail blooms like the red neon of a sign. It is the same trail it was: the same path, the same fade-out and the same length, only drawn as two strokes and a glow. It is the look of the tool and not a setting, and it holds for every laser the editor draws - the teacher's own, a collaborator's in their colour, and the one the presenter window mirrors - and for no other trail (the eraser's and the lasso's are not changed). |
| D-216 | the **emoji bar** has the toolbox's **move** and **close** | locked | The emoji and GIF bar (D-159) becomes one of the editor's **docks**, with the toolbox's own controls: a **move grip** - drag it to put the bar anywhere, and it snaps to an edge it is dropped near - and a **close** button that turns the bar into a **pill** on the nearest edge, which brings it back when it is pressed. Its place and its collapsed state are remembered between sessions, like the toolbox's. It keeps what it had: its tabs, its search, and the drag of an emoji or a GIF onto the canvas (D-157 to D-160). The page bar is not changed. |
| D-217 | the neon glow is **per part** | locked | The one **Neon glow** switch of D-208 is four switches in the Look section: **Neon shape**, **Neon text**, **Neon animation** and **Neon arrows**, each **off** by default. _Shape_ is the node's outline; _text_ is the rectangle on a label's box and its letters; _animation_ is what travels (the beam, the token, the dashes, the pipe); _arrows_ means the flow's **arrows themselves** are drawn by the overlay as neon tubes - a thin bright core, the colour lightened toward white, with a halo of layered shadows round it and no fill - in the **Arrow colour** (D-190), or the animation colour when there is none, while the animation plays; the canvas does not draw an arrow the overlay draws (D-204), and the labels of those arrows are drawn above them (D-203, D-213). The presets of D-206 turn shape, text and animation on and leave the arrows as they are; Classic turns all four off. Each switch is remembered, carried in the class entry (D-183, D-195) and in the presenter's snapshot (D-181), and an entry from before, which has one glow, reads as shape, text and animation on and arrows off (D-188). It is the overlay's: nothing is added to the document. |
| D-218 | the animation **speed** goes down to 10 | locked | The slowest speed of what travels along the arrows (D-187) is **10** and not 40, in the slider and in every place a speed is read - the stored setting, the class entry and the snapshot. The fastest is unchanged. |
| D-219 | a view that moves alone is sent at the **screen's rate** | locked | The camera message of D-212 is sent at most every **16 ms** (about 60 a second) while only the view moves; a scene message keeps its own interval (40 ms). Both leave at once when the sender has been idle. |
| D-220 | the focus stands out by a **margin**, not by a share | locked | The _Focus_ node style lifted the node by a share of its size (12%), which on a wide node was a wide gap. It now stands out from the node by a fixed **margin of 5 scene units** on every side, whatever the node's size, scaled about its own centre; the other styles are as they were. |
| D-221 | a chart **remembers its script** | locked | Every chart the Flow tab inserts carries its **chart id** in the `customData.flow` of every one of its elements, and its **first shape** also carries the **record**: its **preset** (built-in, or custom, D-228, which then travels by value), its **script**, its **fit mode** (D-226) and its **node shape** (D-229). Each node also says which shape it was given. All of it is saved with the document, so the chart can be edited later. Nothing else about a chart's elements changes: it is still ordinary shapes, text and arrows (D-161). A chart made **before** this has no script; _Edit script_ still opens it, with a script rebuilt from what is on the page - one line for each node, in step order (D-43), and `A -> B` lines for the arrows of a network - and the preset the chart's shapes suggest, which the teacher can change. |
| D-222 | the **script editor** opens beside the chart | locked | An **Edit script** button on the animation bar (D-197) and on the Flow tab opens a **panel** for the chart that is selected (or the flow of any selected shape or arrow, D-180). The panel is the toolbox's own kind: it has a **grip** to move it and a **close** button, a place that is remembered, and it never covers the chart it edits when there is room beside it. It holds the script in a text box, a **preset** choice, the **fit mode** (D-226) and the **shape** (D-229), the live check (D-224) and **Apply**. Typing in it never reaches the canvas's single-key shortcuts, and closing it with changes not applied asks nothing and keeps the text until the panel is opened again on the same chart. |
| D-223 | **Apply** keeps the nodes that match | locked | Apply changes the chart **in place**, as **one undo step**. A node of the new script is matched to a node of the chart **by its label** (the same label in the same order, then the same label out of order); a matched node **keeps its id, its position, its size, its shape, its colours and its _Show in class_ mark** (D-183), and the arrows that joined matched nodes keep theirs. A line with no match **adds** a node, placed by the layout, and a node with no line **removes** the node and the arrows that touched it. A chart that was moved, resized (D-173) or laid out by hand keeps what the teacher did to the nodes that remain; the layout is only run for what was added, and only the whole chart if the preset or the fit mode was changed. Changing the preset, the fit mode or the chart-wide shape, and applying to a chart that is **spread over pages** (its pages are cut again), is a **rebuild** of the chart in the same place, which keeps nothing but the chart's id and its animation settings. A steps chart is numbered again when a step comes or goes. The chart's id and its animation settings (D-183) are kept either way. |
| D-224 | the script is **checked while it is typed** | locked | While the script is edited - in the panel and in the Flow tab - each problem is shown **with its line number**: a stray `->`, an empty tier, a `?` without two lines after it, a shape mark that is not closed, a line that is too long for any shape (D-229). Below the box the panel says how many **nodes and arrows** the script makes and the **text size** the chart will have in the chosen fit mode (D-226). **Apply** and **Insert chart** are off while a line cannot be read, and say why; a script with no usable line, or one that is too big for its fit mode, builds nothing and says so when the button is pressed (D-164, D-165). The check is the parser's own - the same rules that build the chart - so what it accepts always builds. |
| D-225 | a **script guide** for every kind of chart | locked | The Flow tab has a **Guide**: for each preset - the seven of D-163 and the teacher's own (D-228) - the script's rules in plain words for a teacher, a **worked example**, what the chart looks like in a sentence, and a **Use this example** button that puts the example into the script box. The guide also teaches the shape marks (D-229) and the fit modes (D-226). It is written in our own words, ships with the code and needs no network (D-169), and the one-line hint under the script box stays. |
| D-226 | a **large chart** is the teacher's choice: fit, spread, or grow | locked | The Flow tab and the panel (D-222) offer three **fit modes**. **Fit to page** is the behaviour of D-165, and the default: the chart is scaled to the page's rect down to the text-size floor, and a script that needs less is reported, not shrunk. When a script does not fit, the message offers the other two. **Spread over pages** builds the chart at a readable size and **continues onto the following pages**: the nodes are cut into groups that each fit a page, in step order, on pages that follow the current one (made when they do not exist, the same size as the current page, D-144), and an arrow that would cross from one page to the next is replaced by a short stub on each side with a label - _continues on page N_ and _from page N_ - so every page is one projector screen. **Allow larger than page** builds **one connected chart** at a readable size even if it is larger than its page, centred on the page, with no page-rect limit; the camera flight (D-170) follows it. In every mode no two shapes overlap, and the text is never below the floor. |
| D-227 | **all seven presets** build very large charts | locked | Every preset of D-163 builds a script of **150 nodes** in **Spread over pages** and in **Allow larger than page** with **no two shapes overlapping** (**Fit to page** keeps the rule of D-165: it shrinks to the floor, and then refuses and offers the other two), the text at or above the floor, the arrows bound at both ends (D-173) and a build time under **one second**. A long _process_ or _steps_ chart **wraps into rows** that run back and forth, so a long chain does not become a long strip; a long _cycle_ stays a ring and grows with the node count; _tiers_, _integration_ and _network_ grow by their own geometry. The animation (D-166) and the step order (D-43) follow the chart across rows and across pages. |
| D-228 | the teacher can define **custom presets** | locked | A **New preset** form in the Flow tab makes a custom preset out of: a **name**, a one-line **description**, an **example script**, a **layout** - _top-down_, _left-right_, _ring_, _tiers_ or _hub_, the geometries the built-in seven use, which also decide the script rules (D-164) - a **colour set** (the five fills of the built-in presets, with the outline colour chosen) and a default **node shape** (D-229). A custom preset is kept in the browser's own storage under its own key, is listed after the built-in seven, can be **edited, duplicated and deleted**, and can be **exported** and **imported** as a small JSON file; an imported file is read defensively (D-188), a file that is not a preset file, has no name or example, or names a layout that does not exist is **refused with the reason** and changes nothing, and a name already in use is made unique. It is data and no code is run, nothing is sent anywhere, and charts built from it remember it by name and by value (D-221), so they edit and open on a machine that does not have it. |
| D-229 | the **shape** of a node can be changed, and a node grows to hold its text | locked | A chart has a **node shape**, chosen in the Flow tab and the panel: _Preset's own_ (the default, what each preset draws today), _Rectangle_, _Rounded_, _Ellipse_, _Circle_ or _Diamond_. A **circle** is an ellipse whose width and height are **equal**. A line of the script can start with a **shape mark** that overrides the chart's shape for that node: `[Label]` a rectangle, `(Label)` a rounded rectangle, `((Label))` an ellipse, `{Label}` a circle and `<Label>` a diamond; a line with no mark takes the chart's shape. The marks work with the rest of the script: `? ` still starts a question, `A -> B` still joins two nodes (each end may carry a mark; the node takes the mark the first time its label is seen), ` | ` still separates the items of a tier and the first line of an integration chart is still the hub; a mark that is opened and not closed is a problem (D-224). A node's shape does not change how it is laid out or animated: the highlight follows the node's own shape (D-192) and a circle or a diamond is bound by its arrows like any shape (D-173). **A node grows to hold its label**: its size is the label's text box plus the room that the shape needs round it - the inscribed rectangle of an ellipse or a diamond is smaller than its bounds - so the text always lies **inside** the shape, with a margin, whatever the shape and the label; a circle grows to the larger of the two sizes, equal on both sides. Shapes survive _Apply_ (D-223): a matched node keeps its shape unless its mark, or the chart's shape, was changed. |
| D-230 | the shape of **one node** is changed by a **keyword**, and from the editor | locked | A line of the script, or one end of an `A -> B`, can start with `@` and the name of a shape - `@rectangle`, `@rounded`, `@ellipse`, `@circle` or `@diamond` - and then the words: `@circle Plan` is what `{Plan}` is (D-229), said in words. It works wherever a node's words are written - a step, an item of a tier, the hub, a question (`? @rounded Ready?`), a branch, either end of an arrow (`@circle A -> @diamond B`) - the marks of D-229 keep working beside it, and the keyword wins when a line has both. A name that is not a shape (`@hexagon`), or a keyword with no words after it, is a **problem with its line** (D-224). In the script editor (D-222), when one or more nodes of the chart are selected on the canvas, the panel offers **This node's shape**: the chart's own, or one of the five. Choosing one rewrites **only those nodes' pieces of the script** - every other line and character is as it was - with the keyword (or with none, for `the chart's shape`), and applies it **in place** (D-223): the node keeps its place, size and colours and its _Show in class_ mark, the change is one undo step, and a node of a network is found by its words, not by its number. **Changing the preset or the chart-wide shape** (D-226, D-229) rebuilds the chart, and every node whose line has a keyword or a mark **keeps its shape** because it is in the script; only the nodes with none follow the new preset or the new chart shape. The guide teaches the keywords beside the marks (D-225). |
| D-231 | a chart can be drawn **without arrows** | locked | Every chart has an **Arrows** setting: _As the preset_ (the default: what each preset draws today) or _None_. With **None** the chart is its shapes and nothing between them: no arrow, no connector and no _yes_ or _no_ label is drawn, the script is read as it always was, and the shapes of a chart that has no arrows are **one flow** by the chart they were made in (D-221), and the animation walks them **in the order of the script** they were made from - the order they were meant to be read in - which is also the order a list, a grid read across and a timeline are read in. It is a choice of the Flow tab and of the script editor (D-222), it is remembered on the chart with its preset, script, fit mode and shape (D-221), it can be a default of a custom preset (D-228), and changing it is a **rebuild** in place (D-223). |
| D-232 | the **gallery**: nine layouts that need no arrows | locked | Nine layouts join the seven of D-163, each filled from a script of one line for each part and each also a built-in preset that the Flow tab lists, the guide teaches (D-225) and a custom preset may be laid out in (D-228): **list** (the lines as blocks, one under the next), **grid** (a table: one line for each row, the cells of a row separated by a vertical bar), **matrix** (a grid of two columns, with larger cells), **pyramid** (stacked blocks, the first line the narrowest, each wider than the last), **funnel** (the same turned over: the first line the widest), **timeline** (the lines as boxes along a horizontal line), **target** (rings one inside the next, the first line the outermost), **venn** (two to four overlapping circles, one for each line) and **hierarchy** (a tree: one line for each node, its level the number of tabs or pairs of spaces it starts with, a node joined to the nearest line above it with one level less). A gallery chart is made of ordinary shapes and text (D-161), is laid out by the same builder, scales to fit the page down to the text floor and is spread, enlarged or refused as D-226 says, has the node shape and keywords of D-229 and D-230, and draws no arrows but the connectors of a hierarchy and the line of a timeline. The shapes a layout draws by default are its own (a pyramid's blocks are rectangles, a target's rings and a venn's sets are ellipses and circles, the rest are rounded) and the node shape of a chart overrides them. |
| D-233 | **switching layout keeps the words** | locked | When the kind of chart is changed - in the script editor (D-222) or in the Flow tab with a script that has been edited - the **words are kept and the script is written again for the new kind**: the nodes' words come across in their order, with the shape keywords they had (D-230); rows and tiers become the rows of a grid, a matrix or the tiers of a tiers chart and the other way, a chart that was joined by arrows keeps the arrows it had when the new kind is a network, a list or a run of steps becomes the first level of a hierarchy under its first line, and a hierarchy becomes a list in the order it is written and, made into rows, a row for each of its levels. Nothing is lost that the new kind can say, and the new script is what the teacher then reads and edits. The script editor applies the change as the rebuild in place that a change of kind always is (D-223). |
| D-234 | the **grid** is a table | locked | In a **grid** the cells of a row are separated by a vertical bar and every row has the columns of the widest row, the shorter rows leaving the cells at their right empty and not drawn. A line that starts with `#` is a **header row**: its cells are drawn in the first fill and bold-filled apart from the others. The columns are as wide as the widest cell of their column and the rows as high as the highest cell of their row, so the cells meet in straight lines with only a small gap between them and nothing overlaps; a **matrix** is a grid of two columns, with larger cells and its four quadrants in four fills. A node is found in a grid by its row and its column, and Apply keeps the cell that a label has (D-223). |
| D-235 | a **target** and a **venn** overlap on purpose | locked | The rule that no two shapes overlap (D-165) is for charts that join shapes; a **target** is rings that lie one inside the next and a **venn** is circles that cross, and in them **overlap is the layout**. Their shapes are drawn with a fill that lets the others show through, so the lens where two circles cross can be seen, and each label is drawn at the shape's own centre of its free part - the ring's band, the circle's far side - so that no two labels overlap. A target takes up to six rings and a venn up to four sets; a line past that is a **problem** (D-224). |
| D-236 | a **hierarchy** is a tree with connector lines | locked | A hierarchy's nodes are laid out in levels, a parent over its children, with the children of a node in the order of the script, and the nodes are joined to their parent by **connector lines with no arrowhead**, bound at both ends like the arrows of D-173 so that they follow a shape that is moved. A line indented by more than one level under the line above it, and a first line that is indented, are read as one level under their parent and as the root, and are **problems** (D-224). The tree is as wide as its widest level needs and is fitted to the page as D-165 says. |
| D-237 | a **timeline** has a line and its boxes alternate | locked | A timeline draws one **horizontal line** across the chart with a small dot where each part is, and the boxes sit on the line's two sides in turn - the first above, the second below - each joined to its dot by a short stem, so that long labels have room and do not overlap. The line is not a shape; its pieces are arrows without heads, and the segments between two dots are read by the animation (D-238) while the lead past the first and the last dot and the stems join nothing. |
| D-238 | the **timeline's line** is animated | locked | A timeline's line is drawn as **one segment for each gap** between two dots, from the dot of one moment to the dot of the next, and each segment **joins its two boxes** in the animation: the step after a moment is the next one in the script, and the beam, the token, the marching dashes and the pipe (D-166) run along the segment from one moment to the next, in the direction the script goes. The segments are real arrows of the animation's graph, so they take the colours, the arrow glow (D-217) and _Hide arrow_ (D-204) like any arrow. The line also runs a little past the first and the last dot, and that part is decoration and joins nothing. With **Arrows** set to _None_ (D-231) the timeline keeps its line but its segments join nothing and nothing travels along them. The connectors of a hierarchy (D-236) are bound arrows and are animated as they always were. |
| D-239 | the kind of chart is chosen from a **picture gallery** | locked | Each kind of chart - the seven, the nine of the gallery and the teacher's own - has a **small picture of its layout**, drawn from the chart that the same builder makes from the kind's example, so the picture is always what the kind draws: its shapes in their fills and shapes, its arrows, connectors and lines, with no words. In the Flow tab the choice of kind (D-161) is a **grid of those pictures with the name under each**; a custom preset's picture is in its own colours and shape (D-228). The script editor (D-222) shows the picture of the kind beside its choice. The buttons still have their names and descriptions for a reader that cannot see the pictures. |
| D-240 | on a timeline the **beam and the token run from box to box** | locked | On a timeline the beam and the token do not run only between the dots: from one moment to the next they leave the first moment's **box**, go down its stem to its dot, along the line to the next dot and up that moment's stem into its **box**, and start again from that box for the next hop. Each stem says which box it belongs to. The marching dashes and the pipe stay on the line and the stems as they are drawn, so that no stem is marked twice. A timeline with Arrows None has nothing to run along (D-238). |
| D-241 | drawn elements have an **ink look**: normal, neon, chalk, marker, pencil | locked | Everything that is drawn with ink - the text (a text on its own, and the words inside a shape or on an arrow), a freedraw stroke, a rectangle, a diamond, an ellipse, a line and an arrow, with its stroke and its fill - has an **ink look**; an image, a frame and an embedded page do not: **Normal** (as today), **Neon**, **Chalk**, **Marker** or **Pencil**. It is chosen in the properties panel when any of them is selected, as a row of five named buttons, and it is set on the selected elements and on the words inside the selected shapes; the look chosen stays for the next element drawn, with any of the tools, until it is changed, and it is saved with the element (`customData.ink`: the look and an **amount** from 1 to 100, 50 when not given), so it is kept by save and load, copy and paste, undo and collaboration, and a file written before it draws as Normal. The look changes how the **ink** is drawn and nothing else: the shape, the box of a text, the selection, the hit test and the size of the element are not changed. The ink colour is the element's own colour; each look offers a row of named colours that sets it (neon: pink, cyan, lime, yellow, orange, violet, white; chalk: white, yellow, pink, blue, green, orange; marker: yellow, pink, green, blue, orange, purple; pencil: graphite, blue, red, green, brown). The **amount** means one thing for each look: the glow of neon, the dust of chalk, the width of marker, the darkness of pencil (the slider is named for it). **Neon** is a core lightened toward white inside a halo in the ink colour (the recipe of the laser, D-215). **Chalk** is the ink with fine gaps in it and rough edges, a faint soft edge, and loose specks of chalk dust scattered around it (more amount, more and farther dust). **Marker** is a highlighter: a wide, half see-through ink, wider with more amount, so that where one element crosses another the overlap is darker. **Pencil** is a thin, grainy ink: a low amount is light, faint and broken up, a high amount is dark and solid. The gaps, the grain and the roughness come from the element's own seed, so an element looks the same every time it is drawn and in every export. A look is drawn on the canvas, in a PNG export, in an SVG export, in the presenter and in a flow's animation; an export makes room around the element for what the look draws beyond it (the halo, the dust), so nothing is cut off. |
| D-242 | the left **hamburger menu** is tidied | locked | The left menu no longer has Excalidraw+, the social links, Sign up / Sign in or Live collaboration. What is left is in three groups with a line between them, so that the menu fits without scrolling: (1) Open, Insert document, Code panel, Save, Export, Save as image; (2) Presenter view, **Canvas background**, Theme, Language, Preferences; (3) Command palette, Search, Help, Clear canvas. Nothing outside the menu is changed: the welcome screen keeps its own entries and the share dialog stays. The visual debug entry is still there in a development build. |
| D-243 | each page has a **paper** | locked | A page has a **paper**: the sheet it is written on. It is a fact about the page (`page.paper`: type, colour, grain, age), so it is saved with the document, copied with the page and undone with it, and a page without one is plain, in the canvas colour, as it always was. The **types** are plain, single line, double line, grid, dot grid, Cornell (single line, a cue column on the left and a summary line at the foot), isometric and music staff (eight staves of five lines); the rules and dots lie behind everything, inside the page's own rect only, and their spacing is a share of the page's height, so a larger page has the same number of rules, farther apart. The **colours** are twelve named ones: white, cream, parchment, legal yellow, sky, mint, rose, lavender, kraft, green chalkboard, blackboard and midnight (the last three are dark; their rules are light), and a paper of the canvas colour lets the canvas show through. **Grain** (0 to 100) is a film grain over the sheet; **age** (0 to 100) is a warm tint (a smudge on a dark paper), darker edges and fibres. The grain and the fibres come from the page's own seed, so a page looks the same every time it is drawn and in every export. The paper is drawn on the canvas, in the presenter and the class view, in a PNG, a PDF and an SVG of the page (an image export draws the paper of **every page that has a paper and some of what is exported on it**, each whole and under its content, and the picture grows to hold those pages with the padding of an export around them; a page with a paper and none of the content, a page without a paper and an export of a frame add nothing; and the preview in the export dialog shows that same picture) and is not selectable. Four one-click **looks** set a paper and the ink that goes with it for the next elements drawn (the stroke colour and the ink look of D-241), and do not change what is drawn already: _Old paper_ (single line, parchment, grain 35, age 70, graphite pencil), _Green chalkboard_ (plain, green chalkboard, grain 25, age 25, white chalk), _Blackboard_ (plain, blackboard, grain 20, age 15, white chalk) and _Neon night_ (dot grid, midnight, grain 10, cyan neon). The paper can be given to every page with one button. |
| D-244 | the page controls are in the **hamburger menu** | locked | The menu has a **Page and paper** entry in its second group, next to Canvas background (which is still the colour of the board outside the pages). It opens one panel with the page's own controls - its frame, its number, its size, its width and its height, moved out of the properties panel, which no longer has them - and the paper: the type, the colours, the grain and the age, the four looks and the button that uses this paper on every page. Every change is a change of the current page, made by the same action as before, so it is undone with it. The entry is there when the pages system is on. |
| D-245 | the **emoji bar** folds into a **round button**, and the folded bar cannot be clicked | locked | Replaces the _pill_ of D-216 for the emoji bar only (the toolbox and the properties panel keep their slim pills). The close button of the emoji bar turns it into a **round button** with a smile on it, 40 pixels across, on the edge nearest to where the bar was; pressing it opens the bar again and dragging it moves the bar's place, as the pill did. While the bar is folded **nothing of it can be pressed**: its emoji and GIFs are not there to click or drag onto the canvas, whatever the bar's own style says. |
| D-246 | a **node** can hold a picture, a GIF or a video, cut to the node's own shape | locked | A node of a chart (a rectangle, a rounded rectangle, an ellipse or a diamond) can hold **media**: a picture, a GIF or a video. It is put in by **dropping the file on the node**, or by selecting the node and pressing **Add picture or video** in the Flow tab (the same button says **Replace picture or video** when it has some, and **Remove picture** takes it out). The media **fills the node's box and is cut to the node's own shape**: a rectangle holds a rectangle, a rounded node a rounded cut with the node's radius, an ellipse a round one and a diamond a diamond one. It is **cut** to the box - the middle of the picture that has the box's shape - and is **never shrunk to fit with borders round it**. A node holds one piece of media: a new one replaces it. The media is an element of its own over the node (a picture, or for a video a still of its first frame and the video panel over it), saved with the node in `customData.media` (what node it is in, the shape and the radius), and it is **grouped with the node and its words**, so it moves, resizes and is copied with them; Layout, Reset and the flow animations treat it as **part of the node's step**, not a step of its own (the step's box holds it). The words of the node are a **caption below it**, or hidden, as D-248 says, and **come back into the node when the media is taken out**. A GIF animates and a video plays as a **silent loop**, in the editor, the presenter and the class view, each cut to the shape; a PNG, a PDF and an SVG export show the **first frame** of a GIF and of a video (the still of its first frame) cut to the shape. A file reaches a node by any road: dragged in from the computer, dragged from the sidebar's list of files, or an emoji or a GIF dragged from the emoji bar; dropped anywhere but on a node of a chart, each is the canvas's own business as before. When the node's **shape or size is changed later** - by the script and Apply, or by its own handles - its media is cut to the new shape and fitted to the new box, a node that is only moved takes its media along, and what the teacher did to the media itself is left alone while the node is as it was. While a flow is highlighted (in the editor, and in the class view) a node that holds media is **only outlined**: no wash of the look's colour is laid over the picture. In the **presenter** the video of a node plays by itself as a loop and does not follow the editor's position, and the editor does not publish one for it. When the chart is **made again from its script** (its kind, its fit, its shape or its arrows were changed, so every node is made anew), the media **comes across** to the node made for the same line, with its group, and is fitted to it; and the media of a node whose line is **taken out of the script** goes with the node, in the editor and in a chart changed in place. The **video** is cut to the shape, and not the panel it is in: what the panel puts round the video - its lock, and the choices of a screenshot of it (read the text, place on this page, place on a new page) - is not cut with it, so a screenshot of a video in a node can be placed. A file that is neither a picture nor a video is not taken. |
| D-247 | the picture of a node has **four fits** and a **crop screen**, and is edited in **Edit chart** | locked | The part of the picture that a node shows is its **view**: a rectangle of the picture, in the picture's own pixels, that has the node's box's shape (so nothing is stretched) and may reach past the edges of the picture, where the node shows through. There are four **fits**, which choose it: **Auto crop to shape** (the picture fills the shape, centred, and is cut to it; the default), **Crop in** (the teacher's own choice of the part to keep, which fills the shape), **Best fit** (all of the picture, as large as the node's box lets it be, and the node's outline trims what lies beyond the shape) and **Fit into the shape** (all of the picture, as large as the _shape_ lets it be, so that its four corners touch the outline: for an ellipse and a diamond it is smaller than best fit, and for a rectangle it is the same). The fit and the view are saved with the media (`customData.media`: `fit`, `view` and the picture's own size, `natural`), and they are drawn by the canvas, the SVG export, the overlay of a GIF and the video of a node alike, each cut to the node's shape (D-246); media saved before this has no view and is drawn as it was. The **crop screen** is opened by **double-clicking the picture** or by **Crop picture** in Edit chart. It is a screen of its own like a photo editor's: the whole picture, a **crop box** over it that has the **shape of the node** (a rectangle, a rounded one, an ellipse or a diamond) with everything outside the shape dimmed, the **rule of thirds** across it and **eight handles**, and a bar across the top with the four fits as buttons, **Reset** (back to auto), **Replace picture or video** (a new file is the node's picture and the screen is open on it again), **Remove picture** (the picture goes, the screen closes and the words are back in the node), the choice of the **words of the node** (a caption below it, or hidden under the picture, D-248: it is the node's and takes effect at once, and Cancel does not take it back), **Cancel** and **Apply**. Dragging a handle resizes the box and dragging the box moves it, and both make the fit _Crop in_; the box always keeps the node's shape, so a corner and a side both resize it about the opposite one and nothing is stretched. **Apply** (or Enter) keeps the fit and the view on the media and closes the screen, **Cancel** (or Esc) keeps nothing. It works for a picture, a GIF and a video (a video is cropped on its first frame; with no frame to show the fits still work). When the node is **moved** the crop is the same; when its **shape or size** is changed the fit is worked out again for the new shape (a crop keeps the middle and the width of its part, in the box's new shape). The button that was **Edit script** is **Edit chart** (on the animation bar and in the Flow tab), and the **picture of the selected node** - **Add picture or video**, **Replace picture or video**, **Remove picture**, **Crop picture** and the choice of the words (D-248) - is a section of its panel, **no longer in the Flow tab**. The section is there whenever the selection is about **one node**: the node, its picture, its caption or its words, or the **whole group** of them (which is what a click on the node selects) - not only when one element is selected. |
| D-248 | the **words** of a node that holds media are a **caption below** it, or hidden | locked | Replaces what D-246 said of the words of a node that holds media (that they stay under it, unseen but selectable). While a node holds media its words are in one of two states, which the teacher chooses in **Edit chart** ("Words of the node", shown when the node holds media): a **caption below the node** (the default) or **hidden under the picture**. As a **caption** the words leave the node and are an ordinary text of their own, centred under the node a little way off, grouped with the node and its media so that they move together; they are seen, not locked, and can be selected and edited by double-clicking them (double-clicking the picture opens the crop screen, D-247). The caption is part of the node's step like the words in a node are: the step's box, the highlight, the presenter and the class view include it, selecting it selects the flow, and the script's Apply matches the node by its words and keeps the caption (and the media) of a node that stays. A caption goes with its node when the node is moved by itself (by a layout or the script), and is left where it is put when the caption is moved by the teacher, alone or with the node as a group. When the node **changes size** (its shape is changed, so it grows or shrinks) a caption that the teacher has not moved is put **under the node again**, centred, and is not left under the picture; one the teacher moved by itself stays where it is put. As **hidden** the words stay in the node, unseen (no opacity) and **locked**, so that a click does not find them, and the media covers the node; the opacity and the lock they had are kept and come back. Either state can be changed to the other at any time, and **taking the media out** (Remove picture) puts the words back into the node, as they were, whichever state they were in. When the chart is **made again** from its script, the new nodes' words are put in the state the choice says. The choice is kept on the media (`customData.media.words`). The words of a node without media are not touched. |
| D-249 | the **arrows of a chart follow its nodes** | locked | An arrow that joins two nodes of a chart is bound to them, and the editor moves its ends when a node is moved or resized by the editor. A node that something else changes - the script's Apply giving it another shape or size, a layout - has its arrows put where the editor puts them, and they do not run into the middle of a node that has grown. This is done once for each change, with the editor's own routine for bound arrows, and the arrows are not changed when they are where they should be. |
| D-250 | the **files of a panel** are kept with the document | locked | The files a panel is made of - the video of a panel that is a file small enough to travel in the document, and the picture of a poster - are saved to the browser's storage and read back when the document is opened, as the picture of an image is, and are not removed as unused when it is opened. A video in a node no longer says its file is _no longer in the document_ after the page is reloaded, and the page is not closed while such a file is being saved. A video that is too big to travel in the document is in the browser's media store, as before. |
| D-251 | the properties of a **panel** are its choices | locked | The Panel section of the properties of a selected panel holds the panel's choices - the camera and the reading of its text - and **nothing that cannot be pressed**: the panel's kind, its state (live or poster) and a heading "Actions" are not shown. |
| D-252 | the public repo has four branches and a pull request goes `dev-draft` -> `dev` only | locked | The public repo keeps `main` (the current public release), **`version-1`** (the first release, kept as it was published), **`dev`** (a **separate history** that starts at the scrubbed tree of private commit 517fd3c and then holds **one commit for each first-parent commit of the private `main`**; only the sync writes to it) and **`dev-draft`** (where collaborators commit). A collaborator commits to `dev-draft` and opens a pull request **from `dev-draft` to `dev` only**; nobody but the owner merges `dev` into `main`, and nothing is pushed to `main` by a collaborator. **The release cycle** (settled by the owner on 2026-10-29): the repaired first release is kept as the branch **`v1.1`**; `main` is the current release, and `dev` and `dev-draft` are brought back in line with it after each release and after each pull request that is taken; when a newer release is to replace `main`, the one that goes is first kept as a branch of its own (`v2`, then `v3`...) with a README that says so, and only then is `dev` merged into `main`. |
| D-253 | the scrub rewrites, it does not only delete | locked | Text written in the method's own wording (comments, docs, commit messages, config remarks) is **rewritten as ordinary English** through a **reviewed rewrite map** (`scripts/public-sync/rewrite-map.json`: a sentence -> plain wording table kept in the private repo). Ids (D-n, AC-n) are removed together with the words that depended on them, so no artefacts are left (no empty brackets, no dangling "of", no half sentences). A remark that says the owner asked is rewritten as a feature statement ("the teacher can ...", "added so that ..."). Text in the method's wording that is **not in the map is refused**: the sync stops with the paragraph named, until a rewrite is added. Locale files are never touched. |
| D-254 | no file a feature needs is left out | locked | Only an explicit **deny list** is removed (the method's own folders and files: sessions, tasks, kit, SPEC.md, HANDOVER.md, the agent notes, the gate and row-check scripts, the private docs). Everything else is kept. A **closure check** runs on the scrubbed tree: every relative import, every asset a file names, every script a `package.json` calls and every file a config names must exist; a missing one stops the sync. Files the public repo owns (its README, `docs/FEATURES.md`, `clips/`) are never overwritten or deleted. |
| D-255 | the public commit message is derived and rewritten | locked | The message of a `dev` commit comes from the description of the private task that was merged (the body of its task file), run through the same rewrite, with a plain one-line subject and the same author and date. A commit with no task description gets a subject written from its changed paths. No id, no session name and no mention of the method may remain. |
| D-256 | a leak guard runs before anything is pushed | locked | After the scrub and before a push, the guard scans the whole scrubbed tree and the new commit messages for the method's words and ids, for secrets (keys, tokens, bridge tokens, local user paths and addresses) and for the deny list. A hit stops the sync and prints file, line and rule. The push is **automatic after the guard passes**; the **first** push of `dev` and any new branch waits for the owner's go. |
| D-257 | each branch has a README that says what the branch is | locked | The README is **public-owned** per branch and is never produced by the scrub. `main` and `dev`: what the product is, its main features, install and offline setup, and how to contribute. **`version-1`**: says plainly that this is the first release, which is **superseded**, lists the features that were published but **do not work** in it (the in-browser Python run, undo of a page delete with its content, the presenter mirror, the code panel's read text and screenshot, the split offer for a big insert, the selection bar's reformat, a video the browser cannot play) and points to the branch that carries the repairs: `main` (the second release) and `v1.1` (the first release with its repairs alone). Each of `dev` and `version-1` keeps its own README and its own copy of the features guide (`docs/FEATURES.md`), so that no branch describes a feature it does not have; the drafts live in `scripts/public-sync/readmes/`. **`dev-draft`**: tells a collaborator to **commit here and open a pull request to `dev`**, how the change comes back into the private project (D-259) and what is checked first. |
| D-258 | the mirror runs by hooks and keeps its state private | locked | `post-commit` and `post-merge` hooks (tracked under `.husky`) run the sync on `main` only. The sync uses plumbing (`git fast-import`) into the clone of the public repo, keeps its state (last mirrored private commit, rewrite map hash) in the private `.git`, and can be run by hand (`status`, `run`, `scan`, `explain`) without pushing. A second run with no new commit changes nothing. |
| D-259 | a collaborator's code comes back in through the same method | locked | `scripts/public-intake.mjs <pr>` fetches a collaborator's pull request into a scratch branch of the clone, lists the changed files and the new behaviour as a draft of rows for the private SPEC.md, and prints the checklist: write the rows, open a new private task and worktree, compare against the collaborator's code, reimplement under the method, merge privately, let the sync publish it, then merge the pull request into `dev`. It never merges into the private repo itself. |

| AC-195 | every preset builds a **connected, bound** chart | `packages/excalidraw/tests/flowPresets.test.ts` | For each of the seven presets and several label counts (one, two, many): every arrow's two bindings name shapes **in the result**, every shape's `boundElements` names the arrow back, every shape's label is bound text, ids are unique, the same seed gives the same chart, and the result touches no DOM. Tests: `AC-195 every preset builds bound arrows with both sides agreeing`, `AC-195 every shape carries its label as bound text`, `AC-195 ids are unique and the seed makes the chart repeatable`, `AC-195 a loop returns to its first step`, `AC-195 the decision rejoins`, `AC-195 the tiers stack and the hub is in the middle`. |
| AC-196 | the **script** becomes nodes by the rules, and a script with nothing in it builds nothing | `packages/excalidraw/tests/flowScript.test.ts` | Pure-function tests: blank lines are ignored, labels are trimmed, a tier line splits into its items, the first line of an integration is the hub, a `?` line takes the next two lines as its branches, `A -> B` makes an edge and creates each node once, and an empty script returns an error and no elements. Tests: `AC-196 blank lines are ignored and labels are trimmed`, `AC-196 a tier line splits into its items`, `AC-196 the first line of an integration is the hub`, `AC-196 a question takes the next two lines as its branches`, `AC-196 an edge line creates each node once`, `AC-196 an empty script builds nothing and says so`. |
| AC-197 | the chart **fits the page** and nothing overlaps | `packages/excalidraw/tests/flowPresets.test.ts` | For one, ten and a hundred nodes on the default page and on a small page: every shape is inside the page rect, no two shapes overlap, the text size never goes below the floor, and a script too big for the floor is **reported** and not shrunk past it. Tests: `AC-197 every shape is inside the page`, `AC-197 no two shapes overlap`, `AC-197 the text never goes below the floor`, `AC-197 a script too big for the floor is reported`. |
| AC-198 | the studio puts the chart on the **current page**, from the script, and fetches nothing | `excalidraw-app/tests/flowStudio.test.tsx` | With the second page active: choosing a preset shows its description; typing a script and pressing the button adds **ordinary elements stamped to that page** and selects them; undo removes them all in one step; a script with nothing in it adds nothing and shows why; and no request is made. Tests: `AC-198 a preset inserts onto the current page`, `AC-198 the script fills the chart`, `AC-198 undo removes the whole chart`, `AC-198 an empty script adds nothing and says why`, `AC-198 nothing leaves the machine`. |
| AC-199 | every style draws along the **arrow's own path** and **writes nothing** | `packages/excalidraw/tests/flowStyles.test.tsx` | For each of beam, marching, token and pulse: the overlay's moving mark sits on the arrow's path, checked **through `sceneCoordsToViewportCoords`** and not a re-derived formula, on a straight and on a curved arrow; the mark moves when the arrow is moved; and after playing, no element is added, changed or removed and `appState` is untouched. Tests: `AC-199 the beam travels the arrow's path`, `AC-199 the token travels the arrow's path`, `AC-199 the marching dashes follow the arrow`, `AC-199 the pulse is on the current step`, `AC-199 a curved arrow is followed, and a moved arrow too`, `AC-199 no style writes into the document`, `AC-199 the overlay is above the canvases it draws on`, `AC-199 the marks are where the arrows are, on the canvas's own pixels`. |
| AC-200 | **present steps** reveals one step at a time, and **play from here** runs downstream | `packages/excalidraw/tests/flowStyles.test.tsx` | Presenting shows only the steps reached so far and dims the rest; next and previous (and the arrow keys) move one step and stop at the ends; the arrow keys are claimed **only while presenting** and are free again after; play from here runs exactly the selected shape's downstream steps in step order; and leaving restores the canvas. Tests: `AC-200 presenting reveals one step at a time`, `AC-200 next and previous stop at the ends`, `AC-200 the arrow keys are only claimed while presenting`, `AC-200 play from here runs the downstream steps in order`, `AC-200 leaving restores the canvas`. |
| AC-201 | the **pipe** is three strokes along the arrow, and its dash runs **in the arrow's direction** at a fixed speed | `packages/excalidraw/tests/flowStyles.test.tsx` | The pipe draws **three** strokes on the arrow's path (the outer one wider than the inner one, and a dashed highlight on top), checked on a straight and on a curved arrow; the highlight's dash offset **advances toward the arrow's end** and moves the same distance in the same time whatever the frame rate; and the arrow in the document is not changed. Tests: `AC-201 the pipe is three strokes on the arrow's path`, `AC-201 the dash runs toward the arrow's end`, `AC-201 the speed does not depend on the frame rate`, `AC-201 the arrow in the document is not changed`. |
| AC-202 | the camera **flies to the step**, stays on the page, and **comes back** | `packages/excalidraw/tests/flowStyles.test.tsx` | With the camera switch on: at the end of a flight the current step's bounds **plus the padding** are inside the viewport, checked **through `sceneCoordsToViewportCoords`** and not a re-derived formula; the flight eases (the view is **between** the start and the target part-way through, and **exactly** on the target at the end); it never leaves the page's rect and never goes below the zoom floor; with the switch **off** the view does not move; and leaving restores `scrollX`, `scrollY` and `zoom` **exactly**. Tests: `AC-202 the step and its padding are in view at the end of the flight`, `AC-202 the flight eases between the start and the target`, `AC-202 the camera stays on the page`, `AC-202 with the switch off the view does not move`, `AC-202 leaving restores the view exactly`. |
| AC-203 | **choose your path** follows the choice, and an end is **drawn on** then filled | `packages/excalidraw/tests/flowStyles.test.tsx` | On a decision chart in present steps: while a question is current its two branches are clickable and nothing else is; clicking **yes** makes the yes target the next step and **no** the no target (not the step order); `next` with no choice follows the step order; an end step's outline is **drawn on** (its dash offset goes from the whole outline to nothing over time) and **then** filled; and the end shape in the document is unchanged. Tests: `AC-203 only the current question's branches are clickable`, `AC-203 the choice decides the next step`, `AC-203 next with no choice follows the step order`, `AC-203 an end is drawn on and then filled`, `AC-203 the end shape in the document is unchanged`. |
| AC-204 | **resizing** a chart keeps its arrows bound and on their shapes | `packages/excalidraw/tests/flowFixes.test.tsx` | For a process, a decision and a network chart, resized to half and to double through the editor's own resize path: every arrow's two bindings still name shapes in the chart, every shape's `boundElements` still names its arrows, each arrow end lies on the edge of its shape, and the text is still inside its shape. Tests: `AC-204 a resized chart keeps both sides of every binding`, `AC-204 every arrow end is on its shape after a resize`, `AC-204 the text scales with its shape`, `AC-204 a decision chart survives being doubled`. |
| AC-205 | **lay this out** keeps the scale, and **reset layout** restores the remembered positions | `packages/excalidraw/tests/flowFixes.test.tsx` | A chart resized to double and laid out keeps its shapes' sizes and text sizes; laying out remembers each shape's first position and does not overwrite it the second time; reset puts every shape back where it was remembered with its arrows following, in **one** undo step; it is offered only for a chart that has a remembered layout; and a shape moved by hand is still reset. Tests: `AC-205 layout does not change the size of the shapes`, `AC-205 layout remembers the first position only`, `AC-205 reset puts the shapes back and the arrows follow`, `AC-205 reset is one undo step`, `AC-205 reset is offered only after a layout`, `AC-205 a hand-moved shape is reset too`. |
| AC-206 | the marks' **size follows the chart** | `packages/excalidraw/tests/flowFixes.test.tsx` | Pure geometry: the chart scale is the median font size over 24, clamped to one half and four; a chart at the floor font size draws a **shorter beam, shorter dashes and a smaller token** than one at the base size, and a chart at double draws larger ones; the numbers are proportional; the same chart at two zooms gets the same scale; and the scale is read again after a resize. Tests: `AC-206 the scale is the median font size over the base`, `AC-206 the scale is clamped`, `AC-206 a smaller chart gets smaller marks`, `AC-206 a larger chart gets larger marks`, `AC-206 the zoom does not change the scale`, `AC-206 a resize changes the marks at once`. |
| AC-207 | the **Insert chart** button is always reachable | `excalidraw-app/tests/flowStudio.test.tsx` | The tab's markup puts the preset choices and the script in a scrolling body and the Insert button and message in a footer outside it; the stylesheet gives the body `overflow-y: auto` and a flexible height, gives the footer no scroll and no shrink, and gives the script a minimum and **no maximum** height; and with a short window the button is in the document, not disabled and clickable. Tests: `AC-207 the button is outside the scrolling body`, `AC-207 the body scrolls and the footer does not`, `AC-207 the script has a minimum height and no maximum`, `AC-207 the button is clickable in a short window`. |

Tests: `AC-209 there are two animation choices`, `AC-209 a node animation and an arrow animation play together`, `AC-209 none for one leaves the other`, `AC-209 an old single style is read as a pair`, `AC-209 the choice writes nothing into the document`. |

| AC-215 | the **class loop** | `packages/excalidraw/tests/flowClassroom.test.tsx` | The state at a time is a pure function (steps, `stepMs`, loop): the steps follow each other every `stepMs`, the loop wraps, and a flow with loop off **rests on its last step**; two flows have **independent** clocks; only the shown page's flows are drawn; the marks follow a moved arrow and the chart scale; and **no animation frame is requested** when no flow is marked. | AC-365 | the mirror has three modes in one control | `packages/excalidraw/tests/panelControl.test.tsx` | The mode is read from the panel's mirror (no region is Whole, a region is Part, a region that follows is Follow) and a mode is written back (Whole clears the region and the follow, Part keeps the region and stops following, Follow keeps the region and follows, a Follow with no region starts with 60% by 60% in the middle and has a size control (a slider of 20% to 90% that keeps the area's shape and its middle, and is there in the follow mode only), and Part opens the picker, drawn into the full-screen panel when the panel is full screen); the panel has one control with three choices and none of the old two (the choose button's picker is opened by Part, Whole shows the window); each choice reaches `onChange` with the new mirror and the lit choice is the mode. | AC-366 | a point of the view is a point of the window | `packages/excalidraw/tests/panelControl.test.tsx` | The point of a pointer over the view's own box becomes a fraction of that box and then a fraction of the whole window through the region (the whole window is the identity, a region maps its corners to its own, a point outside the box is held on its edge), whatever the size of the box or the zoom of the canvas around it; a follow stands still while a button is down.
